P·L EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM MARCUM 114

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 114 ·

EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM MARCUM

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 114, coll. 179–244  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

PROLOGUS B. HIERONYMI.

  • 179A

( Vide Opera ejus. )

THE PROLOGUE OF BLESSED JEROME.

  • 179A

( See his Works. )

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- Initium Evangelii. (ISID.) Evangelium, bona annuntiatio, quod proprie ad regnum Dei et remissionem peccatorum pertinet. Unde: Poenitemini et credite Evangelio: et appropinquavit regnum coelorum.

(HIER.) Quatuor Evangelia unum sunt, et unum quatuor. Itaque et Marci liber dicitur Evangelium, et similiter aliorum: quia unum omnia, et omnia unum.

Jesu Christi. (HIER.) Jesus Hebraice, Soter Graece, Salvator Latine, Christus Graece, Messias Hebraice, unctus Latine, id est, rex sacerdos: dicitur 179Bde genere David regis, et Levitico.

Filii Dei. (BEDA.) Matthaeus dicit: filii David filii Abraham (Matth. I). Marcus dicit: filii Dei, ut paulatim a minoribus ad majora, etc., usque ad Joannes ab aeternitate Verbi Dei inchoat, in resurrectione Domini consummat.

VERS. 2.-- Sicut scriptum est. Marcus prophetarum testimonia praemittit quibus haec vetera et non nova, sed a prophetis praescita et praedicta asserit, in quo et Judaeos qui legem et prophetas susceperant ad Evangelium invitat: et gentiles qui Evangelium tenebant ad venerationem legis et prophetarum provocat, ne quis juxta haereticos alterum sine altero suscipiat.

Ecce mitto. (BEDA.) Angelus dicitur Joannes non 179Csocietate naturae, ut mentitur Origenes, sed officii dignitate: sicut et sacerdotes, de quibus dicitur: Labia sacerdotis custodiunt scientiam, et legem exquirunt ex ore ejus, quia angelus Domini exercituum est (Mal. II).

(ID.) Nota quod hoc testimonium: Ecce ego mitto angelum, etc., Malachiae est, utrumque tamen dicit Isaiae esse, quia, etc., usque ad omnes prophetae uno spiritu locuti sunt quasi uno ore.

VERS. 3.-- Clamantis. (ISID.) Ad Judaeos surdos propter peccata longe positos, indignationis clamore dignos. In deserto. In deserto fit vox et clamor: quia deserta domus Judaea vacans scopata sine spiritu Dei, propheta, rege, sacerdote. Unde Jesus et Joannes quaerunt quod in deserto amissum 179Dest. Ubi vicit diabolus, ibi vincitur: ubi cecidit homo, ibi resurgit.

Parate. (ISID.) Poenitentiam agite et praedicate. Unde Parasceve ante sabbatum, etc., usque ad ut possideamus quieti terram desiderabilem.

Viam Domini. (HIER.) Via Domini qua ad homines ingreditur, poenitentia est, per quam ad nos Deus descendit, et nos ad illum ascendimus. Unde angeli ascendentes descendentesque memorantur super Filium hominis.

Rectas facite semitas. (ISID.) Praecipit, ut regia 180Avia euntes, proximos diligamus ut nos, et nos, etc., usque ad ut non separetur os ab opere, nec opus ab ore.

Semitae post viam, quia mandata moralia post poenitentiam secundum Marcum vel Matthaeum explanantur. Paratur via per fidem et baptismum et poenitentiam.

Rectae semitae per austera indicia vestis, zonae, cibi, potus, vocis humillimae.

VERS. 4.-- Joannes. (ISID.) Gratia Dei. A gratia Dei incipit, unde sequitur: Baptizans, etc. Per baptismum peccata dimittuntur; unde: Quod gratis accepistis, gratis date (Matth. X). Et Apostolus: Gratia salvati estis (Ephes. II).

Baptizans. (HIER.) Baptismum poenitentiae dedit, baptismum in remissionem peccatorum praedicavit, 180Btamen non dedit. Sicut praedicatione, etc., usque ad nunc per amicum sponsi inducitur sponsa, ut Rebecca Isaac, per puerum pallio albo velata.

VERS. 5.-- In Jordane (ID.) Jordanis aliena descensio, ubi peccata absolvuntur. Arca enim, etc., usque ad corpus Christi, non mundatis non proficit, sed in deterius mutat.

Confitentes, etc. (ID.) Confessio et pulchritudo in conspectu ejus (Psal. CXV), id est sponsi. Unde desilit sponsa de camelo, cum se humiliat Ecclesia Christo, qui figuratur per Isaac, in cujus nativitate risum fecit Deus Sarae. Sicut ad Mariam dicitur, quae est princeps cum Deo: Benedicta tu in mulieribus (Luc. I).

180C(BEDA.) Confitentes peccata sua baptizantur. Debent enim melioris vitae novitatem promittere, qui baptismum, etc., usque ad et sic mundatos Christianam religionem promittentes in Ecclesiae membra commuta.

VERS. 6.-- Et erat Joannes, etc. (HIER.) Vestis prophetae et cibus et potus, totam austeram vitam praedicantium significant, et gentes, etc., usque ad quia omnia a gratia Evangelii, in qua non est distinctio Judaei et Graeci.

(BEDA.) Vestitus pilis cameli. Quia peccata non fovit, sed aspere increpavit, dicens: Genimina viperarum, etc. Zona pellicea, etc., usque ad in melle enim dulcedo, in locusta alacer volatus, sed cito deciduus.

VERS. 7.-- Fortior me. (HIER.) Quis fortior est 180Dgratia qua abluuntur peccata? Ille qui, etc., usque ad et usque ad centum quadraginta quatuor millia pervenit.

Solvere corrigiam. (BEDA.) Modus fuit apud veteres, ut si quis eam quae sibi competeret, accipere uxorem nollet, ille, etc., usque ad Redemptoris vestigia nudare non valeo, quia sponsi nomen mihi non usurpo.

(HIER.) Corrigia calcamentorum, id est mysterium Incarnationis Dei. Calx enim, etc., usque ad sponsa in patriarchis, amica in prophetis, proxima 181Ain Maria et Joseph, dilecta in Joanne Baptista, columba in Christo et apostolis.

VERS. 8.-- Ego baptizavi vos aqua. (BEDA.) Nondum Joannes dicit Deum aut Dei Filium esse aperte, quia rudes erant auditores: sed paulatim per humanitatem glorificatam ad fidem divinitatis ducit, dicens: Ille vero baptizabit vos Spiritu sancto. Sed postea cum capaciores jam videret, Dei Filium jam aperte praedicat dicens: Testimonium perhibuit, quia hic est Dei Filius (Joan. III).

(ISID.) Ego baptizo vos aqua, ille, etc. Dei fluvius semper plenus, semper aequalis, de quo: Fluminis impetus laetificat civitatem Dei (Psal. XLV). Multum distat, etc., usque ad unctio Christi de amaritudine passionis pervenit ad gloriam resurrectionis; 181Bunde Apostolus: Hoc sentite in vobis, quod et in Christo Jesu, etc., usque: Propter quod et Deus illum exaltavit (Phil. II).

VERS. 9.-- Et baptizatus est in Jordane. Tribus causis: Ut justitiam et humilitatem impleret; ut baptismum Joannis comprobaret; ut Jordanis aquas sanctificans per descensionem columbae Spiritum sanctum in baptismo adesse monstraret.

(HIER.) Marcus: Sicut cervus desiderans ad fontes aquarum saltus dat, culmina pratorum carpit, palmas in vertice portat, velut apis melliflua flores agri (cui odor veri Isaac similatur) summatim gustat. Unde a Nazareth Galilaeae venientem Jesum ad baptismum, etc., usque ad: Vincenti dabo edere de ligno vitae, quod est in paradiso Dei mei (Apoc. II).

181CVERS. 10.-- Vidit apertos coelos, etc. (BEDA.) Mysterium Trinitatis in baptismo demonstratur: Filius baptizatur, Spiritus est in columba, Pater in voce sonat. Aperiuntur coeli, etc., usque ad apud nos manebit et in nobis erit juxta mensuram fidei Spiritus. De Christo autem dicitur: Plenum gratiae et veritatis (Joan. I).

Manentem in ipso. Manet in eo Spiritus, non ex quo baptizatus, sed ex quo conceptus. Quod autem in baptismo descendit, significat nobis gratiam spiritualem in baptismo dari, quibus in remissionem peccatorum ex aqua et Spiritu regeneratis solet amplior Spiritus gratia per impositionem manus episcopi coelitus dari.

VERS. 11.-- Tu es Filius meus dilectus. (BEDA.) 181DNon quod nesciebat docetur, sed nobis quod credere debeamus ostenditur, ipsum scilicet, etc., usque ad bene autem in specie columbae descendit Spiritus, quae simplex animal et sine felle: quia simplices quaerit, nec habitat in mentibus impuris.

VERS. 12.-- Spiritus. (ID.) Ne dubium quis spiritus, Lucas consulte praemittit: Jesus plenus Spiritu sancto regressus est a Jordane; deinde intulit: et agebatur a Spiritu in desertum, ne putetur immundus spiritus quidquam contra eum potuisse.

VERS. 13.-- Quadraginta diebus. (ID.) Totum tempus significat, quo membra ejus diabolus tentat. Quadripartitus enim mundus per observantiam decem praeceptorum contra diabolum certando Domino famulatur. 182AQuater vero decem quadraginta faciunt. Ubique et semper adest adversarius, qui iter nostrum impedire non cessat.

Eratque cum bestiis. Nos quoque si in eremo sanctae conversationis bestiales hominum mores impolluta mente toleramus, mysterium angelorum meremur, a quibus corpore absoluti in coelum transferamur

VERS. 14.-- Postquam autem. (ISID.) Accepimus gratiam pro gratia, carnalia decollantur, spiritualia vivificantur. Cessante umbra, adest veritas. Joannes in carcere, lex in Judaea, Jesus in Galilaea, salus in gentibus.

Praedicans, etc. (HIER.) Beati pauperes spiritu, quoniam ipsi possidebunt (Matth. V), etc. Regno terreno 182Bsuccedit paupertas, paupertati Christianorum regnum sempiternum; unde: Regnum meum non est de hoc mundo (Joan. XVIII): omnis enim honor terrenus, spuma, fumus, somnus.

VERS. 15.-- Poenitemini. (HIER.) Amaritudinem radicis dulcedo pomi compensat: pericula maris spes portus delectat, dolorem medicinae spes salutis mitigat. Qui desiderat nucleum, nucem frangat: poenitentiam agat, qui vult aeterno adhaerere bono. Praeconia Christi narrare possunt, qui ad palmam indulgentiae pervenire meruerunt. Unde post poenitentiam praedicatores eliguntur.

VERS. 16.-- Vidit Simonem. (HIER.) Simon, obediens; Andraeas, virilis; Jacobus, supplantator; Joannes, gratia. His nominibus, etc., usque ad per prudentiam 182Cobedimus, per justitiam viriliter agimus, per temperantiam serpentes calcamus, per fortitudinem Dei gratiam meremur.

(ID.) Quem non inebriat eloquentia saecularis? Difficile homines potentes, et nobiles, et divites, et multo his difficilius eloquentes credunt Deo. Obcaecatur mens eorum divitiis et opibus atque luxuria, et circumdati vitiis non possunt videre virtutes, simplicitatemque Scripturae sanctae, non ex majestate sensuum, sed ex verborum judicant utilitate.

(ID.) Vocantur quatuor piscatores, Simon, Andraeas, Jacobus et Joannes: relictis omnibus sequuntur. Hac quadriga vehimur ad aethera, etc., usque ad pellibus Salomonis tegimur, quibus sponsa 182Dse gloriatur formosam.

(BEDA.) Quaeritur quomodo primum Petrum et Andream et post pusillum Jacobum et Joannem vocaverit, sicut narrat, etc., usque ad tunc enim non subductis ad terram navibus tanquam cura redeundi, sed tanquam vocantem et jubentem secuti sunt.

Et ingrediuntur. (HIER.) Marcus dicta Evangelii in semetipso disponens, ordinem historiae non secutus, mysteriorum ordinem servat, unde in sabbatis primam virtutem narrat dicens: Et ingrediuntur, etc.

Capharnaum. (ID.) Capharnaum villa consolationis, sabbatum requies. Homo in spiritu immundo, 183Agenus humanum, in quo immunditia, etc., usque ad Pharao dimissus ab Israel, persequimur eum, diabolus contemptus surgit in scandala.

(BEDA.) Quod sabbato maxime curat et docet, ostendit se non sub lege esse, sed supra: et docet nos verum sabbatum, ut saluti scilicet animarum studentes, ab omni opere servili, id est, ab omnibus illicitis abstineamus.

VERS. 22.-- Erat enim, etc. (BEDA.) Scribae dicebant quae scripta erant in Moyse et prophetis; Jesus, ut Deus et Dominus, ipsius Moysi libere quae deerant legi addebat vel commutabat; unde in Matthaeo: Audistis quia dictum est antiquis, etc. Ego autem dico vobis.

VERS. 24.-- Quid nobis, etc. (ID.) Non est hic voluntatis 183Bconfessio, sed necessitatis extorsio: sicut servus fugitivus visum dominum timet, et de verberibus deprecatur. Daemones enim in terris Dominum cernentes, se continuo judicandos credebant esse.

VERS. 25.-- Obmutesce. (ID.) Congruo ordine lingua serpentina quae prima virus sparsit, ne ultra spargat primo obcluditur, deinde, etc., usque ad ut sit idem ordo restaurationis in Domino, qui perditionis in protoplasto.

VERS. 26.-- Discerpens. (ID.) Vel convexans, sicut quidam codices habent. Lucas autem dicit quia nihil ei nocuit. Sed quod Marcus ait, discerpens, idem est quod Lucas: cum projecisset eum in medium. Quod 183Cvero addidit, nihilque nocuit: intelligendum est quia illa jactatio non eum debilitavit: sicut exeunt daemones amputatis vel avulsis aliquibus membris.

(ID.) Virum a daemonio liberatum, animum ab immunda cogitatione purgatum intelligimus. Feminam vero a febribus consequenter curatam, carnem a concupiscentiae fervore per continentiae praecepta frenatam.

VERS. 27.-- Quidnam est? Per ea quae vident ad inquisitionem eorum quae audierant excitantur. Ad hoc enim et ipse Dominus signa facit, et discipulis facere dedit, ut per hoc Evangelio certius credatur, dum qui coelestia promittunt terrenis, coelestia et divina opera operentur in terris.

VERS. 30.-- Et statim dicunt ei, etc. Lucas dicit 183Dquia rogaverunt pro illa. Modo enim Salvator rogatus, modo ultro curat, ut contra vitiorum quoque passiones, et se precibus fidelium semper adesse, et quae non intelliguntur, vel intelligenda dare, vel non intellecta, dimittere pie petentibus ostendat. Unde: Delicta quis intelligit? ab occultis meis munda me (Psal. XVIII).

VERS. 31.-- Apprehensa. Socrus Petri febricitans apprehensa manu levatur. Febris intemperantia est, de qua nos filii Synagogae per manum disciplinae et levationem desiderii sanamur, et hujus qui sanat nos ad vesperam declinantis a nobis ministramus voluntati.

Ministrabat eis. (BEDA.) Tropologice: Id est, 184Amembra quae servierant immunditiae, serviant justitiae.

VERS. 32.-- Vespere autem. (ID.) Occasus solis mors Christi. Afferebant ad eum omnes male habentes. Plures enim per apostolos post mortem suam sanavit, quam cum in carne vivens Judaeis praedicavit.

VERS. 33.-- Ad januam. (HIER.) Janua regni moraliter poenitentia est cum fide, quae operatur salutem variis languoribus. Varia enim sunt vitia, quibus languescit civitas mundi.

VERS. 34.-- Quoniam sciebant eum. (BEDA.) Lucas dicit apertius: Exibant daemonia a multis, etc., quia sciebant eum esse Christum. Daemonia ergo Christum esse sciunt et Filium Dei, etc., usque ad unde 184BPaulus: Nemo principum hujus saeculi cognovit. Si enim cognovissent, nunquam Dominum gloriae crucifixissent (I Cor. II).

VERS. 35.-- Et diluculo. (ID.) Sicut per occasum solis mors, sic per diluculum resurrectio Christi cujus manifesta luce abiit in desertum gentium et in fidelibus orabat, quia eorum corda ad orationem excitabat.

VERS. 38.-- Ad hoc enim veni. (VICT. ANT.) Commode hoc subdit evangelista. Neque enim solo illius divinitatis auctoritas per hoc adumbratur, verum etiam spontaneae evacuationis humilitas. Lucas autem, quo verbi oeconomiam exprimat, ad hoc enim missus sum, scribit. Quare is Patris de Verbi incarnatione beneplacitum apostolatus nomine designat.

184CVERS. 39.-- Et erat praedicans in synagogis. (BEDA.) In hac praedicatione intelligitur etiam sermo ille habitus in monte quem refert Matthaeus; nam, etc., usque ad: Et ecce leprosus veniens adorabat eum, dicens: Domine, si vis, potes me mundare (Matth. VIII).

VERS. 40.-- Et genu flexo, etc. (BEDA.) Humilis pudibundus. Debet enim quisque de maculis vitae suae erubescere, sed confessionem verecundia non reprimat: ostendat vulnus, remedium poscat.

(HIER.) Leprosus genuflexo Domini voluntatem deprecans mundatur qui non vult mortem peccatoris, sed, etc., usque ad: Date eleemosynam: et ecce omnia munda sunt. (Luc. XI).

184D(BEDA.) Leprosus genus humanum significat, qui, secundum Lucam, lepra plenus fuisse perhibetur. Omnes enim, etc., usque ad et vero sacerdoti offerre corpora sua hostiam viventem, sanctam, Deo placentem (Rom. XII).

Si vis potes. (ID.) In voluntate tribuit potestatem, nec dubitat ut incredulus de voluntate Domini, sed colluvionis suae conscius non praesumit.

(ID.) Qui exclusus a lege Domini se potestate curari postulat, supra legem judicat gratiam esse. Ut enim in Domino potestatis auctoritas, sic in illo fidei constantia.

VERS. 41.-- Extendit manum. (ID.) Leprosum tangi lex prohibet, sed quia Dominus legis est, non 185Aobsequitur legi, sed legem facit. Non ideo tangit, quia sine tactu curare non poterat, sed sic probat, quia legi subjectus non erat.

Volo, mundare. (ID.) Illud mirabile, quo rogatur eo sanat genere. Volo, mundare: et cum dixisset, statim discessit ab eo lepra. Nihil medium inter opus et praeceptum. Dixit et facta sunt (Psal. XXXII). Vides quia voluntas potestas est. Itaque, etc., usque ad: Volo enim dicit propter Potinum, imperat propter Arium, tangit propter Manichaeum.

VERS. 44.-- Vide, etc. (ID.) Non quia voluit quod non potuit, quia omnia quaecunque voluit fecit (Psal. CXIII). Sed docet nostra beneficia reprimi, non tantum a mercede pecuniae, sed etiam gratiae.

Ostende te, etc. (ID.) Ut intelligat non legis ordine, 185Bsed gratia quae super legem est esse curatum. Offerre jubet, ut ostendat quia non legem solvit, sed implet, dum secundum legem gradiens supra legem sanat, quos remedia legis non sanabant. Nec sacrificium significans cessare debebat, cum nondum significatum confirmatum erat constitutione apostolorum et fide credentium.

VERS. 45.-- At ille egressus. (ID.) Unius salvatio multos ad Dominum cogit. Non potest tacere beneficium, etiam jussus ab eo qui dedit. Praecepit Dominus abscondi. Vult enim, ut in bonis quae agimus latere velimus, quod est humilitatis; et inviti prodamur ut aliis prosimus, quod magnae est sublimitatis, cum etiamsi ipsi velimus, opera non possunt taceri.

185CIta ut jam, etc. (HIER.) Non omnibus manifestatus est Jesus, qui latis atque plataneis serviunt laudibus et propriis voluptatibus, sed his, etc., usque ad quos nihil potest separare a charitate Dei.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- The beginning of the Gospel. (ISID.) The Gospel, good tidings, which belongs properly to the kingdom of God and to the remission of sins. Whence: Repent ye and believe the Gospel: and the kingdom of heaven hath drawn nigh. [Vulg. Mc 1:15 has regnum Dei, "the kingdom of God," and sets the two members in the other order, the kingdom's approach before the summons.]

(HIER.) The four Gospels are one, and the one is four. And so the book of Mark also is called the Gospel, and likewise that of the others: because all are one, and one is all.

Of Jesus Christ. (HIER.) Jesus in Hebrew, Soter in Greek, Salvator in Latin, Christus in Greek, Messias in Hebrew, anointed in Latin, that is, king and priest: he is said 179Bto be of the line of David the king, and of the Levitical line.

Of the Son of God. (BEDA.) Matthew says: of the son of David, the son of Abraham (Matth. I). Mark says: of the Son of God, so that little by little from the lesser to the greater, etc., as far as John begins from the eternity of the Word of God, and ends in the resurrection of the Lord.

VERS. 2.-- As it is written. Mark sets the testimonies of the prophets first, by which he asserts that these things are old and not new, but foreknown and foretold by the prophets, and in this he invites also the Jews, who had received the law and the prophets, to the Gospel: and he calls the Gentiles, who held the Gospel, to the veneration of the law and the prophets, that no one, after the heretics, may receive the one without the other.

Behold, I send. (BEDA.) John is called an angel not 179Cby fellowship of nature, as Origen falsely pretends, but by the dignity of his office: as the priests also are, of whom it is said: The lips of the priest keep knowledge, and they seek the law out of his mouth, because he is the angel of the Lord of hosts (Mal. II).

(ID.) Note that this testimony: Behold, I send my angel, etc., is Malachi's, yet he says that both are Isaiah's, because, etc., as far as all the prophets spoke by one spirit as with one mouth.

VERS. 3.-- Of one crying. (ISID.) To the Jews, deaf because of their sins, set far off, worthy of a cry of indignation. In the desert. In the desert the voice and the cry are made: because the house of Judaea is deserted, standing empty and swept, without the Spirit of God, without prophet, king, priest. Whence Jesus and John seek what was lost 179Din the desert. Where the devil conquered, there he is conquered: where man fell, there he rises again.

Prepare ye. (ISID.) Do penance and preach. Whence the Parasceve before the sabbath, etc., as far as that we, being at rest, may possess the desirable land.

The way of the Lord. (HIER.) The way of the Lord by which he enters in unto men is penance, through which God comes down to us, and we ascend to him. Whence the angels are remembered as ascending and descending upon the Son of man.

Make straight the paths. (ISID.) He commands that, going by the royal 180Aroad, we love our neighbours as ourselves, and ourselves, etc., as far as that the mouth be not sundered from the work, nor the work from the mouth.

The paths come after the way, because the moral commandments are expounded after penance, according to Mark or Matthew. The way is prepared by faith and baptism and penance.

The paths are made straight by the austere tokens of raiment, of girdle, of food, of drink, of a most humble voice.

VERS. 4.-- John. (ISID.) The grace of God. He begins from the grace of God, whence there follows: Baptizing, etc. Through baptism sins are forgiven; whence: That which ye have freely received, freely give (Matth. X). And the Apostle: By grace ye are saved (Ephes. II).

Baptizing. (HIER.) He gave the baptism of penance, he preached a baptism unto the remission of sins, 180Byet he did not give it. As by preaching, etc., as far as now the bride is brought in by the friend of the bridegroom, as Rebecca to Isaac, veiled by the servant with a white cloak.

VERS. 5.-- In the Jordan (ID.) Jordan is the descent of another, where sins are loosed. For the ark, etc., as far as the body of Christ profits not those who are not cleansed, but changes them for the worse.

Confessing, etc. (ID.) Confession and beauty are in his sight (Psal. CXV), that is, in the sight of the bridegroom. Whence the bride alights from her camel, when the Church humbles herself to Christ, who is figured by Isaac, at whose birth God made laughter for Sara. As it is said to Mary, who is a princess with God: Blessed art thou among women (Luc. I).

180C(BEDA.) Confessing their sins, they are baptized. For they ought to promise the newness of a better life, who baptism, etc., as far as and so change those who are cleansed and promise the Christian religion into members of the Church.

VERS. 6.-- And John was, etc. (HIER.) The prophet's raiment and food and drink signify the whole austere life of preachers, and the Gentiles, etc., as far as because all things are from the grace of the Gospel, in which there is no distinction of Jew and Greek.

(BEDA.) Clothed with camel's hair. Because he did not cherish sins, but rebuked them sharply, saying: Ye brood of vipers, etc. The leathern girdle, etc., as far as for in the honey is sweetness, in the locust a nimble flight, but one soon falling.

VERS. 7.-- Mightier than I. (HIER.) Who is mightier than 180Dthe grace by which sins are washed away? He who, etc., as far as and it reaches even to the hundred and forty-four thousand.

To loose the latchet. (BEDA.) There was a custom among the ancients, that if any man were unwilling to take to wife her who fell to him, he, etc., as far as I am not able to bare the feet of the Redeemer, because I claim not for myself the name of bridegroom.

(HIER.) The latchet of the shoes, that is, the mystery of the Incarnation of God. For the heel, etc., as far as the bride among the patriarchs, the friend among the prophets, the neighbour 181Ain Mary and Joseph, the beloved in John the Baptist, the dove in Christ and the apostles.

VERS. 8.-- I have baptized you with water. (BEDA.) John does not yet say openly that he is God or the Son of God, because his hearers were untaught: but little by little, through the humanity glorified, he leads them to faith in the divinity, saying: But he shall baptize you with the Holy Ghost. Yet afterwards, when he saw them now more capable, he preaches the Son of God openly, saying: He gave testimony, that this is the Son of God (Joan. III).

(ISID.) I baptize you with water, he, etc. The river of God is always full, always equal, of which: The stream of the river maketh the city of God joyful (Psal. XLV). There is a great distance, etc., as far as the anointing of Christ comes from the bitterness of the passion to the glory of the resurrection; 181Bwhence the Apostle: Have this mind in you, which was also in Christ Jesus, etc., even to: For which cause God also hath exalted him (Phil. II).

VERS. 9.-- And he was baptized in the Jordan. For three causes: that he might fulfil justice and humility; that he might approve the baptism of John; that, sanctifying the waters of Jordan by the descent of the dove, he might show the Holy Ghost to be present in baptism.

(HIER.) Mark: As the hart, longing for the fountains of waters, gives leaps, crops the tops of the meadows, bears palms upon his crown, so, like the honey-flowing bee, he tastes but lightly the flowers of the field (to which the odour of the true Isaac is likened). Whence Jesus coming from Nazareth of Galilee to the baptism, etc., as far as: To him that overcometh I will give to eat of the tree of life, which is in the paradise of my God (Apoc. II).

181CVERS. 10.-- He saw the heavens opened, etc. (BEDA.) The mystery of the Trinity is shown in the baptism: the Son is baptized, the Spirit is in the dove, the Father sounds in the voice. The heavens are opened, etc., as far as the Spirit shall abide with us and shall be in us according to the measure of faith. But of Christ it is said: Full of grace and truth (Joan. I).

Remaining in him. The Spirit remains in him, not from the time he was baptized, but from the time he was conceived. But that he descended at the baptism signifies that spiritual grace is given to us in baptism, to whom, being regenerated of water and the Spirit unto the remission of sins, a fuller grace of the Spirit is wont to be given from heaven by the imposition of the bishop's hand.

VERS. 11.-- Thou art my beloved Son. (BEDA.) 181DHe is not taught what he knew not, but it is shown to us what we ought to believe, namely that he, etc., as far as and rightly did the Spirit descend in the likeness of a dove, which is a simple creature and without gall: because he seeks the simple, nor does he dwell in impure minds.

VERS. 12.-- The Spirit. (ID.) Lest it be doubtful which spirit, Luke deliberately sets before it: Jesus being full of the Holy Ghost returned from the Jordan; then he added: and was led by the Spirit into the desert, lest an unclean spirit be thought to have had any power against him.

VERS. 13.-- Forty days. (ID.) It signifies the whole time in which the devil tempts his members. For the fourfold world, by the observance of the ten commandments, serves the Lord in striving against the devil. 182AAnd four times ten make forty. Everywhere and always the adversary is at hand, who ceases not to hinder our journey.

And he was with the beasts. We also, if in the desert of holy conversation we bear with undefiled mind the bestial manners of men, deserve the mystery of the angels, by whom, loosed from the body, we may be carried over into heaven

VERS. 14.-- But after that. (ISID.) We have received grace for grace, things carnal are beheaded, things spiritual are quickened. The shadow ceasing, the truth is at hand. John in prison, the law in Judaea, Jesus in Galilee, salvation among the Gentiles.

Preaching, etc. (HIER.) Blessed are the poor in spirit, for they shall possess (Matth. V), etc. [Vulg. Mt 5:3 reads quoniam ipsorum est regnum caelorum, "for theirs is the kingdom of heaven"; ipsi possidebunt belongs to the next beatitude, Mt 5:4, "Blessed are the meek, for they shall possess the land."] To the earthly kingdom 182Bsucceeds poverty, and to the poverty of Christians an everlasting kingdom; whence: My kingdom is not of this world (Joan. XVIII): for every earthly honour is froth, smoke, a dream.

VERS. 15.-- Repent ye. (HIER.) The sweetness of the fruit makes up for the bitterness of the root: the hope of the harbour delights away the perils of the sea, the hope of health softens the pain of the medicine. He who desires the kernel, let him break the nut: let him do penance who wishes to cleave to the eternal good. They can tell forth the praises of Christ who have deserved to come to the palm of pardon. Whence after penance preachers are chosen.

VERS. 16.-- He saw Simon. (HIER.) Simon, obedient; Andrew, manly; James, supplanter; John, grace. By these names, etc., as far as by prudence 182Cwe obey, by justice we act manfully, by temperance we tread upon serpents, by fortitude we merit the grace of God.

(ID.) Whom does worldly eloquence not intoxicate? With difficulty do men that are powerful, and noble, and rich, and with much more difficulty than these the eloquent, believe God. Their mind is blinded by riches and wealth and luxury, and, encompassed about with vices, they cannot see the virtues, and they judge the simplicity of Holy Scripture not from the majesty of its meanings, but from the serviceableness of its words.

(ID.) Four fishermen are called, Simon, Andrew, James and John: leaving all things they follow. By this chariot we are borne to the upper air, etc., as far as we are covered with the skins of Solomon, in which the bride 182Dglories that she is beautiful.

(BEDA.) It is asked how he called first Peter and Andrew, and after a little James and John, as he relates, etc., as far as for then they followed him, not with the ships drawn up to land as though with a care of returning, but as one calling and commanding.

And they enter in. (HIER.) Mark, disposing the sayings of the Gospel within himself, not following the order of the history, keeps the order of the mysteries, whence on the sabbath he relates the first miracle, saying: And they enter in, etc.

Capharnaum. (ID.) Capharnaum is the village of consolation, the sabbath is rest. The man in an unclean spirit, 183Athe human race, in which is uncleanness, etc., as far as Pharao, let go by Israel, we pursue him, the devil, despised, rises up into scandals.

(BEDA.) That he heals and teaches chiefly on the sabbath shows that he is not under the law, but above it: and he teaches us the true sabbath, that, being zealous namely for the salvation of souls, we may abstain from every servile work, that is, from all unlawful things.

VERS. 22.-- For he was, etc. (BEDA.) The scribes said the things that were written in Moses and the prophets; Jesus, as God and Lord, freely added to the law of Moses himself, or changed, the things that were wanting; whence in Matthew: You have heard that it was said to them of old, etc. But I say to you.

VERS. 24.-- What have we, etc. (ID.) This is not a confession 183Bof the will, but an extortion of necessity: as a runaway servant fears his master when he sees him, and entreats concerning stripes. For the demons, beholding the Lord upon earth, believed that they were straightway to be judged.

VERS. 25.-- Hold thy peace. (ID.) In fitting order the serpent's tongue, which first scattered venom, is first stopped, that it scatter no more, then, etc., as far as that the order of restoration in the Lord may be the same as that of perdition in the first-formed man.

VERS. 26.-- Tearing him. (ID.) Or shaking him, as certain codices have it. But Luke says that it hurt him nothing. Yet what Mark says, tearing, is the same as Luke's: when he had cast him into the midst. But as to what 183Che added, and hurt him not: it is to be understood that that tossing did not weaken him: as demons go out with some limbs cut off or torn away.

(ID.) The man delivered from the demon we understand to be the mind cleansed from unclean thought. But the woman consequently healed of her fevers, the flesh bridled from the fervour of concupiscence by the precepts of continence.

VERS. 27.-- What thing is this? By the things which they see they are stirred up to the search of the things which they had heard. For to this end the Lord himself both works signs and gave to his disciples to work them, that by this the Gospel may be the more surely believed, while they who promise heavenly things to earthly men work heavenly and divine works upon earth.

VERS. 30.-- And forthwith they tell him, etc. Luke says 183Dthat they besought him for her. For sometimes the Saviour heals when he is asked, sometimes of his own accord, that against the passions of vices also he may show both that he is always present to the prayers of the faithful, and that the things which are not understood he either gives to be understood, or, when they are not understood, forgives to them that ask devoutly. Whence: Who can understand sins? from my secret sins cleanse me (Psal. XVIII).

VERS. 31.-- Taking her by the hand. Peter's mother-in-law, sick of a fever, is lifted up when her hand is taken. The fever is intemperance, of which we, the sons of the Synagogue, are healed by the hand of discipline and by the lifting up of desire, and we minister to the will of him who heals us as he declines from us toward evening.

She ministered unto them. (BEDA.) Tropologically: that is, 184Alet the members which had served uncleanness serve justice.

VERS. 32.-- And in the evening. (ID.) The setting of the sun is the death of Christ. They brought unto him all that were ill. For he healed more by the apostles after his death than when, living in the flesh, he preached to the Jews.

VERS. 33.-- At the door. (HIER.) The door of the kingdom, morally, is penance with faith, which works salvation for divers infirmities. For divers are the vices by which the city of the world languishes.

VERS. 34.-- Because they knew him. (BEDA.) Luke says more openly: Demons went out from many, etc., because they knew him to be Christ. The demons therefore know that he is Christ and the Son of God, etc., as far as whence 184BPaul: None of the princes of this world knew. For if they had known, they would never have crucified the Lord of glory (I Cor. II).

VERS. 35.-- And in the morning. (ID.) As by the setting of the sun, death, so by the dawn, the resurrection of Christ, by whose manifest light he went away into the desert of the Gentiles and prayed in the faithful, because he was stirring up their hearts to prayer.

VERS. 38.-- For to this I came. (VICT. ANT.) Fitly does the evangelist subjoin this. For not by this alone is the authority of his divinity shadowed forth, but also the humility of his voluntary emptying. But Luke, that he may express the dispensation of the Word, writes, for to this was I sent. Wherefore he designates by the name of apostleship the Father's good pleasure concerning the incarnation of the Word.

184CVERS. 39.-- And he was preaching in the synagogues. (BEDA.) In this preaching is understood also that sermon delivered on the mount which Matthew relates; for, etc., as far as: And behold a leper came and adored him, saying: Lord, if thou wilt, thou canst make me clean (Matth. VIII).

VERS. 40.-- And kneeling down, etc. (BEDA.) Humble, ashamed. For every man ought to blush at the stains of his life, but let shame not check confession: let him show the wound, let him ask the remedy.

(HIER.) The leper, kneeling, entreating the will of the Lord, is made clean by him who willeth not the death of the sinner, but, etc., as far as: Give alms: and behold, all things are clean. (Luc. XI).

184D(BEDA.) The leper signifies the human race, which, according to Luke, is reported to have been full of leprosy. For all, etc., as far as and to offer to the true priest their bodies a living sacrifice, holy, pleasing unto God (Rom. XII).

If thou wilt, thou canst. (ID.) In the will he assigns the power, nor does he doubt of the Lord's will as an unbeliever, but, conscious of his own foulness, he does not presume.

(ID.) He who, shut out by the law, asks to be healed by the Lord's power, judges grace to be above the law. For as in the Lord the authority of power, so in him the constancy of faith.

VERS. 41.-- He stretched forth his hand. (ID.) The law forbids a leper to be touched, but because he is the Lord of the law, he does not 185Aobey the law, but makes the law. He touches him not because he could not heal without a touch, but he thus proves that he was not subject to the law.

I will, be thou made clean. (ID.) That is wonderful, that he heals in the same manner in which he is asked. I will, be thou made clean: and when he had said it, immediately the leprosy departed from him. There is nothing between the work and the command. He spoke, and they were made (Psal. XXXII). Thou seest that the will is the power. And so, etc., as far as: I will for he says on account of Potinus, he commands on account of Arius, he touches on account of the Manichee.

VERS. 44.-- See thou, etc. (ID.) Not because he willed what he could not do, since he hath done all things whatsoever he would (Psal. CXIII). But he teaches that our good deeds are to be kept back, not only from the wage of money, but also from that of favour.

Show thyself, etc. (ID.) That he may understand that he was healed not by the order of the law, 185Bbut by the grace which is above the law. He bids him offer, that he may show that he does not destroy the law, but fulfils it, while, walking according to the law, he heals above the law those whom the remedies of the law were not healing. Nor ought the sacrifice which signified to cease, since that which was signified had not yet been confirmed by the ordinance of the apostles and the faith of believers.

VERS. 45.-- But he going out. (ID.) The salvation of one compels many to the Lord. He cannot keep silence about the benefit, even when bidden by him who gave it. The Lord commanded that it be hidden. For he wills that in the good things we do we should wish to lie hid, which belongs to humility; and that we be brought forward unwilling, that we may profit others, which belongs to great sublimity, since even if we ourselves should wish it, the works cannot be kept silent.

185CSo that now, etc. (HIER.) Jesus was not made manifest to all, who serve broad and public praises and their own pleasures, but to those, etc., as far as whom nothing can separate from the charity of God.

CAPUT II.

VERS. 2.-- Ita ut non caperet, etc. (BEDA.) Praedicante Domino, in domo non capiuntur, neque ad januam: quia praedicante in Judaea Christo gentiles ad audiendum nondum intrare voluerunt, ad quos tamen (etsi foris positos) doctrinae suae verba direxit per praedicatores suos.

VERS. 3.-- Paralyticum. (HIER.) Paralysis typus est torporis, quo piger jacet in mollitie carnis, habens desiderium salutis: sed turbis, etc., usque ad 185Dut per aliam viam cum Magis in suam regionem redeat.

A quator portabatur. (BEDA.) Quia quatuor virtutibus ad Dominum fiducia mentis erigitur. De quibus dicitur in libro Sapientiae: Sobrietatem et sapientiam docet, et scientiam et virtutem (Sap. VIII), quas alii prudentiam, fortitudinem, temperantiam et justitiam nuncupant.

VERS. 4.-- Nudaverunt tectum ubi erat. Tectum domus in qua Christus docet, sublimitas est Scripturae, in qua corrigit adolescentior viam suam, in custodiendo sermones tuos (Psal. CXVIII).

(BEDA.) Domus Jesu secundum alium evangelistam tegulis tecta dicitur, quia vilitate litterae sensus spiritualis 186Ategitur. Cum grabato ante Jesum deponitur, quia cognoscere Jesum debet etiam in carne hac constitutus.

VERS. 5.-- Cum autem vidisset. (ID.) Multum valet apud Deum fides propria, ubi tantum valuit aliena: ut homo interius et exterius sanatus repente exsurgeret, aliorumque meritis sua ei relaxarentur errata.

Fili. Mira humilitas, despectum ab omnibus, dissolutum totis artubus, filium vocat. Aut certe ideo, quia dimittuntur ei peccata.

VERS. 6.-- Dimittuntur tibi peccata. Ostendit propter peccata fuisse morbo percussum, et ideo non posse sanari nisi prius his dimissis. Sicut paralytico ad piscinam: Ecce sanus factus es, jam noli peccare, 186Bne deterius tibi contingat (Joan. V). (BEDA.) Quinque de causis afficiuntur homines molestiis carnis. Aut propter merita augenda, ut Job et martyres. Aut propter humilitatem, etc., usque ad Lazarus, cujus infirmitas non fuit ad mortem, sed pro gloria Dei, aut ad initium aeternae damnationis, ut Herodes.

VERS. 7.-- Quis potest dimittere. (ID.) Verum dicunt: Solus Deus hoc potest, solus etiam per eos dimittit quibus potestatem dimittendi peccata tribuit, ideo vere Deus. Verum testantur, sed personam Christi negando falluntur. Errant igitur, quia ut Deum esse, et peccata dimittere credunt, Christum tamen esse eum non credunt. Sed dementius Ariani, qui et Christum esse et peccata dimittere negare non audent: Deum esse negare non timent.

186CVERS. 9.-- Quid est. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Qua potentia cordis occulta intueor, eadem peccata dimitto. Quasi dicat: Ex vobis intelligite quid consequatur paralyticus.

VERS. 10.-- Filius hominis. (ID.) Solus Deus dimittit peccata, et Filius hominis habet potestatem dimittendi peccata: ergo idem Deus et Filius hominis est. Ita homo Christus per divinitatem dimittit peccata, per humanitatem pro peccatoribus potest mori.

VERS. 11.-- Vade in domum tuam. (ID.) Qui languerat, grabatum domum reportat, cum anima peccatorum remissione curata, se ad diuturnam sui custodiam cum ipso corpore refert: ne quid iterum unde feriatur admittat.

186DVERS. 13.-- Et egressus, etc. (ID.) Post curationem paralytici in Capharnaum ad mare egreditur, quia non solum cives, sed et maris habitatores instruit, et fluctivagos mundi motus fidei firmitate, etc., usque ad et ab undis cupiditatum separatos ad solitudinem, quiete conversationis quae est in spe coelestium, pertrahit.

VERS. 14.-- Levi. (HIER.) Levi additus, quia a telonio negotiorum saecularium sequitur solum verbum quod dicit: quia qui non renuntiat omnibus quae possidet, non potest meus esse discipulus (Luc. XIV).

(BEDA.) Marcus et Lucas verecundiae causa et honore evangelistae nomen vulgatum non ponunt. Ipse (secundum hoc quod scriptum est: Justus in primis 187Aaccusator est sui (Prov. XVIII); Matthaeum se et publicanum nominat: ut ostendat nullum conversum debere de salute diffidere cum apostolus et evangelista de publicano sit factus repente.

Secutus est eum. (ID.) Lucas plenius: Et relictis omnibus secutus est eum (Luc. V). Sequitur, qui ut pauperem Christum non tam corporis gressu quam mentis affectu possit sequi, omnia relinquit, etc., usque ad merito ergo qui obediendo humana contempsit negotia, divinorum dispensator factus est talentorum.

VERS. 15.-- Et factum est. (ID.) Lucas: Fecit eis convivium magnum Levi in domo sua (Luc. V). Qui enim Christum intus recipit, voluptatum delectatur ubertate, et Dominus libenter ingreditur, et in ejus 187Brecumbit affectu. Hoc est spirituale convivium bonorum pauperum, quo dives eget, pauper epulatur.

Publicani. Qui publica vectigalia exigunt, vel saeculi lucra sectantes, qui exemplo publicani conversi jam de salute praesumunt, nec ut Pharisaei calumniantur in vitiis permanentes, sed poenitentes.

(BEDA.) Ad convivium peccatorum vadit: ut docendi occasionem habeat, et spirituales cibos impertiat. Eundo ad peccatores, Domini humilitas ostenditur: in conversione poenitentium patet doctrinae ejus potentia.

(ID.) Per vocationem Matthaei fides gentium: quae, relictis lucris saeculi, epulis charitatis et operum bonorum cum Domino convivantur. Per pharisaeorum supercilium, de salute gentium Judaeorum invidia 187Cdesignatur, quibus dicitur: Publicani et meretrices praecedent vos in regno Dei (Matth. XXI).

VERS. 17.-- Non necesse. (ID.) Si medicum dicit, qui miro medicandi genere vulneratus est propter iniquitates nostras, et livore ejus sanati sumus (Isa. LIII).

VERS. 18.-- Et erant discipuli. (ID.) Alii evangelistae dicunt pharisaeos et discipulos Joannis hoc quaesivisse. Hic videtur dicere, quod alii quaesierunt quos ejusdem rei cura movisset. Unde colligitur hoc a pluribus fuisse objectum, a pharisaeis, a discipulis Joannis, a convivis, vel quibuslibet aliis.

(ID.) Discipuli Joannis et Pharisaeorum jejunant, quia qui operibus legis gloriantur, vel (quod pejus est) traditiones sequuntur, etc., usque ad manducavit 187Dcum peccatoribus, ut gratiam cernens potestatem agnosceres.

VERS. 19.-- Nonquid possunt. Matthaeus ita: Nunquid possunt filii sponsi lugere quandiu cum illis est sponsus, etc. (Matth. IX.) Sponsus Christus, sponsa Ecclesia, de quo spirituali connubio apostoli creati sunt, qui lugere et jejunare non possunt, quandiu sponsum cum sponsa vident. Quando transierint nuptiae, ac passionis et resurrectionis tempus advenerit, tunc jejunabunt.

(BEDA.) Quandiu sponsus nobiscum est, in laetitia sumus, nec jejunare possumus, nec lugere; cum vero pro peccato recedit et avolat, tunc jejunium indicendum, tunc luctus multiplicandus.

188AVERS. 21.-- Nemo assumentum. (ID.) Discipulos, vestimenta vetera dicit, id est, adhuc carnales, necdum innovatos fidei firmitate, etc., usque ad vinum, fervor fidei, spei et charitatis, quo in conspectu Dei intus sensus novitate reformamur.

VERS. 22.-- Sed vinum novum. Erant autem novi, cum post ascensionem desiderio consolationis ejus sperando et orando innovabantur: tunc Spiritum sanctum acceperunt, quo quasi novo musto novi utres repleti sunt.

VERS. 23.-- Cum sabbatis. (BEDA.) Nota primo apostolos litteram sabbati destruere contra Ebionitas, qui, cum alios discipulos recipiant, Paulum quasi transgressorem legis repudiant.

Et vellere spicas. Esuriunt ut homines, prae turba 188Bmanducandi spatium non habentes. Sed exemplum austerioris vitae praebent, non praeparatas epulas, sed simplices quaerunt.

(BEDA.) Discipuli per sata transeunt, cum praelati subditos pia sollicitudine circumspiciunt, et qualiter quemque ad fidem trahant, sedule, etc., usque ad hoc Dominus sabbati probat, stulti defensores sabbati improbant, qui solam litterae superficiem diligunt, mentis refectionem nesciunt, requiem animarum non norunt.

VERS. 25.-- Nunquam legistis, etc. Prae caeteris in medium affert Davidem, qui legis sanctimoniae adversatus videri potuit. Facit autem hoc eo consilio quo per honorem et reverentiam quam Davidi deferebant, compescat impudentem sycophantiam, 188Cquam apostolis maligne intentarunt. Quasi diceret: An non Davidem legis servitute obstrictum meministis? Ego autem, etsi Filius hominis dicar, sabbati Dominus sum.

VERS. 26.-- Sub Abiathar, etc. Non te turbet quod alii dicunt Achimelech, ambo enim tunc illic aderant. Achimelech pater, et Abrathar filius: qui occiso patre a Saule, comes exsilii David factus est, et eo regnante summus pontifex majoris nominis quam pater: et ideo dignus cujus Dominus mentionem faceret, quasi summi sacerdotis, etiam patre vivente.

Panes propositionis. (BEDA.) Quasi dicat: Si David et Achimelech a nobis non reprehenduntur, sed pro 188Dfame excusantur, cur in his causam improbatis quam in caeteris probatis? quamvis sit, etc., usque ad et omnes filii Ecclesiae sacerdotes sunt, juncti in sacerdotium sanctum, offerentes seipsos spirituales hostias Deo.

Et dedit eis. Hoc secutus. Misericordiam volo, et non sacrificium (Ose. VI): melius judicavit a famis periculo homines liberare quam sacrificium offerre. Hostia Deo placabilis, hominum salus.

VERS. 27.-- Homo propter sabbatum. (BEDA.) Ita sabbatum custodiri praeceptum est, ut si necessitas fuerit, non sit reus, etc., usque ad si quis jejunium fregerit aegrotus, reus non tenetur.

VERS. 28.-- Itaque Dominus. Quasi dicat: Si David rex et sacerdotes (secundum alium evangelistam) 189Apropter templi ministerium sabbatum violantes, crimine carent, quanto magis Filius hominis verus Rex et Sacerdos! et ideo Dominus sabbati in sabbato vulsarum spicarum crimine non tenetur reus.

CHAPTER II.

VERS. 2.-- So that there was no room, etc. (BEDA.) While the Lord preached, they are not contained in the house, nor at the door: because, while Christ preached in Judaea, the Gentiles did not yet wish to enter in to hear, yet to them (though set without) he directed the words of his doctrine by his preachers.

VERS. 3.-- The paralytic. (HIER.) The palsy is a type of that torpor in which the slothful man lies in the softness of the flesh, having a desire of health: but by the crowds, etc., as far as 185Dthat by another way he may return with the Magi into his own country.

He was carried by four. (BEDA.) Because by four virtues the confidence of the mind is raised up to the Lord. Of which it is said in the book of Wisdom: She teacheth sobriety and wisdom, and knowledge and strength (Sap. VIII), [Vulg. Sap 8:7 reads sobrietatem enim et prudentiam docet, et justitiam, et virtutem, "for she teacheth sobriety and prudence, and justice, and strength."] which others call prudence, fortitude, temperance and justice.

VERS. 4.-- They uncovered the roof where he was. The roof of the house in which Christ teaches is the sublimity of Scripture, by which a young man correcteth his way, in keeping thy words (Psal. CXVIII).

(BEDA.) The house of Jesus, according to another evangelist, is said to be roofed with tiles, because by the meanness of the letter the spiritual sense 186Ais covered. He is let down with his bed before Jesus, because a man ought to know Jesus even while set in this flesh.

VERS. 5.-- And when he had seen. (ID.) A man's own faith avails much with God, where another's availed so much: so that the man, healed within and without, rose up suddenly, and by the merits of others his own faults were loosed for him.

Son. Wonderful humility, him who was despised by all, loosened in all his limbs, he calls son. Or else for this reason, because his sins are forgiven him.

VERS. 6.-- Thy sins are forgiven thee. He shows that he had been stricken with disease on account of sins, and therefore could not be healed unless these were first forgiven. As to the paralytic at the pool: Behold thou art made whole, sin no more, 186Blest worse befall thee (Joan. V). (BEDA.) For five causes are men afflicted with troubles of the flesh. Either that their merits may be increased, as Job and the martyrs. Or for humility's sake, etc., as far as Lazarus, whose infirmity was not unto death, but for the glory of God, or unto the beginning of eternal damnation, as Herod.

VERS. 7.-- Who can forgive. (ID.) They say true: God alone can do this, and he alone forgives through those to whom he has given the power of forgiving sins, therefore he is truly God. They bear witness to the truth, but they are deceived in denying the person of Christ. They err, therefore, because, though they believe him to be God and to forgive sins, yet they do not believe that he is Christ. But more madly the Arians, who dare not deny that he is Christ and that he forgives sins: they fear not to deny that he is God.

186CVERS. 9.-- Which is easier. (ID.) As if he should say: By the same power by which I behold the secrets of the heart, by that same I forgive sins. As if he should say: From yourselves understand what the paralytic obtains.

VERS. 10.-- The Son of man. (ID.) God alone forgives sins, and the Son of man has power to forgive sins: therefore the same is both God and the Son of man. So Christ the man forgives sins through his divinity, and through his humanity can die for sinners.

VERS. 11.-- Go into thy house. (ID.) He who had been sick carries his bed back home, when the soul, healed by the remission of sins, betakes itself together with the body itself to a long keeping of itself: lest it again admit anything whereby it may be smitten.

186DVERS. 13.-- And he went forth, etc. (ID.) After the healing of the paralytic in Capharnaum he goes forth to the sea, because he instructs not only citizens, but the dwellers of the sea also, and the wave-wandering motions of the world by the firmness of faith, etc., as far as and them that are separated from the waves of covetousness he draws to solitude, by the quiet of that conversation which is in the hope of heavenly things.

VERS. 14.-- Levi. (HIER.) Levi is "added," because from the custom-house of worldly business he follows only the word which he speaks: for he that doth not renounce all that he possesseth cannot be my disciple (Luc. XIV).

(BEDA.) Mark and Luke, for modesty's sake and out of honour for the evangelist, do not set down the name commonly known. He himself (according to that which is written: The just man is first of all 187Athe accuser of himself (Prov. XVIII); names himself Matthew and a publican: that he may show that no convert ought to despair of salvation, since of a publican he was suddenly made an apostle and an evangelist.

He followed him. (ID.) Luke more fully: And leaving all things he followed him (Luc. V). He follows, who leaves all things that he may be able to follow Christ the poor not so much with the step of the body as with the affection of the mind, etc., as far as rightly therefore he who by obeying despised human business was made the steward of divine talents.

VERS. 15.-- And it came to pass. (ID.) Luke: Levi made a great feast for them in his own house (Luc. V). For he who receives Christ within is delighted with an abundance of pleasures, and the Lord gladly enters in, and reclines 187Bin his affection. This is the spiritual banquet of the good poor, which the rich man lacks and the poor man feasts upon.

Publicans. They who exact the public taxes, or those pursuing the gains of the world, who, converted by the example of the publican, now presume upon salvation, and slander not as the Pharisees do, remaining in vices, but repenting.

(BEDA.) He goes to the banquet of sinners: that he may have occasion of teaching, and may impart spiritual food. In going to sinners the humility of the Lord is shown: in the conversion of the penitent the power of his doctrine is made plain.

(ID.) By the calling of Matthew, the faith of the Gentiles: who, having left the gains of the world, feast with the Lord at the banquets of charity and of good works. By the pride of the Pharisees, the envy of the Jews at the salvation of the Gentiles 187Cis signified, to whom it is said: Publicans and harlots shall go before you in the kingdom of God (Matth. XXI).

VERS. 17.-- They have no need. (ID.) If he calls him a physician, who by a wondrous manner of healing was wounded for our iniquities, and by his bruises we are healed (Isa. LIII).

VERS. 18.-- And the disciples were. (ID.) Other evangelists say that the Pharisees and the disciples of John asked this. He seems here to say that others asked it, whom a concern for the same matter had moved. Whence it is gathered that this was objected by many, by the Pharisees, by the disciples of John, by the guests, or by any others whatsoever.

(ID.) The disciples of John and of the Pharisees fast, because they who glory in the works of the law, or (which is worse) follow traditions, etc., as far as he ate 187Dwith sinners, that thou, beholding the grace, mightest acknowledge the power.

VERS. 19.-- Can they. Matthew thus: Can the children of the bridegroom mourn as long as the bridegroom is with them, etc. (Matth. IX.) The bridegroom is Christ, the bride the Church, of which spiritual wedlock the apostles were created, who cannot mourn and fast as long as they see the bridegroom with the bride. When the nuptials shall have passed, and the time of the passion and of the resurrection shall have come, then shall they fast.

(BEDA.) As long as the bridegroom is with us, we are in gladness, and we can neither fast nor mourn; but when for sin he withdraws and flies away, then a fast is to be proclaimed, then mourning is to be multiplied.

188AVERS. 21.-- No man seweth a piece. (ID.) He calls the disciples old garments, that is, still carnal, not yet renewed by firmness of faith, etc., as far as the wine is the fervour of faith, of hope and of charity, by which we are inwardly reformed in the sight of God by newness of understanding.

VERS. 22.-- But new wine. And they were new, when after the ascension they were being renewed by hoping and praying in the desire of his consolation: then they received the Holy Ghost, with whom, as with new must, the new bottles were filled.

VERS. 23.-- When on the sabbath days. (BEDA.) Note first that the apostles do away with the letter of the sabbath against the Ebionites, who, though they receive the other disciples, repudiate Paul as a transgressor of the law.

And to pluck the ears of corn. They hunger as men, having no space 188Bfor eating because of the crowd. But they set forth an example of a more austere life, they seek not prepared feasts, but simple ones.

(BEDA.) The disciples pass through the corn, when prelates look upon their subjects with holy solicitude, and how they may draw each one to the faith, diligently, etc., as far as this the Lord of the sabbath approves, the foolish defenders of the sabbath disapprove, who love only the surface of the letter, know not the refreshment of the mind, are not acquainted with the rest of souls.

VERS. 25.-- Have you never read, etc. Before all others he brings David forward into the midst, who could seem to have opposed the sanctity of the law. And he does this with the design that, through the honour and reverence which they paid to David, he may check the shameless slander 188Cwhich they maliciously aimed at the apostles. As if he should say: Do you not remember that David was bound by the servitude of the law? But I, though I be called the Son of man, am the Lord of the sabbath.

VERS. 26.-- Under Abiathar, etc. Let it not trouble thee that others say Achimelech, for both were then present there. Achimelech the father, and Abrathar the son: who, when his father had been slain by Saul, was made the companion of David's exile, and, in his reign, high priest of greater name than his father: and therefore worthy that the Lord should make mention of him, as of a high priest, even while his father was living.

The loaves of proposition. (BEDA.) As if he should say: If David and Achimelech are not blamed by us, but are excused for 188Dhunger's sake, why do you condemn in these the cause which in the rest you approve? although it be, etc., as far as and all the sons of the Church are priests, joined into a holy priesthood, offering themselves as spiritual sacrifices to God.

And he gave to them. Following this. I will mercy, and not sacrifice (Ose. VI): he judged it better to free men from the peril of famine than to offer sacrifice. The victim well-pleasing to God is the salvation of men.

VERS. 27.-- Man for the sabbath. (BEDA.) The sabbath is so commanded to be kept, that if there be necessity, he is not guilty, etc., as far as if a sick man break the fast, he is not held guilty.

VERS. 28.-- Therefore the Lord. As if he should say: If David the king and the priests (according to another evangelist), 189Aviolating the sabbath because of the ministry of the temple, are free from crime, how much more the Son of man, the true King and Priest! and therefore the Lord of the sabbath is not held guilty of the crime of the ears of corn plucked on the sabbath.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 1.-- Manum aridam. (BEDA.) Quae ad fructum vetitum extensa est, a bono opere aruit, per manus Christi in cruce extensas, bonorum operum succis restituta.

(HIER.) Homo iste significat avaros, qui, nolentes dare, volunt accipere, praedari et non largiri: quibus dicitur ut extendant manus suas, id est, qui furabatur jam non furetur, magis autem laboret operans 189Bmanibus suis quod bonum est, ut habeat unde tribuat necessitatem patienti (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 2.-- Et observabant. (BEDA.) Quia discipulos probabiliter excusaverat: ipsum observant, ut si in sabbato curet, transgressionis: si non curet, crudelitatis aut imbecillitatis arguant.

Licet sabbatis benefacere. (ID.) Arguit, quod legis praecepta prava interpretatione violant, aestimantes in sabbato a bono feriandum, cum lex dicat, etc., usque ad similitudinem praeposuit de ove, et conclusit quod licet sabbato benefacere.

Animam. (ID.) Hominem totum per partem significat. Vel quia propter animam hoc faciebat, vel quod haec manus sanatio animae salutem significat.

Perdere. (ID.) Non quod summe pius quemquam 189Cpossit perdere, sed ejus non salvare est perdere. Sicut dicitur indurasse cor Pharaonis, non quod obduravit, sed obduratum non emollivit.

VERS. 5.-- Extende manum. (BEDA.) Infructuosa debilitas animae non melius curatur quam eleemosynae largitione; unde: Qui habet duas tunicas, det non habenti: et qui habet escas, similiter faciat (Luc. III). Et in Ecclesiastico: Fili, non sit manus tua porrecta ad capiendum et ad dandum collecta (Eccl. IV). Frustra enim in oratione manus ad Dominum expandit, qui eas ad rogantem viduam non extendit.

Et restituta est manus illi. Magnum studium nequitiae in crimen reputant., quod ad verbum illius sanata est manus. Neque et sabbato potest convinci 189Dlaborasse, qui dixit et facta sunt: cum ipsi in sabbatis cibos portent, calices porrigant, et caetera victui necessaria.

VERS. 6.-- Exeuntes autem. Qui per se non possunt, socios iniquitatis quaerunt. Cum Herodianis. Ministri Herodis propter inimicitias quas ipse adversus hominem exercebat, Jesum insidiis et odiis persequebantur quem Joannes praedicabat.

VERS. 7.-- Secessit. Fugit ut homo, quia nondum venerat hora ejus, et passionis ejus extra Jerusalem non erat locus.

(BEDA.) De synagoga secedit ad mare, etc. Judaeam relinquit, ad gentes venit cum discipulis, scilicet praedicantibus apostolis corda gentium adiit: et sic 190Amultos ad se venientes suscepit, et desideratam salutem dedit.

Et multa turba. (ID.) Magistri plebis et ministri regis odio persequuntur: vulgus indoctum dilectione sequitur, et plurimi sanari merentur.

VERS. 9.-- Navicula. Ecclesia de gentibus congregata fluctus saeculi mente transgressa: quae quanto sinum cordis gratiae recipiendae dilatat, tanto fluctus saeculi securius calcat. Ne comprimerent. Comprimunt, qui Ecclesiae pacem turbant. Tangunt, qui fidem vero corde et dilectione suscipiunt, qui tetigere sanati esse perhibentur: fugiens comprimentes naviculam ascendit.

VERS. 11.-- Et spiritus immundi, etc. (BEDA.) Uterque et plagas habentes, et immundi spiritus. 190BSed infirmi pia intentione salutis: daemoniaci, vel potius in eis daemones: non solum timore coacti ad procidendum, verum etiam ad confitendum majestatem ejus. Praesentia enim ejus perterriti, quem Filium Dei esse jamjamque noverant, celare non poterant. Saniores Arianis, qui etiam hodie negant Filium Dei esse.

VERS. 12.-- Ne manifestarent. (ID.) Non solum daemones, qui inviti, vel ab eo sanati, qui sponte confitentur: sed et apostoli toto orbe post passionem praedicaturi, ante passionem tacere jubentur, ne divina majestate praedicata passio differretur, et sic salus mundi.

VERS. 13.-- Et ascendens. (ID.) Postquam immundos spiritus se praedicare prohibuit, sanctos elegit, 190Cqui eum praedicarent et nefandos spiritus ejicerent.

Mons iste significat altitudinem justitiae, qua instruendi, et quam praedicaturi erant, per locum admonet ad coelestia erigi, sic et legem in monte dedit.

(HIER.) Consuetudo divinae Scripturae est allegorice loqui: sed nos sic excelsa sensibus nostris applicemus, ut historiae veritatem, etc., usque ad in montem vocantur excelsi et merito et verbo, ut locus congruat meritis altis.

VERS. 14.-- Ut essent duodecim. Speciem Jacob dilexit Deus cum videret excelsus gentes, et separaret filios Adam. Statuit terminos populorum juxta numerum filiorum Israel, ut sint super thronos duodecim, judicantes duodecim tribus Israel. Quibus 190Ddata est potestas, quam Filius accepit a Patre secundum carnem, ut opera quae ipse fecit et ipsi faciant, et majora horum faciant.

(BEDA.) Dedit etiam potestatem mortuos suscitandi, sicut Matthaeus dicit, ut virtus ostensa fidem, etc., usque ad quia linguae in signum sunt non fidelibus, sed infidelibus (I Cor. XIV).

VERS. 16.-- Et imposuit. (ID.) Non modo primum, sed cum adducto ab Andraea dixit: Tu vocaberis Cephas, quod interpretatur Petrus (Joan. I). Sed hic evangelista te attentum reddit dicens, quod, etc., usque ad vel audiens tristitiam mortis, scilicet cum ei dicitur: Alius te cinget et ducet quo tu non vis (I Cor. IV).

191AVERS. 17.-- Et Jacobum. Qui supplantata habet desideria carnis. Et Joannem, qui gratia accepit quod alii per laborem. Et imposuit eis nomen Boanerges, quod est filii tonitrui. Quorum sublime meritum in monte meretur audire tonitruum Patris per nubem de Filio tonantis: Hic est Filius meus dilectus (Matth. XVII), ut per nubem carnis et ipsi Christi ignem ac fulgura in pluviam spargerent in terris. quoniam Dominus in pluviam fulgura fecit, ut exstinguat misericordia quod judicium exurit; unde: Misericordiam et judicium cantabo tibi, Domine (Psal. C).

Jacobum. Qui curam carnis Domino vocante supplantavit, et ipsam carnem Herode trucidante contempsit.

191BJoannem. (BEDA.) In quo est gratia, vel Domini gratia, qui ob amoris praecipui gratiam quam virginali gloria promeruit, supra Redemptoris sui pectus in coena recubuit.

Filii. (ID.) Quorum unus intonans, vocem theologam (quam nemo prius edere noverat) emisit: In principio erat Verbum, etc. (Joan. I.) Quam tanto pondere gravidam reliquit, ut si aliquanto plus intonare voluisset, nec ipse mundus capere potuisset.

VERS. 18.-- Et Andream. (HIER.) Qui viriliter vim facit perditionis suae ut responsum mortis semper in se habeat et animam semper in manibus suis.

Et Philippum. (ID.) Qui os lampadis est, qui illuminat ore quod accepit, cui datum est opus oris 191Cilluminati. Hoc locutionis genus saepe Scriptura commemorat, quando nomina propter mysteria sunt posita: quod nunc evangelista, ex Dei persona vehementer affectans interserit.

Et Bartholomaeum. (ID.) Qui est filius suspendentis aquas, illius scilicet qui dixit: Mandabo nubibus meis ne pluant (Isa. V). Nomen vero filii Dei per pacem et dilectionem acquiritur: Beati enim pacifici, quoniam filii Dei vocabuntur (Matth. V). Et: Diligite inimicos vestros ut sitis filii Dei (Luc. VI).

Et Matthaeum. Qui est donatus: cui non solum remissio peccatorum, sed etiam in apostolorum numero esse datur, ut leo et bos simul comedant, et lupus cum agno (Isa. XI).

Nota, quod alii evangelistae Matthaeum praeponunt, 191Det publicanum non dicunt. Ipse post Thomam se ponit, et publicanum se dicit.

Et Thomam. (ID.) Qui est abyssus. Multi enim profunda sciunt, sed non proferunt. Unde scio hominem in Christo, etc., usque: quae non licet homini loqui (II Cor. XII).

(BEDA.) Thomas abyssus: quia altitudinem virtutis in resurrectione penetravit. Vel geminus, graece quod dicitur didymus, id est, dubius propter dubium cor, quia resurrectionem vix credidit.

Et Jacobum Alphaei. Id est, docti vel millesimi, cujus a latere cadent mille, et decem millia a dextris tuis (Psal. XC).

(BEDA.) Jacobum Alphaei, qui et frater Domini dicitur: 192Aquia Maria Alphaei fuit soror, etc., usque ad post resurrectionem statim Jerosolymorum ordinatur episcopus, tanquam doctus vel docti filius.

Thaddaeum. Custos cordis. Hunc Lucas in Evangelio, et in Actibus apostolorum Judam Jacobi nominat, quia frater Jacobi fratris Domini. Unde dicitur filius Mariae matris Jacobi et Joseph et Judae et Simonis.

(HIER.) Et Thaddaeum, qui est corculus, id est, cordis cultor qui servat cor suum omni custodia. Munditia namque cordis videtur Deus ut per vitrum mundum.

Et Simonem. (BEDA.) Simon Cananaeus a Cana vico Galilaeae, qui et Simon Zelotes, id est, aemulator. Cana, Zelus; Cananaeus, Zelotes.

192BEt Simonem Cananaeum. Qui et Zelotes Simon ponens vel habens tristitiam. Beati namque qui lugent, quoniam ipsi consolabuntur (Matth. V). Trinum luctum implet qui consolationem quaerit futuram. Deflet propria peccata cum David et Maria. Cum Paulo flet cum flentibus. Et flet multum cum Joanne qui dicebat: Et ego flebam multum: quia nemo dignus inventus est aperire librum et solvere signacula ejus (Apoc. V). Zelotes autem dicitur quem comedit zelus domus Dei, ut Phinees: Et cessavit quassatio (Num. XXV).

VERS. 19.-- Et Judam Scariotem. Qui non delet peccatum suum per poenitentiam, nec illud deletur per memoriam. Unde: Et peccatum matris ejus non deleatur. Fiat contra Dominum semper (Psal. CVIII).

192CJudas confitens vel gloriosus; Scarioth memoria mortis. Confessores multi sunt in Ecclesia superbi et gloriosi quorum primus Simon Magus, et alii haeretici, quorum memoria mortalis in Ecclesia celebratur: ut deleatur.

(HIER.) Judas Scarioth a vico in quo ortus, aut a tribu Issachar. Issachar merces, pretium scilicet perditionis: Scarioth, etc., usque ad quos unius exemplum ad timorem humiliat, ut angelos luciferi casus, ut Non glorietur sapiens in sapientia sua (Jer. IX), sed qui gloriatur, in Domino glorietur (I Cor. I).

Et veniunt, etc. (BEDA.) Qui aliorum curam susceperunt, sollicite custodire debent conscientiae donum, ut se prius, inde alios regant.

192DVERS. 20.-- (HIER.) Domus in qua conveniunt, primitiva Ecclesia est. Turbae quae impediunt panem manducare, peccata et vitia sunt, quia qui manducat corpus Domini indigne, judicium sibi manducat et bibit, non dijudicans corpus Domini (I Cor. XI). Unde Dominus in furorem vertitur cum dicit: Nisi manducaveritis carnem Filii hominis et biberitis ejus sanguinem, non habebitis vitam (Joan. VI).

VERS. 21.-- Et cum audissent, etc. Turba frequentat eum, sui tanquam furiosum despiciunt, in quo salus gentium notatur, et invidia Judaeorum reprobatur, de quibus: In propria venit, et sui eum non receperunt (Joan. VI).

193AVERS. 22.-- Et Scribae. (BEDA.) Si qui tarditate mentis non intelligunt verbum Dei, remanet tamen spes salutis; si forte intelligant, nulla. Qui autem intelligere nolunt et invertunt quod intelligunt, nulla spes est. Unde, Scribae, id est sapientes, etc. Ab Hierosolymis. Quod significat eum a civibus suis esse persequendum usque ad mortem. Legimus supra quia secuta est eum turba a Galilaea et Jerosolymis, et trans Jordanem et circa Tyrum et Sidonem, quae sunt civitates gentilium: et Scribae ab Jerosolymis blasphemabant: quia turba populi Judaeorum illum cum palmis et laudibus perduxit; gentiles desiderabant; Scribae et seniores populi de nece ejus tractabant. Beelzebub habet. Vir muscarum sive habens muscas: quo nomine principem 193Bdaemoniorum cognominabant propter sordes immolatitii cruoris.

VERS. 23.-- Quomodo potest Satanas, etc. (BEDA.) Eligant quod volunt. Si Satanas Satanam non potest ejicere, nihil contra Dominum dicunt. Si autem potest, recedant a regno ejus, quod divisum stare non potest.

VERS. 27.-- Et tunc domum ejus diripiet. (ID.) Domum diripuit, quia omnes mundi partes a diabolo possessas, apostolis et eorum successoribus quasi divisas provincias, ut populos ad fidem converterent, distribuit.

VERS. 29.-- Qui autem blasphemaverit. (HIER.) Quia non meretur poenitentiam agere ut recipiatur, qui Christum intelligens principem daemoniorum esse 193Cdicebat, etc., usque ad: Qui enim fecerit et docuerit, hic magnus vocabitur in regno coelorum (Matth. V).

(BEDA.) Matthaeus dicit (Matth. XII): Qui blasphemaverit in Spiritum sanctum vel verbum, etc. Ubi ostendit quaedam revelari in futuro. Quod de minimis intelligendum est, ut de sermone otioso, de risu nimio; quorum etiam haec vita veniam promeruit.

In aeternum. Hoc contra Origenem, qui omnibus peccatoribus veniam promittit post judicium transactis innumeris saeculis.

Reus erit aeterni delicti. (BEDA.) Non quia spiritum non esse: vel quia esse, sed non Deum esse: vel Deum esse, sed minorem Patre et Filio: quia hoc non invidia diabolica, sed humana ignorantia, etc.

193DVERS. 31.-- Fratres. (HIER.) Non filii Mariae semper Virginis, ut mentitur Helvidius: vel Joseph ex alia uxore, sicut alii garriunt, sed cognati.

(BEDA.) Mater et fratres, populus Judaeorum ex quibus Christus secundum carnem, foris stant in litterali sensu. Turba in domo, gentes in spirituali mysterio. Foris volunt Judaei videre Christum, quia, spiritualem intelligentiam non quaerentes, potius ad carnalia docenda eum exire cogunt vel quaerunt.

VERS. 33.-- Quae est mater. (ID.) Non refutat obsequi matri qui dicit: Honora patrem et matrem (Exod. XX): sed ostendit se plus debere paternis mysteriis, quam maternis affectibus, idem exemplo monstravit, quod verbo ait: Qui amat patrem aut 194Amatrem plus quam me, non est me dignus (Matth. X). Nec fratres injuriose contemnit, sed opus spirituale praeficit cognationi, docens meliorem esse copulam cordium quam corporum.

VERS. 34.-- Ecce mater mea, etc. (HIER.) Ut sciamus nos esse et fratres ejus et matrem si voluntatem Patris ejus fecerimus, ut cohaeredes simus, quia non in sexibus, sed in factis discernit. Soror et frater credendo, mater praedicando, dum per praedicationem amor Christi quasi ipse Christus in corde auditoris generatur.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1.-- The withered hand. (BEDA.) Which was stretched out to the forbidden fruit, and withered from good work, and was restored by the sap of good works through the hands of Christ stretched out upon the cross.

(HIER.) This man signifies the covetous, who, unwilling to give, wish to receive, to plunder and not to bestow: to whom it is said that they should stretch forth their hands, that is, he who stole, let him now steal no more, but rather let him labour, working 189Bwith his hands the thing which is good, that he may have wherewith to give to him that suffereth need (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 2.-- And they watched him. (BEDA.) Because he had plausibly excused the disciples: they watch himself, that if he heal on the sabbath they may accuse him of transgression: if he heal not, of cruelty or of weakness.

It is lawful to do good on the sabbath days. (ID.) He shows that they violate the precepts of the law by a wicked interpretation, thinking that on the sabbath one must keep holiday from good, whereas the law says, etc., as far as he set before them the likeness of the sheep, and concluded that it is lawful to do good on the sabbath.

A soul. (ID.) By the part he signifies the whole man. Either because he did this for the soul's sake, or because this healing of the hand signifies the salvation of the soul.

To destroy. (ID.) Not that he who is supremely merciful could 189Cdestroy anyone, but for him not to save is to destroy. As he is said to have hardened the heart of Pharao, not that he hardened it, but that he did not soften it when it was hardened.

VERS. 5.-- Stretch forth thy hand. (BEDA.) The unfruitful weakness of the soul is not better healed than by the bestowing of alms; whence: He that hath two coats, let him give to him that hath none: and he that hath meat, let him do in like manner (Luc. III). And in Ecclesiasticus: Son, let not thy hand be stretched out to take, and shut to give (Eccl. IV). For in vain does he spread out his hands to the Lord in prayer, who does not stretch them out to the widow that asks.

And the hand was restored unto him. A great zeal of wickedness, they reckon it for a crime., that at his word the hand was healed. Nor even on the sabbath can he be convicted 189Dof having laboured, who spoke and they were made: whereas they themselves on the sabbath carry food, hand round cups, and the rest that is necessary for living.

VERS. 6.-- And going forth. They who cannot do it by themselves seek companions of iniquity. With the Herodians. The ministers of Herod, because of the enmities which he himself was exercising against the man, pursued with snares and hatreds Jesus, whom John preached.

VERS. 7.-- He retired. He flees as man, because his hour had not yet come, and there was no place for his passion outside Jerusalem.

(BEDA.) From the synagogue he retires to the sea, etc. He leaves Judaea, he comes to the Gentiles with his disciples, that is, when the apostles preached he approached the hearts of the Gentiles: and so 190Ahe received many that came to him, and gave the salvation they had desired.

And a great crowd. (ID.) The teachers of the people and the ministers of the king pursue him with hatred: the unlearned common folk follow him with love, and very many deserve to be healed.

VERS. 9.-- A small ship. The Church gathered out of the Gentiles, having passed over the waves of the world in mind: which, the more it widens the bosom of the heart for the receiving of grace, the more securely it treads upon the waves of the world. Lest they should throng him. They throng him who trouble the peace of the Church. They touch him who receive the faith with a true heart and with love, and they who touched him are reported to have been healed: fleeing those who thronged him, he went up into the little ship.

VERS. 11.-- And the unclean spirits, etc. (BEDA.) Each of the two, both they that had plagues, and the unclean spirits. 190BBut the sick with a holy intention of health: the possessed, or rather the demons in them: compelled by fear not only to fall down, but also to confess his majesty. For, terrified by his presence, they could not hide him whom they now well knew to be the Son of God. Sounder than the Arians, who even to-day deny that he is the Son of God.

VERS. 12.-- That they should not make him known. (ID.) Not only the demons, who confess unwillingly, or those healed by him, who confess of their own accord: but the apostles also, who were to preach in all the world after the passion, are bidden to be silent before the passion, lest, his divine majesty being preached, the passion should be deferred, and so the salvation of the world.

VERS. 13.-- And going up. (ID.) After he had forbidden unclean spirits to preach him, he chose holy men, 190Cwho should preach him and cast out wicked spirits.

This mountain signifies the height of justice, in which they were to be instructed and which they were to preach, by the place he admonishes them to be raised up to heavenly things, and so he gave the law also upon a mountain.

(HIER.) It is the custom of divine Scripture to speak allegorically: but let us so apply high things to our own understandings, that the truth of the history, etc., as far as they are called into the mountain who are lofty both in merit and in word, that the place may accord with their high merits.

VERS. 14.-- That they should be twelve. God loved the beauty of Jacob when the Most High looked upon the nations, and divided the sons of Adam. He appointed the bounds of the peoples according to the number of the children of Israel, that they might be upon twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel. To whom 190Dwas given the power which the Son received from the Father according to the flesh, that they also might do the works which he himself did, and might do greater than these.

(BEDA.) He gave also the power of raising the dead, as Matthew says, that the power shown, faith, etc., as far as because tongues are for a sign not to believers, but to unbelievers (I Cor. XIV).

VERS. 16.-- And he imposed. (ID.) Not then for the first time, but when he said to him who had been brought by Andrew: Thou shalt be called Cephas, which is interpreted Peter (Joan. I). But here the evangelist makes thee attentive, saying that, etc., as far as or hearing the sadness of death, that is, when it is said to him: Another shall gird thee and lead thee whither thou wouldst not (I Cor. IV).

191AVERS. 17.-- And James. Who has supplanted the desires of the flesh. And John, who received by grace what others receive by labour. And he imposed on them the name Boanerges, which is, the sons of thunder.
