Whose lofty merit deserves to hear upon the mountain the thunder of the Father thundering through the cloud concerning the Son: This is my beloved Son (Matth. XVII), that through the cloud of the flesh they also might scatter upon the earth the fire and the lightnings of Christ into rain. for the Lord made the lightnings into rain, that mercy might quench what judgment burns; whence: I will sing to thee mercy and judgment, O Lord (Psal. C).

James. Who, at the Lord's calling, supplanted the care of the flesh, and, when Herod slew him, despised the flesh itself.

191BJohn. (BEDA.) In whom is grace, or the grace of the Lord, who, for the favour of that special love which he earned by the glory of virginity, reclined upon the breast of his Redeemer at the supper.

Sons. (ID.) Of whom one, thundering, uttered the theological voice (which no man before had known how to bring forth): In the beginning was the Word, etc. (Joan. I.) Which he left so heavy with weight, that if he had wished to thunder somewhat more, not even the world itself could have contained it.

VERS. 18.-- And Andrew. (HIER.) Who manfully does violence to his own perdition, that he may always have in himself the sentence of death, and his soul always in his own hands.

And Philip. (ID.) Who is the mouth of a lamp, who enlightens by his mouth what he has received, to whom is given the work of an enlightened 191Cmouth. This kind of speech Scripture often records, when names are set down for the sake of mysteries: which the evangelist now, earnestly aiming at it in the person of God, inserts.

And Bartholomew. (ID.) Who is the son of him that suspends the waters, of him namely who said: I will command my clouds that they rain not (Isa. V). But the name of a son of God is acquired through peace and love: for Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the sons of God (Matth. V). And: Love your enemies that you may be the sons of God (Luc. VI). [Vulg. Lc 6:35 reads filii Altissimi, "the sons of the Most High."]

And Matthew. Who is given: to whom is granted not only the remission of sins, but also to be in the number of the apostles, that the lion and the ox may eat together, and the wolf with the lamb (Isa. XI).

Note, that the other evangelists put Matthew before, 191Dand do not call him a publican. He himself sets himself after Thomas, and calls himself a publican.

And Thomas. (ID.) Who is an abyss. For many know deep things, but do not utter them. Whence I know a man in Christ, etc., even to: which it is not lawful for a man to utter (II Cor. XII).

(BEDA.) Thomas an abyss: because he penetrated the depth of the power in the resurrection. Or a twin, which in Greek is called didymus, that is, doubting, because of his doubting heart, since he scarcely believed the resurrection.

And James the son of Alpheus. That is, of the learned or of the thousandth, of whom at his side a thousand shall fall, and ten thousand at thy right hand (Psal. XC).

(BEDA.) James the son of Alpheus, who is also called the brother of the Lord: 192Abecause Mary of Alpheus was a sister, etc., as far as immediately after the resurrection he is ordained bishop of Jerusalem, as being learned or the son of a learned man.

Thaddeus. The keeper of the heart. Him Luke, in the Gospel and in the Acts of the Apostles, names Jude of James, because he was the brother of James the brother of the Lord. Whence he is called the son of Mary the mother of James and Joseph and Jude and Simon.

(HIER.) And Thaddeus, who is a little heart, that is, a tiller of the heart, who keeps his heart with all watchfulness. For by cleanness of heart God is seen, as through clean glass.

And Simon. (BEDA.) Simon the Cananean, from Cana a village of Galilee, who is also Simon Zelotes, that is, the zealous. Cana is zeal; the Cananean, Zelotes.

192BAnd Simon the Cananean. Who is also Zelotes, Simon laying down or having sorrow. For Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted (Matth. V). He fulfils a threefold mourning who seeks the comfort that is to come. He weeps for his own sins with David and Mary. With Paul he weeps with them that weep. And he weeps much with John, who said: And I wept much: because no man was found worthy to open the book and to loose the seals thereof (Apoc. V). [Vulg. Apc 5:4 closes nec videre eum, "nor to look upon it"; the words about loosing the seals stand at Apc 5:2.] But he is called Zelotes whom the zeal of the house of God eats up, as Phinees: And the slaughter ceased (Num. XXV).

VERS. 19.-- And Judas Scariot. Who does not blot out his sin by penance, nor is it blotted out by remembrance. Whence: And let not the sin of his mother be blotted out. Let it be against the Lord continually (Psal. CVIII).

192CJudas is confessing or glorious; Scarioth, the memory of death. There are many confessors in the Church, proud and boastful, of whom the first is Simon Magus, and other heretics, whose deadly memory is kept in the Church: that it may be blotted out.

(HIER.) Judas Scarioth, from the village in which he was born, or from the tribe of Issachar. Issachar is a wage, namely the price of perdition: Scarioth, etc., as far as whom the example of one humbles unto fear, as the fall of Lucifer humbled the angels, that Let not the wise man glory in his wisdom (Jer. IX), but he that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord (I Cor. I).

And they come, etc. (BEDA.) They who have undertaken the care of others ought carefully to guard the gift of conscience, that they may rule first themselves, and thence others.

192DVERS. 20.-- (HIER.) The house in which they come together is the primitive Church. The crowds which hinder the eating of bread are sins and vices, because he that eateth the body of the Lord unworthily, eateth and drinketh judgment to himself, not discerning the body of the Lord (I Cor. XI). [Vulg. 1 Cor 11:29 reads qui enim manducat et bibit indigne, "for he that eateth and drinketh unworthily," with no mention there of the body of the Lord.] Whence the Lord is turned into fury when he says: Unless you eat the flesh of the Son of man and drink his blood, you shall not have life (Joan. VI).

VERS. 21.-- And when they had heard, etc. The crowd throngs him, his own despise him as one mad, in which the salvation of the Gentiles is marked, and the envy of the Jews is reproved, of whom: He came unto his own, and his own received him not (Joan. VI).

193AVERS. 22.-- And the Scribes. (BEDA.) If any through slowness of mind do not understand the word of God, there yet remains a hope of salvation; if perchance they should understand, none. But for those who are unwilling to understand and who pervert what they do understand, there is no hope. Whence, Scribes, that is, the wise, etc. From Jerusalem. Which signifies that he was to be persecuted by his own fellow-citizens even unto death. We read above that a crowd followed him from Galilee and Jerusalem, and from beyond Jordan and about Tyre and Sidon, which are cities of the Gentiles: and the Scribes from Jerusalem were blaspheming: because the crowd of the people of the Jews conducted him with palms and praises; the Gentiles longed for him; the Scribes and the elders of the people were treating of his murder. He hath Beelzebub. Man of flies, or one having flies: by which name they surnamed the prince 193Bof demons, on account of the filth of the blood of the sacrifices.

VERS. 23.-- How can Satan, etc. (BEDA.) Let them choose which they will. If Satan cannot cast out Satan, they say nothing against the Lord. But if he can, let them withdraw from his kingdom, which, being divided, cannot stand.

VERS. 27.-- And then shall he plunder his house. (ID.) He plundered the house, because he distributed all the parts of the world possessed by the devil to the apostles and to their successors, as provinces parted out among them, that they might convert the peoples to the faith.

VERS. 29.-- But he that shall blaspheme. (HIER.) Because he does not deserve to do penance so as to be received, who, understanding Christ, was 193Csaying that he was the prince of demons, etc., as far as: For he that shall do and teach, he shall be called great in the kingdom of heaven (Matth. V).

(BEDA.) Matthew says (Matth. XII): He that shall blaspheme against the Holy Ghost or the word, etc. Where he shows that certain things are revealed in the world to come. Which is to be understood of the least matters, as of an idle word, of excessive laughter; for which even this life has earned pardon.

For ever. This against Origen, who promises pardon to all sinners after the judgment, when countless ages have passed.

He shall be guilty of an everlasting sin. (BEDA.) Not because [it is said] that he is not a spirit: or that he is, but is not God: or that he is God, but less than the Father and the Son: because this comes not of diabolical envy, but of human ignorance, etc.

193DVERS. 31.-- Brethren. (HIER.) Not sons of Mary ever Virgin, as Helvidius lies: nor of Joseph by another wife, as others prate, but kinsmen.

(BEDA.) The mother and the brethren, the people of the Jews of whom Christ is according to the flesh, stand outside in the literal sense. The crowd within the house, the Gentiles in the spiritual mystery. The Jews wish to see Christ outside, because, not seeking the spiritual understanding, they rather compel or seek him to come out to teach carnal things.

VERS. 33.-- Who is my mother. (ID.) He does not refuse obedience to a mother, who says: Honour father and mother (Exod. XX): but he shows that he owes more to his Father's mysteries than to a mother's affections, and he showed by example the same thing which he says in word: He that loveth father or 194Amother more than me, is not worthy of me (Matth. X). Nor does he contemn his brethren injuriously, but he sets the spiritual work before kinship, teaching that the bond of hearts is better than the bond of bodies.

VERS. 34.-- Behold my mother, etc. (HIER.) That we may know that we are both his brethren and his mother, if we shall have done the will of his Father, so that we may be co-heirs, because he discerns not by sexes but by deeds. Sister and brother by believing, mother by preaching, while through preaching the love of Christ, as it were Christ himself, is begotten in the heart of the hearer.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Et iterum coepit docere. (BEDA.) Exiens de domo ubi daemonium habere dicitur, docet ad 194Bmare, ut ostenderet se ob perfidiae culpam Judaeam relicturum et ad gentes transiturum. Unde apte praemittitur, quia praedicante eo in domo, mater et fratres foris steterunt.

(HIER.) Coepit docere ad mare, ut locus indicet auditores amaros et instabiles. Et ideo, etc., usque ad qui amara dulcificat, tristia convertit in gaudium, aegritudinem in salutem.

Navim ascendens, etc. (BEDA.) Ecclesiam, quae est in mari inter infideles, quam Deus gratia suae visitationis illustrat et dilectam sibi mansionem consecrat.

Et omnis turba circa mare. (HIER.) Hi sunt qui nuper ad audiendum verbum venerunt, et inde pietate animi, ab amaritudine reproborum tamquam a 194Cmari secuti sunt: sed nondum cum Domino in navi sunt, quia nondum coelestibus mysteriis vel sacramentis quae desiderant imbuti sunt.

VERS. 2.-- In parabolis multa. Parabola est rerum natura discrepantium sibi sub aliqua similitudine facta comparatio. More suae prudentiae loquitur, ut qui coelestia sapere non poterant, per similitudinem terrenam auditam percipere potuissent. Graece similitudo dicitur quando quod intelligi volumus, per aliquas comparationes indicamus, ut ferrum, aliquem durum, velocem, ventis comparamus et avibus. Idem fit in tricesimo sexagesimo et centesimo fructu, id est lege, prophetia et Evangelio, quod in ore trium testium mysterium regni in monte ostensum, id est, Moysi, Eliae et Jesu, consistit.

194DVERS. 4.-- Aliud cecidit secus viam, etc. Lucas: Et conculcatum est, et volucres coeli comederunt (Luc. VIII). Via mens, metu carnalium cogitationum trita: unde semen perit nec fructum affert, et a volucribus rapitur, id est a daemonibus, qui volucres coeli dicuntur sive quia coelestis et spiritualis naturae, vel, quia in aere habitant.

VERS. 15.-- Hi autem, etc. Differentias ponit eorum qui audiunt verbum. Sunt autem quidam, ut gentiles, qui nec audiunt.

VERS. 19.-- Reliqua concupiscentiae, etc. Quod praeceptum est. Praecipitur enim: Concupisces sapientiam: serva mandata (Prov. VII), etc. Et alibi: Beatus vir qui timet Dominum, in mandatis ejus volet 195Animis (Psal. III). Hae concupiscentiae bonae: qui reliqua concupiscit, aberrat.

VERS. 21.-- Lucerna, etc. (HIER.) Lucerna verbum Dei. Modius aut lectus, auditus inobedientium. Candelabrum, apostoli, quos illuminavit verbum Dei. Absconditum et occultum, quod palam fit cum a Domino tractatur.

(BEDA.) Modius, vita nostra divina praescientia mensurata. Lectus, caro, in qua, etc., usque ad quia ei carnis concupiscentias praeponit.

Nonne ut super candelabrum ponatur? (ID.) Ut dominetur praedicatio veritatis, subsit servitus corporis, per quam tamen excelsior appareat doctrina tanquam per candelabrum lucerna.

VERS. 22.-- Non est enim aliquid, etc. Quasi 195Bdicat: Nolite erubescere Evangelium, quia nihil occultum modo, quod non fiat palam in judicio, quando illuminabit Deus abscondita tenebrarum, et manifestabit consilia cordium (Matth. X); ubi vobis a Deo laus, illis autem poena aeterna.

VERS. 24.-- In qua mensura, etc. (HIER.) Etenim secundum mensuram fidei unicuique dividitur intelligentia mysteriorum, et adjicietur nobis donum scientiae, etiam adjicientur virtutes.

VERS. 25.-- Qui enim habet, etc. Scilicet qui fidem habet, habebit virtutem, et qui habet opus verbi, habebit intelligentiam mysterii. Et econtra, qui non habet fidem, deficit virtute; et qui non habet opus verbi ejus intelligentia caret; et qui non intelligit, auditum perdit, ac si non audisset.

195CVERS. 26.-- Sic est regnum Dei. (HIER.) Scriptura divina per terrenas similitudines monstrat coelestia sacramenta, sicut per totum Evangeliorum corpus constat. Regnum Dei est Ecclesia quae, etc., usque ad: Quoniam adest messis, id est consummatio saeculi, in qua justi gaudebunt qui in lacrymis seminaverunt.

VERS. 29.-- Mittit falcem. (VICT. ANT.) Messis quidem tempus consummationis, falx Dei verbum omni gladio ancipiti penetrabilius. Caeterum falx hoc loco non denotat, etc., usque ad omnes enim ferunt frumentum, etsi non aeque multum omnes.

VERS. 31.-- Sicut granum. (HIER.) Hoc semen iterum comparat grano sinapis, quod minimum in timore est, magnum in charitate, quae major est 195Domnibus oleribus, quia Deus charitas est, et omnis caro fenum, et qui infirmus est, olera manducet. Et facit ramos misericordiae et compassionis. Cujus sub umbra pauperes Christi qui sunt coeli animalia delectantur habitare.

Seminatum fuerit. A Christo sive ab homine in terra, vel in agro, id est in corde suo. Hic homo est sensus noster et animus, qui fovens granum praedicationis humore fidei facit pullulare in agro pectoris sui. Minus est, etc. Quasi contemptibilia praedicans, et primo non creditur, quia praedicat Deum hominem mortuum, crucifixum, hoc eloquentiae et sapientiae philosophorum comparatum minus invenitur.

196AVERS. 32.-- Ascendit, etc. Quae altitudine, amplitudine, annositate transcendit herbarum naturam: Alta est, quia ad coelestia sustollit; ampla, quia totum mundum occupavit; annosa, quia non poterit finiri. Oleribus. (BEDA.) Philosophicis disciplinis, quae tanto apertius excrescunt, viliores fiunt: quia sicut olera herbarum cito decident.

VERS. 34.-- Sine parabola. (ID.) Non quod nullum sermonem turbis apertum faceret, sed quia nullus sermo ejus invenitur in quo aliquid parabolae non inveniatur.

Seorsum autem discipuli. (HIER.) Illi enim digni erant audire mysteria in intimo penetrali sapientiae, qui, remotis cogitationum tumultibus, in solitudine virtutum permanebant. Sapientia enim est in tempore 196Botii discenda.

VERS. 36.-- In navi. (BEDA.) Allegorice: Mare saeculi aestus. Navicula, crux qua mundi fluctus ascendimus et ad littus patriae coelestis pervenimus. Aliae naves, etc., usque ad erant enim forsitan illae naves nuper factae et in portum tantum deductae, vel post maris pericula ad portum reductae.

VERS. 38.-- In puppi. (HIER.) Puppis mortuis pellibus vivos continet et fluctus arcet, et ligno solidatur, etc., usque ad: Tranquillitas magna; pax Ecclesiae post pressuras, sive theorica post activam vitam.

Dormiens. (BEDA.) Discipulis navigantibus Christus obdormit, quia fidelibus regni futuri quietem meditantibus et Spiritus sancti secundo flatu vel 196Cremigio proprii conatus mundi fluctus post terga jactantibus, subito Christi passio advenit, quo ascendente, etc., usque ad sed ad gubernatorem recurramus, illum sedulo excitemus, qui non servit, sed imperat ventis, qui omnia sedabit, qui portum salutis indulgebit.

VERS. 39.-- Tace, obmutesce. Nota quod omnis creatura sentit imperium Creatoris. Non quod omnia animata, ut mentiuntur haeretici, sed majestate conditoris quae nobis insensibilia, illi sunt sensibilia.

VERS. 40.-- Quid timidi estis? (BEDA.) Quasi dicat: Quid me praesente timetis? Cui simile est, quod post resurrectionem exprobravit incredulitatem illorum, et duritiam cordis, quia hi qui viderunt eum resurgere, non crediderunt (Marc. XVI). Et item: 196DO stulti et tardi corde ad credendum in omnibus quae locuti sunt prophetae! Nonne oportuit pati Christum et sic intrare in gloriam suam (Luc. XXIV)?

Et timuerunt. Matthaeus: Porro homines mirati sunt, dicentes: Qualis est, etc. (Matth. VIII.) Non ergo discipuli, sed alii qui in navi erant mirabantur, de eo dubitantes, cui mare et ventus obedirent. Vel etiam ipsi discipuli recte homines dicti, quia necdum noverant potentiam Salvatoris.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- And again he began to teach. (BEDA.) Going out from the house where he is said to have a demon, he teaches by the 194Bsea, that he might show that for the guilt of unbelief he would leave Judaea and pass over to the Gentiles. Whence it is fitly set before, that while he was preaching in the house, his mother and his brethren stood outside.

(HIER.) He began to teach by the sea, that the place might indicate hearers bitter and unstable. And therefore, etc., as far as who sweetens bitter things, turns sad things into joy, sickness into health.

Going up into a ship, etc. (BEDA.) The Church, which is in the sea among unbelievers, which God enlightens by the grace of his visitation and consecrates as a dwelling beloved to himself.

And all the crowd was about the sea. (HIER.) These are they who have lately come to hear the word, and thence, by the devotion of their mind, have followed away from the bitterness of the reprobate as from the 194Csea: but they are not yet with the Lord in the ship, because they are not yet steeped in the heavenly mysteries or sacraments which they desire.

VERS. 2.-- Many things in parables. A parable is a comparison made, under some likeness, of things that differ from one another in nature. He speaks after the manner of his own prudence, that they who could not savour heavenly things might have been able to perceive them through an earthly likeness that they had heard. In Greek it is called a likeness when we point out, by certain comparisons, that which we wish to be understood, as we compare iron with some hard man, and a swift man with winds and with birds. The same is done in the thirtyfold, sixtyfold and hundredfold fruit, that is, in the law, in prophecy and in the Gospel, because in the mouth of three witnesses, that is, of Moses, of Elias and of Jesus, the mystery of the kingdom shown on the mountain stands firm.

194DVERS. 4.-- Another fell by the way side, etc. Luke: And it was trodden down, and the birds of the air devoured it (Luc. VIII). The way, the mind, worn by the fear of carnal thoughts: whence the seed perishes and bears no fruit, and is snatched away by the birds, that is, by the demons, who are called birds of the air either because they are of a heavenly and spiritual nature, or because they dwell in the air.

VERS. 15.-- And these, etc. He sets down the differences of those who hear the word. But there are some, as the Gentiles, who do not even hear.

VERS. 19.-- The lusts after other things, etc. Which is a thing commanded. For it is commanded: Thou shalt covet wisdom: keep the commandments (Prov. VII) [Vulg. Prov. VII, 2 reads serva mandata mea, et vives, "keep my commandments, and thou shalt live", with no clause about coveting wisdom; the nearest received wording, Eccli. VI, 37, has the desire of wisdom given, not commanded], etc. And elsewhere: Blessed is the man that feareth the Lord, in his commandments he shall delight 195Aexceedingly (Psal. III). These desires are good: he that desires the other things goes astray.

VERS. 21.-- A candle, etc. (HIER.) The candle, the word of God. The bushel or the bed, the hearing of the disobedient. The candlestick, the apostles, whom the word of God enlightened. The hidden and the secret, which is made open when it is handled by the Lord.

(BEDA.) The bushel, our life measured by the divine foreknowledge. The bed, the flesh, in which, etc., as far as because he sets the lusts of the flesh before it.

Is it not that it may be set upon a candlestick? (ID.) That the preaching of truth may bear rule, and the service of the body lie subject beneath it, through which nevertheless the teaching may appear the loftier, as the candle by the candlestick.

VERS. 22.-- For there is nothing, etc. As if 195Bhe said: Be not ashamed of the Gospel, because there is nothing hidden now which shall not be made open in the judgment, when God shall enlighten the hidden things of darkness, and shall make manifest the counsels of hearts (Matth. X); where there is praise for you from God, but for them everlasting punishment.

VERS. 24.-- With what measure, etc. (HIER.) For according to the measure of faith the understanding of the mysteries is divided to each one, and the gift of knowledge shall be added to us, and virtues also shall be added.

VERS. 25.-- For he that hath, etc. That is, he that has faith shall have virtue, and he that has the work of the word shall have the understanding of the mystery. And on the contrary, he that has not faith fails of virtue; and he that has not the work of the word lacks the understanding of it; and he that does not understand loses the hearing, as though he had not heard.

195CVERS. 26.-- So is the kingdom of God. (HIER.) Divine Scripture shows the heavenly sacraments through earthly likenesses, as is plain throughout the whole body of the Gospels. The kingdom of God is the Church, which, etc., as far as: Since the harvest is at hand, that is, the consummation of the age, in which the just shall rejoice, they that have sown in tears.

VERS. 29.-- He putteth in the sickle. (VICT. ANT.) The harvest indeed is the time of the consummation, the sickle, the word of God, more piercing than any two-edged sword. But the sickle in this place does not denote, etc., as far as for all bear wheat, though not all equally much.

VERS. 31.-- As a grain. (HIER.) This seed he compares again to a grain of mustard, which is least in fear, great in charity, which is greater than 195Dall herbs, because God is charity, and all flesh is grass, and let him that is weak eat herbs. And it makes the branches of mercy and of compassion. Under whose shadow the poor of Christ, who are the living creatures of heaven, delight to dwell.

It shall have been sown. By Christ or by man in the earth, or in the field, that is, in his own heart. This man is our sense and our mind, which, cherishing the grain of preaching with the moisture of faith, makes it sprout in the field of its own breast. It is less, etc. As one preaching things contemptible, and at the first he is not believed, because he preaches God a man, dead, crucified, this, compared with the eloquence and the wisdom of the philosophers, is found less.

196AVERS. 32.-- It groweth up, etc. Which in height, in breadth, in length of years surpasses the nature of herbs: it is high, because it lifts up to heavenly things; broad, because it has taken possession of the whole world; full of years, because it shall not be able to be ended. Than herbs. (BEDA.) The philosophic disciplines, which grow up so much the more openly, become the more worthless: because, like the pot-herbs of the grasses, they will quickly fall.

VERS. 34.-- Without a parable. (ID.) Not that he made no discourse plain to the crowds, but because no discourse of his is found in which something of parable is not found.

But apart, the disciples. (HIER.) For they were worthy to hear the mysteries in the innermost sanctuary of wisdom, who, the tumults of their thoughts being removed, abode in the solitude of the virtues. For wisdom is to be learned in a time 196Bof leisure.

VERS. 36.-- In the ship. (BEDA.) Allegorically: the sea, the surge of the age. The little ship, the cross, by which we mount the waves of the world and come to the shore of the heavenly country. Other ships, etc., as far as for those ships perhaps had lately been made and only brought down into the harbour, or had been brought back to harbour after the perils of the sea.

VERS. 38.-- In the stern. (HIER.) The stern with dead skins holds the living and keeps off the waves, and is made solid with wood, etc., as far as: A great calm; the peace of the Church after her afflictions, or the contemplative after the active life.

Sleeping. (BEDA.) While the disciples are sailing Christ falls asleep, because, while the faithful are meditating on the rest of the kingdom to come and, by the favouring breath of the Holy Ghost or by the 196Coarage of their own effort, are casting the waves of the world behind their backs, suddenly the passion of Christ comes upon them, and as he goes up, etc., as far as but let us run back to the pilot, let us diligently rouse him, who does not serve the winds but commands them, who will still all things, who will grant the harbour of salvation.

VERS. 39.-- Peace, be still. Note that every creature feels the command of the Creator. Not that all things are animate, as the heretics lie, but that by the majesty of the founder the things which are insensible to us are sensible to him.

VERS. 40.-- Why are you fearful? (BEDA.) As if he said: Why do you fear while I am present? To which is like that which, after the resurrection, he upbraided their unbelief and hardness of heart, because they who saw him rise again did not believe (Marc. XVI). And again: 196DO foolish and slow of heart to believe in all things which the prophets have spoken! Ought not Christ to have suffered, and so to enter into his glory (Luc. XXIV)?

And they feared. Matthew: But the men wondered, saying: What manner of man is this, etc. (Matth. VIII.) Not therefore the disciples, but others who were in the ship, were wondering, doubting of him whom the sea and the wind obeyed. Or even the disciples themselves are rightly called men, because they did not yet know the power of the Saviour.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Et venerunt, etc. (BEDA.) Gerasa urbs insignis Arabiae, trans Jordanem, juncta monti Galaad in tribu Manasse, non longe a stagno Tiberiadis, 197Ain quo porci praecipitati sunt, significat, etc., usque ad et qui erat longe, factus est prope in sanguine Christi.

VERS. 2.-- Homo in spiritu. (ISID.) Homo iste desperatissimus est populus gentium quem enumerat Apostolus. Elatus, superbus, immundus, sanguinarius, idololatra, ignominiosus, etc., usque ad flagellato enim stulto sapiens sapientior fit vel prudentior.

VERS. 3.-- Et neque catenis, etc. (BEDA.) Catenis et compedibus graves, et duae leges gentium signantur, quibus peccata in re eorum publica cohibebantur. Ruptis catenis (ut Lucas scribit) subito agebatur a Domino in deserto, quia etiam transgressis illis legibus ad ea scelera cupiditate raptabantur, quae jam vulgarem excederent consuetudinem.

197BVERS. 7.-- Torqueas. (ID.) Magnum tormentum ei a laesione hominis cessare, et tanto dimittit gravius quanto possidet diutius.

VERS. 9.-- Quod tibi nomen est? (BEDA.) Non quasi nescius interrogat, sed ut confessa publica peste quam toleraverat, virtus curantis gratior appareat. Sed etiam sacerdotes nostri temporis qui per exorcismum daemones ejiciunt solent dicere: non aliter patientes posse curari, nisi quantum sapere possunt, omne quod a daemonibus visu, auditu, vel quolibet sensu corporis aut animi vigilantes dormientesve pertulerunt, patenter confiteantur.

Legio mihi nomen est. (ID.) Significat populum gentilem innumeris idololatriae cultibus mancipatum: cui contra, est, etc., usque ad reprobos autem plures 197Csectae quam linguae dissociando confundunt.

VERS. 12.-- Mitte nos. (ID.) Lucas: Et rogabant eum ne imperaret illis, ut in abyssum irent (Luc. VIII). Sciebant ergo aliquando futurum ut per adventum ejus mitterentur in abyssum, non futura, etc., usque ad nam nisi quis porci more vixerit, in eum diabolus potestatem non accipit, nisi forte ad probandum, non autem ad perdendum.

VERS. 13.-- Grex praecipitatus est. (BEDA.) Quia jam clarificata Ecclesia, et liberato populo gentium a daemonum dominatione, in abditis agunt sacrilegos ritus, etc., usque ad quoniam ob unius hominis salutem duo millia porcorum suffocantur.

VERS. 14.-- Qui autem. Quia quidam primates impiorum, quamvis Christianam legem fugiant, potentiam 197Dtamen ejus stupendo et mirando per gentes praedicant.

VERS. 15.-- Et vident illum. (BEDA.) Lucas: Sedentem ad pedes ejus. quod signat eos qui a peccatis correcti sunt, fixa mentis intentione vestigia Salvatoris quae sequantur, intueri.

Vestitum. Virtutum studio, quod vesana mente perdiderat. Unde filius prodigus ad patrem rediens, mox stola prima cum annulo induitur: quia quisquis vero corde poenituerit potest donante Christi gratia pristina justitiae opera, de quibus ceciderat, cum annulo inviolatae fidei recuperare.

Et timuerunt. Multi enim antiqua suavitate delectati honorare se, sed pati nolle Christianam legem 198Adicunt, quia eam implere non possunt, admirantes tamen fidelem populum a pristina conversatione sanatum.

VERS. 17.-- Et rogare coeperunt eum. (RAB.) Ut Petrus: Exi a me, Domine, quia homo peccator sum, etc. (Luc. V.) Ne tangant arcam Dei, ut Oza.

VERS. 19.-- Vade in domum tuam. (BEDA.) Sic quisque post resurrectionem peccatorum in conscientiam bonam redeat et propter aliorum salutem Evangelio serviat: ut post cum Christo quiescat, ne dum praepropere vult jam esse cum Christo, negligat praedicationis mysterium fraternae redemptioni accommodatum.

(HIER.) Homo sanatus mittitur in domum suam, id est, in spiritualem legis observantiam: et praedicat, 198Betc., usque ad quia moralia ad litteram observanda sunt.

(BEDA.) Matthaeus duos, Marcus et Lucas unum a daemone curatum dicunt, quia unus eorum clarioris nominis, est famosioris: et ideo curatio ejus est famosior. Allegorice: Duo, gentes significant de duobus filiis Noe. Cum enim Noe tres filios genuerit, unus assumptus est in possessionem Dei, duo idolis dediti.

VERS. 22.-- Et venit quidam, etc. (HIER.) Post haec Jairus archisynagogus venit, quia cum intraverit plenitudo gentium, tunc omnis Israel, etc., usque ad Abraham et Moyses et Samuel rogant pro plebe mortua, et sequitur Jesus preces eorum.

(BEDA.) Jairus illuminans vel illuminatus: quia 198Cverbis vitae populum illuminavit: et ut hoc posset, a Spiritu sancto illuminatus est.

VERS. 23.-- Quoniam filia mea. (BEDA.) Lucas: Quia filia unica erat illi fere annorum duodecim, et haec moriebatur (Luc. VIII). Filia Archisynagogi est Synagoga sola legali institutione, etc., usque ad subito errorum languore consternata, spirituales vias desperabiliter omisit.

VERS. 24.-- Et comprimebant. Dum ad puellam sanandam vadit, a turba comprimitur: quia Judaeis salutis monita praebens, quibus aegram eorum conscientiam erigeret, noxia carnalium populorum consuetudine gravatus est.

VERS. 25.-- Et mulier, etc. Pergente Domino ad filiam archisynagogi, morbosa mulier annis duodecim 198Dpraeripit sanitatem, et post filia sanata est et suscitata est: quia sic dispensata est salus humani generis, ut primo aliqui ex Israel, deinde plenitudo gentium, et sic omnis Israel salvus fiet.

(BEDA.) Notandum quod puella duodenis et mulier duodecim annis sanguine fluxit, etc., usque ad haec dum Christus Judaeam sanare discerneret, jam paratam aliisque promissam salutem spe certa praevenit.

VERS. 26.-- Multa perpessa, etc. (ID.) Falsis theologis, philosophis, legum saecularium doctoribus, qui se utilia vivendi praecepta dare promittebant. Daemones quoque, etc., usque ad coeperunt languoris sui et sperare et inquirere remedium.

199AVERS. 27.-- Venit in turba. (ID.) Si quis mihi ministrat, me sequatur (Joan. XII). Et alibi: Post Dominum Deum ambulabis (Ose. XI). Vel quia Deum praesentem in carne non videt, sed post ad fidei agnitionis gratiam, etc., usque ad sacramentis Evangelicis, quasi suis vestimentis, fontes obsceni sanguinis emundat.

VERS. 31.-- Tetigit. (BEDA.) Sola fidelis mulier Dominum tangit, quem passim comitans turba comprimit. Quia qui diversis inordinate glomerantium haeresibus, vel perversis moribus gravatur, solo fideli Ecclesiae corde tangitur; unde: Noli me tangere: nondum enim ascendi ad Patrem meum (Joan. XX). Quia veraciter tangere est eum aequalem Patri credere.

199B(HIER.) Septima virtute suscitatur mortua, sed inter viam tangit fimbriam retro mulier sanguinaria, quae cum salute substantiam, etc., usque ad cymbalum confessionem; malum punicum, martyrii voluntatem significat et virtutum candorem.

VERS. 33.-- Mulier autem. (BEDA.) Ecce quo tendebat interrogatio, ut mulier scilicet confiteatur veritatem diuturnae infirmitatis, et subitae credulitatis et sanationis: et ita ipsa confirmaretur in fide et aliis praeberet exemplum.

VERS. 35.-- Adhuc, etc. (BEDA.) Muliere sanata statim nuntiatur puella mortua: quia Ecclesia gentium a labe vitiorum curata, et ob fidei meritum, etc., usque ad sed quod hominibus est impossibile, Deo est possibile.

199CVERS. 36.-- Archisynagogo. Archisynagogus coetus est doctorum legis de quibus: Super cathedram Moysis sederunt scribae et Pharisaei (Matth. XXVIII). Qui si credere voluerint, etiam subjecta eis Synagoga salva erit.

VERS. 37.-- Et non admisit, etc. (BEDA.) Blasphemis et irrisoribus non sunt revelanda mysteria, sed fidelibus qui honorent. Unde alibi: Justificata est sapientia ab omnibus filiis suis (Matth. XI).

VERS. 38.-- Flentes et ejulantes. (ID). Laetentur omnes qui sperant in Domino, et in aeternum exsultabunt, et habitabis in eis (Psal. V). Hanc laetitiam merito infidelitatis Synagoga amisit: et ideo inter flentes et ululantes mortua jacet. Sed Jesus eam funditus interire non patitur.

199DVERS. 39.-- Non est mortua. Mihi, in cujus dispositione et anima recepta vivit, et caro suscitanda quiescit. Hinc mos est Christianis, ut mortui qui resurrecturi non dubitantur, dormientes vocentur; unde: Nolumus vos ignorare, fratres, de dormientibus (I Thess. IV).

(BEDA.) Cum anima quae peccaverit moriatur, ea tamen quam Dominus ad vitam aeternam praevidet, nobis quidem mortua fuisse, sed ei obdormisse non incongrue dici potest.

VERS. 40.-- Ejectis omnibus. (ID.) Turba ejicitur ut puella suscitetur: quia nisi saecularium curarum multitudo a corde ejiciatur, anima quae intus jacet mortua non suscitatur. Dum enim se per universas 200Acogitationes spargit, ad considerationem sui sese nullatenus colligit.

VERS. 41.-- Talitha cumi. In Syro sermone quem evangelista ponit, non plus est quam: Puella, surge. Sed forte ad vim Dominicae jussionis exprimendam hoc addidit: Tibi dico, surge, et magis sensum loquentis quam verba intimare curavit. Sic saepe apostoli et evangelistae cum aliquod testimonium assumunt, magis sensum quam verba ponunt.

(HIER.) Ad puellam dicitur Talitha cumi, id est, Puella, surge. Archisynagogo, etc., usque ad unde dicitur: Credidit Abraham Deo, et reputatum est ei ad justitiam (Jac. II).

(BEDA.) Quod Christus tres mortuos suscitavit, tria genera animarum significat. Puellam suscitat, etc., 200Busque ad quartum mortuum annuntiante discipulo audivit: sed quia vivi qui pro eo precarentur defuerunt: Dimitte, inquit, mortuos sepelire mortuos suos (Matth. VIII).

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- And they came, etc. (BEDA.) Gerasa, a notable city of Arabia beyond Jordan, joined to mount Galaad in the tribe of Manasses, not far from the lake of Tiberias, 197Ainto which the swine were cast headlong, signifies, etc., as far as and he that was far off has been made nigh in the blood of Christ.

VERS. 2.-- A man with an unclean spirit. (ISID.) That most desperate man is the people of the Gentiles whom the Apostle enumerates. Puffed up, proud, unclean, bloody, an idolater, dishonourable, etc., as far as for when the fool is scourged the wise man becomes wiser, or more prudent.

VERS. 3.-- And not even with chains, etc. (BEDA.) Heavy with chains and fetters, and the two laws of the Gentiles are signified, by which sins were restrained in their commonwealth. The chains being burst (as Luke writes), he was suddenly driven by the Lord into the desert, because, those laws also being transgressed, they were hurried by lust into those crimes which now exceeded the common usage.

197BVERS. 7.-- Torment me not. (ID.) It is a great torment to him to cease from the injuring of a man, and by so much the more grievously does he let go as the longer he possesses.

VERS. 9.-- What is thy name? (BEDA.) He asks not as though ignorant, but that, the public plague which he had endured being confessed, the power of him that heals may appear the more welcome. But the priests also of our time, who cast out demons by exorcism, are wont to say: that the sufferers cannot be healed otherwise, unless, so far as they are able to understand, they plainly confess all that they have endured from the demons by sight, by hearing, or by any sense of body or of mind, waking or sleeping.

My name is Legion. (ID.) It signifies the Gentile people made over to countless cults of idolatry: against which is, etc., as far as but more 197Csects than tongues confound the reprobate by setting them at variance.

VERS. 12.-- Send us. (ID.) Luke: And they besought him that he would not command them to go into the abyss (Luc. VIII). They knew therefore that it would one day come to pass that by his coming they should be sent into the abyss, not as things now to be, etc., as far as for unless a man live after the manner of a swine, the devil receives no power over him, unless perchance to prove him, but not to destroy him.

VERS. 13.-- The herd was cast headlong. (BEDA.) Because, the Church being now made glorious and the people of the Gentiles set free from the dominion of the demons, they carry on their sacrilegious rites in hidden places, etc., as far as since for the salvation of one man two thousand swine are drowned.

VERS. 14.-- But they that fed them. Because certain chief men of the impious, although they flee the Christian law, yet by being amazed at its power 197Dand wondering at it, preach it among the Gentiles.

VERS. 15.-- And they see him. (BEDA.) Luke: Sitting at his feet. which signifies that they who have been corrected from their sins behold, with fixed intention of mind, the footsteps of the Saviour which they are to follow.

Clothed. With the zeal of the virtues, which he had lost by a maddened mind. Whence the prodigal son, returning to his father, is straightway clothed with the first robe together with the ring: because whosoever shall have repented with a true heart can, by the gift of Christ's grace, recover the former works of justice from which he had fallen, together with the ring of unviolated faith.

And they feared. For many, delighted with the old sweetness, say that they honour the Christian law but are unwilling to suffer for it, 198Abecause they cannot fulfil it, yet admiring the faithful people healed from their former manner of life.

VERS. 17.-- And they began to beseech him. (RAB.) As Peter: Depart from me, O Lord, for I am a sinful man, etc. (Luc. V.) Lest they touch the ark of God, as Oza.

VERS. 19.-- Go into thy house. (BEDA.) So let each one, after the resurrection from sins, return into a good conscience and serve the Gospel for the salvation of others: that afterwards he may rest with Christ, lest, while he too hastily wishes to be already with Christ, he neglect the mystery of preaching that is fitted to his brother's redemption.

(HIER.) The man that is healed is sent into his own house, that is, into the spiritual observance of the law: and he preaches, 198Betc., as far as because moral things are to be observed according to the letter.

(BEDA.) Matthew says that two, Mark and Luke that one, were healed of the demon, because one of them was of a more illustrious name, is of a more famous: and therefore his healing is the more famous. Allegorically: the two signify the nations from the two sons of Noe. For whereas Noe begot three sons, one was taken into the possession of God, two were given over to idols.

VERS. 22.-- And there cometh a certain man, etc. (HIER.) After these things Jairus the ruler of the synagogue comes, because when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have entered in, then all Israel, etc., as far as Abraham and Moses and Samuel pray for the dead people, and Jesus follows their prayers.

(BEDA.) Jairus, enlightening or enlightened: because 198Che enlightened the people with the words of life: and that he might be able to do this, he was enlightened by the Holy Ghost.

VERS. 23.-- For my daughter. (BEDA.) Luke: For he had one only daughter, about twelve years old, and she was dying (Luc. VIII). The daughter of the ruler of the synagogue is the Synagogue, by the legal institution alone, etc., as far as suddenly laid low by the languor of errors, she despairingly forsook the spiritual ways.

VERS. 24.-- And they thronged him. While he goes to heal the girl, he is thronged by the crowd: because, offering to the Jews the warnings of salvation by which he might raise up their sick conscience, he was burdened by the harmful custom of carnal peoples.

VERS. 25.-- And a woman, etc. As the Lord goes on toward the daughter of the ruler of the synagogue, the diseased woman of twelve years 198Dsnatches health before her, and afterwards the daughter was healed and was raised up: because the salvation of the human race was so dispensed that first some out of Israel, then the fulness of the Gentiles, and so all Israel shall be saved.

(BEDA.) It is to be noted that the girl was twelve years old and that the woman flowed with blood twelve years, etc., as far as while Christ was determining to heal Judaea, she forestalled with sure hope the salvation already prepared and promised to others.

VERS. 26.-- Having suffered many things, etc. (ID.) From false theologians, philosophers, teachers of the secular laws, who promised that they would give useful precepts of living. The demons too, etc., as far as they began both to hope for and to seek out a remedy for their languor.

199AVERS. 27.-- She came in the crowd. (ID.) If any man minister to me, let him follow me (Joan. XII). And elsewhere: Thou shalt walk after the Lord God (Ose. XI). Or because she does not see God present in the flesh, but afterwards comes to the grace of the acknowledgment of faith, etc., as far as by the evangelical sacraments, as by his own garments, he cleanses the fountains of unclean blood.

VERS. 31.-- Who touched me. (BEDA.) The faithful woman alone touches the Lord, whom the crowd accompanying him on every side throngs. Because he, who is burdened by the diverse heresies of them that disorderly throng together, or by perverse manners, is touched only by the faithful heart of the Church; whence: Touch me not: for I am not yet ascended to my Father (Joan. XX). Because to touch him truly is to believe him equal to the Father.

199B(HIER.) By the seventh virtue the dead girl is raised, but on the way the woman with the issue of blood touches the hem from behind, who together with health her substance, etc., as far as the cymbal, confession; the pomegranate signifies the will of martyrdom and the whiteness of the virtues.

VERS. 33.-- But the woman. (BEDA.) Behold whither the questioning tended, namely, that the woman should confess the truth of her long infirmity, and of her sudden believing and healing: and that so she herself might be confirmed in faith and might afford an example to others.

VERS. 35.-- While he was yet, etc. (BEDA.) The woman being healed, straightway the girl is announced dead: because the Church of the Gentiles, cured of the stain of vices, and for the merit of her faith, etc., as far as but what is impossible to men is possible to God.

199CVERS. 36.-- To the ruler of the synagogue. The ruler of the synagogue is the company of the teachers of the law, of whom: Upon the chair of Moses have sitten the scribes and the Pharisees (Matth. XXVIII). And if they shall be willing to believe, the Synagogue also that is subject to them shall be saved.

VERS. 37.-- And he admitted not, etc. (BEDA.) The mysteries are not to be revealed to blasphemers and mockers, but to the faithful who may honour them. Whence elsewhere: Wisdom is justified by all her children (Matth. XI).

VERS. 38.-- Weeping and wailing. (ID). Let all rejoice that hope in the Lord, and they shall exult for ever, and thou shalt dwell in them (Psal. V). This gladness the Synagogue lost by the desert of her unbelief: and therefore she lies dead among those that weep and howl. But Jesus does not suffer her to perish utterly.

199DVERS. 39.-- She is not dead. To me, in whose disposing both the soul that has been received lives and the flesh that is to be raised rests. Hence it is a custom among Christians that the dead, who are not doubted to be about to rise again, are called sleepers; whence: We will not have you ignorant, brethren, concerning them that sleep (I Thess. IV).

(BEDA.) Since the soul that shall have sinned dies, yet that soul which the Lord foresees unto eternal life may not unfittingly be said to have been dead indeed to us, but to have fallen asleep to him.

VERS. 40.-- All being cast out. (ID.) The crowd is cast out that the girl may be raised: because unless the multitude of secular cares be cast out from the heart, the soul that lies dead within is not raised. For while it scatters itself through all 200Aits thoughts, it in no wise gathers itself to the consideration of itself.

VERS. 41.-- Talitha cumi. In the Syriac speech which the evangelist sets down it is no more than: Girl, arise. But perhaps to express the force of the Lord's command he added this: I say to thee, arise, and took care to convey the sense of the speaker rather than his words. So often the apostles and the evangelists, when they take up some testimony, set down the sense rather than the words.

(HIER.) To the girl it is said Talitha cumi, that is, Girl, arise. To the ruler of the synagogue, etc., as far as whence it is said: Abraham believed God, and it was reputed to him unto justice (Jac. II).

(BEDA.) That Christ raised three dead persons signifies three kinds of souls. He raises the girl, etc., 200Bas far as he heard of a fourth dead man when a disciple announced it: but because living men to pray for him were wanting: Let, he says, the dead bury their dead (Matth. VIII).

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 2.-- Unde huic, etc. Mira caecitas. In factis et dictis Christum cognoscere possunt, ob solam generis notitiam contemnunt.

VERS. 3.-- Faber. (BEDA.) Qui operatur igni et spiritu; unde: Ipse baptizabit vos in igne et spiritu (Joan. I). Faber est qui fabricatus est auroram et solem.

VERS. 4.-- Non est propheta. Prope naturale est cives civibus invidere quia non opera, sed infantiam 200Cquam viderunt recordantur.

(ISID.) Comitatur saepe vilitas originem. Ut, Quis est filius Isai (III Reg. XII). Sed: Humilia Dominus respicit, et alta a longe cognoscit (Psal. CXXXVII).

VERS. 5.-- Et non poterat, etc. Ne cives incredulos pejus condemnaret faciens multas virtutes. Vel quia in patria sua despicitur, paucas ibi virtutes fecit et signa, ne penitus excusabiles sint. Majora quotidie in gentibus signa facit, non tam in corporum sanatione quam animarum.

VERS. 6.-- Et mirabatur. (BEDA.) Non quasi de improviso, sed quasi mirandum ostendit, quia nec prophetis credunt, nec ipsi quem praesentem cernunt.

VERS. 7.-- Et vocavit. (HIER.) Mittuntur duodecim 200Dapostoli, et datur eis potestas praecepta docendi ut comitetur verbum simul et factum: et cum promissis invisibilibus virtutes misceantur, et sic cum ungunt aegros oleo, infirmitatem fidei virtute corroborant.

Et dabat illis, etc. Benignus Dominus et magister non invidet discipulis et servis potestatem suam. Sed ipse potestate sua agit, illi sua infirmitate, si quid agunt, et Domini virtutem confitentur dicentes: In Jesu nomine surge et ambula (Act. III).

VERS. 8.-- Ne quid tollerent, etc. Tanta sit in Domino fiducia, ut nihil deesse certissime sciant, ne dum sibi provident temporalia, minus aliis provideant aeterna.

201A(BEDA.) Mattheus et Lucas memorant Dominum discipulis dixisse, ut nec virgam ferrent: Marcus ne quid tollerent nisi virgam. Sed illi realiter virgam accipiunt. Marcus per virgam potestatem accipiendi necessaria a subditis intelligit.

VERS. 9.-- Calceatos sandaliis. (ID.) Ut pes neque tectus sit neque nudus ad terram, id est, nec Evangelium occultetur, nec terrenis commodis innitatur.

Ne induerentur duabus tunicis. (ID.) Quod est ut simpliciter ambulent, non dupliciter.

VERS. 11.-- Excutite pulverem de pedibus. (ID.) Pulvis excutitur de pedibus in testimonium laboris sui, quod ingressi sunt civitatem, quod praedicatio usque ad illos pervenerit. Vel quod nihil ab eis acceperint 201Bad victum necessarium qui Evangelium spreverunt.

VERS. 13.-- Et ungebant oleo. (ID.) Jacobus dicit: Infirmatur quis in vobis? inducat presbyteros Ecclesiae, et orent super eum ungentes eum oleo (Jac. V), etc. Unde patet ab apostolis hunc morem esse traditum ut energumeni et alii aegroti ungantur oleo a pontifice consecrato.

VERS. 14.-- Et audivit rex. (ID.) Lucas (Luc. IX): Audivit Herodes tetrarcha omnia quae fiebant, etc. Unde intelligendum, aut confirmatum eum ex aliorum verbis credendo dixisse, aut adhuc haesitando, etc., usque ad non resurrexisse, sed sublevatum esse furtim credere maluerunt.

VERS. 17.-- Philippi fratris sui. (BEDA.) Vetus 201Chistoria narrat Philippum majoris Herodis fuisse filium, et fratrem Herodis sub quo passus est Dominus, et duxisse Herodiadem, filiam Arethae, et postea Aretham ortis simultatibus filiam suam tulisse, et Herodi inimico Philippi in dolorem ipsius nuptiis copulasse. Egesippus vel Josephus tamen longe aliter.

VERS. 18.-- Non licet tibi habere, etc. (ID.) Qui venerat in spiritu et virtute Eliae, Herodem arguit et Herodiadem, sicut, etc., usque ad homo enim ad laborem nascitur, et sancti per mortem transeunt ad requiem.

VERS. 23.-- Et juravit. (BEDA.) Non excusatur a perjurio per juramentum. Ideo enim forte juravit, ut occasionem inveniret occidendi. Et si illa patris aut 201Dmatris interitum postulasset, non utique concessisset Herodes.

VERS. 26.-- Et contristatus, etc. (ID.) Consueto more Scripturae contristatus, non re, sed multorum opinione. Sicut et ipse Joseph, etc., usque ad ut sub occasione pietatis impius fieret.

VERS. 27.-- Caput ejus in disco. (ID.) Caput legis quod est Christus, a corpore abscinditur, etc., usque ad Christus autem, qui est lux mundi, cum incipiunt crescere dies.

VERS. 29.-- Et tulerunt. (ID.) Josephus narrat Joannem vinctum, in castellum Macheronta adductum ibique truncatum. Ecclesiastica narrat historia, in Sebaste urbe Palaestinae, quae dicta est quondam Samaria. 202ATempore vero Juliani, etc., usque ad expurgato a sordibus templo Serapis ossa eadem ibi posuit, et basilicam in honore sancti Joannis consecravit.

VERS. 30.-- Et convenientes. (HIER.) Ad locum unde exeunt flumina redeunt. Deo gratias semper referunt super his quae acceperunt.

Omnia. Non solum quae ipsi egerant et docuerant apostoli Domino renuntiant, sed etiam quae Joannes eis docentibus passus sit. Vel sui vel ejusdem Joannis discipuli, sicut Matthaeus scribit.

VERS. 31.-- Venite seorsum. (BEDA.) Non solum requisitionis causa, sed mystice significat quod, relicta Judaea, quae, etc., usque ad venit seorsum, ducit quos eligit, ut inter male viventes mala non attendant, 202But Loth in Sodomis, et Job in terra Hus, et Abdias in domo Achab.

Requiescite. Ut aves in ramis sinapis. Pusilla hic requies sanctis, longus labor: sed post dicitur illis ut requiescant a laboribus suis.

Erant enim, etc. (HIER.) In arca Noe animalia quae intus erant, foras mittebantur, et quae foris erant, intus erumpebant. Sic in Ecclesia Judas, etc., usque ad tunc cessabit ventus et procella, Jesu sedente et regnante in navi, quae est universalis Ecclesia.

VERS. 32.-- Abierunt in deserto. (BEDA.) Petendo solitudinem fidem turbarum an se sequatur explorat, et exploratam digna mercede remunerat. Turbae autem non jumentis, non vehiculis, sed proprio labore 202Cpedum, iter deserti arripiunt, et salutis desiderium ostendunt. Rursus ipse excipiendo fatigatos, docendo inscios, sanando aegrotos, recreando jejunos, quantum devotione credentium delectetur insinuat.

VERS. 33.-- Et pedestres. Nota quia non in aliam maris ripam sine Jordanis navigio pervenerunt. Sed transito aliquo freto vel stagno, proximos ejusdem regionis locos adierunt, quo etiam pedestres indigenae pervenire potuerunt.

VERS. 34.-- Et misertus est. (BEDA.) Quomodo misertus sit, Matthaeus plenius exponit hoc modo: Et misertus est eis, et curavit languores eorum. Hoc est enim pauperum et non habentium pastorem veraciter misereri, et viam veritatis aperire, et languidos 202Dcurare et jejunos reficiendo ad laudem supernae largitatis animare; quae sequentia eum fecisse declarant.

VERS. 35.-- Et cum jam hora. Die declinata Salvator turbas reficit, quia vel fine saeculi propinquante, vel cum sol justitiae pro nobis occubuit, a diutina spiritalis inediae sumus liberati fame.

(BEDA.) Christum deserta gentium petentem multae fidelium catervae relictis moenibus priscae conversationis, neglectoque variorum dogmatum praesidio, sequuntur, et cum primum notus in Judaea Deus, tunc exaltatus est super coelos et super omnem terram gloria ejus.

VERS. 37.-- Date illis. Provocat apostolos ad fractionem 203Apanis, ut illis se non habere testantibus, magnitudino signi notesceret. Et insinuans, quia quotidie per eos jejuna corda sunt pascenda, cum eorum exemplis vel verbis ad amanda coelestia suscitamur. Notandum, quod hoc panum miraculum Joannes scripturus praemisit, quod proximum esset Pascha. Matthaeus et Marcus hoc, interfecto Joanne, continuo esse factum dicunt: unde colligitur Joannem imminente Pascha fuisse decollatum, et alio sequente Pascha cruxifixum Dominum.

VERS. 38.-- Quinque. (BEDA.) Quinque panes, quinque Mosaicae legis libri, quibus divinae aeternitatis cognitio, mundi creatio, cursus labentis saeculi, et vera Deo serviendi religio, etc., usque ad ad ultimum vero magno munere dat electis ut edant et 203Bbibant super mensam suam in regno suo.

VERS. 39.-- Secundum contubernia. (ID.) Diversi discubitus, distincti ordines ecclesiarum qui unam catholicam faciunt.

VERS. 40.-- Per centenos et quinquagenos. Per quinquaginta jubilaei requies. Quinquagenarius autem bis ductus centum facit; et ideo utriusque quietis perfectionem significat, scilicet, corporis et mentis. Qui enim quiescit ab actibus pravis, requiem habet corporis. Qui autem a cogitationibus etiam perversis, requiem habet mentis.

Bene autem super viride fenum discumbentes, Dei pascuntur alimentis, qui per studium abstinentiae, calcatis illecebris carnis, audiendis implendisque verbis Dei operam impendunt.

203CIdeo alii quinquageni, alii centeni, quia requiescitur prius a malo opere, ut post sit etiam requies in mente.

VERS. 41.-- Et acceptis. Non nova cibaria creat quia incarnatus, non alia quam quae scripta erant praedicat: sed legem et prophetas mysteriis gravida esse demonstrat.

Fregit. Et ante turbas ponenda distribuit discipulis suis, quia sacramenta sanctis doctoribus, qui haec toto orbe praedicent, patefecit.

VERS. 43.-- Et sustulerunt, etc. (BEDA.) Secretiora mysteria quae rudes capere nequeunt, perfectiores diligenter inquirunt. Nam per cophinos, duodecim apostoli: et per apostolos sequentes doctores figurantur, foris despecti, intus salutaris cibi reliquiis 203Dad alenda humilium corda cumulati. In cophinis enim servilia geruntur opera: sed ille eos panis fragmentorum implevit, qui infirma mundi, ut frangat vel confundat, elegit.

VERS. 44.-- Quinque millia. (ID.) Propter quinque sensus corporis. Hi Dominum secuti, significant eos qui in saeculari adhuc habitu positi, exterioribus quae possident bene uti norunt. Qui recte quinque panibus aluntur: quia necesse est ut tales legalibus adhuc praeceptis instruantur. Qui autem mundo omnino renuntiant, et quatuor sunt millia et septem panibus refecti, hoc est evangelica perfectione sublimes, et spirituali gratia intus eruditi.

VERS. 45.-- Et statim coegit. Exemplum dat nobis 204Aut in omnibus bonis quae agimus humani favoris retributionem vitemus: ne nos operatio virtutum ad concupiscentiam flectat temporalium. Quod ipse Dominus nobis insinuans, cum hi qui virtutes ejus admirabantur, regem eum facere vellent, fugit in montem orare. Crucifixoribus promptus occurrit, ut scilicet parati simus ad adversa toleranda, cauti ad blandimenta si arriserint, et ne nos, si affluant, emolliendo decipiant, crebris a Domino precibus imploremus.

Ad Bethsaidam. (BEDA.) Bethsaida civitas est in Galilaea Andreae et Petri et Philippi prope stagnum Genesareth. Sed Marcus dicit, etc., usque ad quae ambae sunt civitates in Galilaea juxta stagnum Genesareth: quod etiam Tiberiadis a Tiberiade civitate 204Bsic vocatur.

VERS. 46.-- Abiit in montem. (ID.) Bene orat qui Deum orando quaerit, a terrenis ad superiora progrediens, verticem curiae sublimioris ascendit. Qui de divitiis, etc., usque ad omnia quae vult potest, quia advocatus et judex noster est; alterum pietatis officium, alterum potestatis insigne.

VERS. 47.-- Et ipse solus, etc. Qui aliquando omnino in periculis Ecclesiam deseruisse videtur; unde: Ut quid, Domine, recessisti longe (Psal. X) etc. Sed quia differt auxilium, non aufert subsidium.

VERS. 48.-- Et circa quartam. (BEDA.) Stationes et vigiliae militares, horarum spatio dividuntur: patet ergo eos, etc., usque ad Lucifer namque tres horas noctis, id est, totam vigiliam matutinam illuminare 204Cdicitur.

Et volebat praeterire eos. (ID.) Ut ad horam, scilicet, conturbati: sed continuo liberati, plus liberationis suae miraculum stuperent, et ereptori suo, etc., usque ad Cum ambulaveris in igne, non combureris, et flamma non ardebit in te;

VERS. 49.-- Putaverunt. (BEDA.) Adhuc haeretici putant phantasma esse, nec veram carnem de virgine assumpsisse. Theodorus enim, etc., usque ad et qualiter non infusis pedibus corporale pondus habentibus et materiale onus, deambulabat in humidam et instabilem substantiam.

VERS. 50.-- Nolite timere. Prima subventio est pellere timorem; secunda, tempestatem praesentiae 204Dsuae virtute compescere. Nec mirum si Domino ascendente in navim cessat ventus: quia in quocunque corde per gratiam sui amoris adest, mox universa vitiorum et adversantis mundi et malignorum spirituum bella quiescunt.

VERS. 52.-- Non enim intellexerunt. (BEDA.) Miraculo panum, quod esset rerum conditor ostendit. Ambulando super undas, quod totum corpus habebat liberum ab omni gravedine peccati. Placando ventos et undas, quod elementis dominaretur ostendit. Sed carnales adhuc discipuli magnitudine quidem virtutum stupebant, sed divinae majestatis veritatem non cognoscebant.

VERS. 54.-- Cognoverunt. Rumore, non facie vel 205Amagnitudine signorum: etiam vultu plurimis notus erat.

VERS. 56.-- Fimbriam vestimenti. (BEDA.) Minimum mandatum quod qui transgressus fuerit, minimus vocabitur in regno coelorum: vel assumptionem carnis per quam venimus ad Verbum Dei.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 2.-- Whence hath this man, etc. Wonderful blindness. In his deeds and in his words they are able to know Christ, for the mere knowledge of his stock they despise him.

VERS. 3.-- A craftsman. (BEDA.) He who works with fire and with spirit; whence: He shall baptize you in fire and in spirit (Joan. I) [Vulg. Matt. III, 11 and Luc. III, 16 read ipse vos baptizabit in Spiritu Sancto, et igni, "in the Holy Ghost and fire", in the reverse order; Joan. I, 33 has only the Holy Ghost]. He is the craftsman who crafted the dawn and the sun.

VERS. 4.-- A prophet is not without honour. It is well-nigh natural that fellow-citizens should envy fellow-citizens, because they remember not his works but the childhood 200Cwhich they saw.

(ISID.) Meanness often attends a man's origin. As, Who is the son of Isai (III Reg. XII). But: The Lord beholdeth the lowly, and the high he knoweth afar off (Psal. CXXXVII).

VERS. 5.-- And he could not, etc. Lest by doing many mighty works he should condemn the worse his unbelieving fellow-citizens. Or because he is despised in his own country, he did few mighty works and signs there, lest they should be wholly excusable. Greater signs he does daily among the Gentiles, not so much in the healing of bodies as of souls.

VERS. 6.-- And he wondered. (BEDA.) Not as at something unforeseen, but he shows it as a thing to be wondered at, because they believe neither the prophets, nor him whom they behold present.

VERS. 7.-- And he called. (HIER.) The twelve 200Dapostles are sent, and there is given them power, precepts of teaching, that word and deed together may accompany them: and that mighty works may be mingled with invisible promises, and so, when they anoint the sick with oil, they strengthen the weakness of faith by a mighty work.

And he gave them, etc. The kind Lord and master does not grudge his own power to his disciples and servants. But he acts by his own power, they by their own weakness, if they do anything, and they confess the Lord's might, saying: In the name of Jesus arise and walk (Act. III).

VERS. 8.-- That they should take nothing, etc. Let their confidence in the Lord be so great that they may know most certainly that nothing is wanting, lest, while they provide temporal things for themselves, they provide eternal things the less for others.

201A(BEDA.) Matthew and Luke record that the Lord said to his disciples that they should not carry even a staff: Mark, that they should take nothing save a staff. But they take the staff really. Mark understands by the staff the power of receiving what is necessary from those subject to them.

VERS. 9.-- Shod with sandals. (ID.) That the foot be neither covered nor bare to the ground, that is, that neither the Gospel be hidden, nor lean upon earthly advantages.

That they should not put on two coats. (ID.) Which is, that they should walk in singleness, not in doubleness.

VERS. 11.-- Shake off the dust from your feet. (ID.) The dust is shaken off from the feet in testimony of their labour, that they have entered the city, that the preaching has reached even unto them. Or that they have received nothing from them 201Btoward the food that was needful, who despised the Gospel.

VERS. 13.-- And they anointed with oil. (ID.) James says: Is any man sick among you? let him bring in the priests of the Church, and let them pray over him, anointing him with oil (Jac. V), etc. Whence it is plain that this custom was handed down from the apostles, that the possessed and other sick persons should be anointed with oil consecrated by a bishop.

VERS. 14.-- And the king heard. (ID.) Luke (Luc. IX): Herod the tetrarch heard all things that were done, etc. Whence it is to be understood that either, being confirmed by the words of others, he said it in belief, or that he was still hesitating, etc., as far as they chose rather to believe that he had not risen again, but had been carried off by stealth.

VERS. 17.-- Of Philip his brother. (BEDA.) An ancient 201Chistory relates that Philip was the son of the elder Herod, and the brother of that Herod under whom the Lord suffered, and that he married Herodias the daughter of Aretas, and that afterwards Aretas, quarrels having arisen, took away his own daughter, and joined her in marriage to Herod, the enemy of Philip, to Philip's own grief. Egesippus, however, or Josephus, far otherwise.

VERS. 18.-- It is not lawful for thee to have, etc. (ID.) He who had come in the spirit and power of Elias reproves Herod and Herodias, as, etc., as far as for man is born to labour, and the saints pass through death to rest.

VERS. 23.-- And he swore. (BEDA.) He is not excused from perjury by his oath. For perhaps he swore for this reason, that he might find an occasion of killing. And if she had asked the death of her father or 201Dof her mother, Herod assuredly would not have granted it.

VERS. 26.-- And being made sorrowful, etc. (ID.) Made sorrowful after the accustomed manner of Scripture, not in fact, but in the opinion of many. As Joseph himself also, etc., as far as that under the occasion of piety he might become impious.

VERS. 27.-- His head in a dish. (ID.) The head of the law, which is Christ, is cut off from the body, etc., as far as but Christ, who is the light of the world, when the days begin to grow.

VERS. 29.-- And they took. (ID.) Josephus relates that John was bound, brought into the castle of Macherus and there beheaded. The Ecclesiastical history relates that it was in Sebaste, a city of Palestine, which was once called Samaria. 202ABut in the time of Julian, etc., as far as the temple of Serapis being purged of its filth, he laid those same bones there, and consecrated a basilica in honour of Saint John.

VERS. 30.-- And gathering together. (HIER.) To the place whence the rivers go out they return. They always render thanks to God for those things which they have received.

All things. The apostles report to the Lord not only what they themselves had done and taught, but also what John suffered while they were teaching. Either his own disciples, or those of the same John, as Matthew writes.

VERS. 31.-- Come apart. (BEDA.) Not only for the sake of a seeking again, but mystically it signifies that, Judaea being left, which, etc., as far as he comes apart, he leads those whom he chooses, that among men living ill they may not attend to evil things, 202Bas Lot in Sodom, and Job in the land of Hus, and Abdias in the house of Achab.

Rest ye. As the birds in the branches of the mustard. Little is the rest here for the saints, long the labour: but afterwards it is said to them that they should rest from their labours.

For there were, etc. (HIER.) In the ark of Noe the animals that were within were sent forth, and those that were without burst in. So in the Church Judas, etc., as far as then shall the wind and the storm cease, Jesus sitting and reigning in the ship, which is the universal Church.

VERS. 32.-- They went away in the desert. (BEDA.) By seeking solitude he tries the faith of the crowds, whether it follows him, and having tried it he rewards it with a worthy recompense. But the crowds, not with beasts of burden, not with carriages, but by the very labour 202Cof their feet, take up the journey of the desert, and show their desire of salvation. Again, by receiving the weary, by teaching the ignorant, by healing the sick, by refreshing the fasting, he intimates how much he is delighted by the devotion of them that believe.

VERS. 33.-- And on foot. Note that they did not come to the other bank of the sea without a sailing of the Jordan. But, some strait or lake being passed over, they went to the nearest places of the same region, whither even the natives on foot were able to come.

VERS. 34.-- And he had compassion. (BEDA.) How he had compassion Matthew sets forth more fully in this manner: And he had compassion on them, and healed their infirmities. [Vulg. Matt. XIV, 14 reads curavit languidos eorum, "healed their sick"; the gloss immediately following expounds the healing of the sick, not of infirmities] For this is truly to have compassion on the poor and on them that have no shepherd, and to open the way of truth, and the languishing 202Dto heal, and by refreshing the fasting to hearten them to the praise of the heavenly bounty; and the things that follow declare that he did this.

VERS. 35.-- And when the hour was now. The day being far spent, the Saviour refreshes the crowds, because either as the end of the age draws near, or when the sun of justice set for us, we were delivered from the long hunger of spiritual want.

(BEDA.) Christ, as he seeks the desert places of the Gentiles, many bands of the faithful follow, the walls of their former manner of life being left and the safeguard of divers doctrines neglected, and whereas God was first known in Judaea, then was he exalted above the heavens, and his glory above all the earth.

VERS. 37.-- Give ye them. He provokes the apostles to the breaking 203Aof bread, that, when they testified that they had none, the greatness magnitudino of the sign might become known. And intimating that daily through them fasting hearts are to be fed, when by their examples or by their words we are stirred up to love heavenly things. It is to be noted that John, about to write this miracle of the loaves, set before it that the Pasch was at hand. Matthew and Mark say that this was done immediately when John had been killed: whence it is gathered that John was beheaded when the Pasch was imminent, and that the Lord was crucified at the next following Pasch.

VERS. 38.-- Five. (BEDA.) The five loaves, the five books of the Mosaic law, in which is the knowledge of the divine eternity, the creation of the world, the course of the passing age, and the true religion of serving God, etc., as far as but at the last with a great gift he grants to the elect that they may eat and 203Bdrink at his table in his kingdom.

VERS. 39.-- By companies. (ID.) The diverse reclinings, the distinct orders of the churches which make one catholic Church.

VERS. 40.-- By hundreds and by fifties. By fifty, the rest of the jubilee. But the number fifty taken twice makes a hundred; and therefore it signifies the perfection of both rests, namely, of the body and of the mind. For he who rests from depraved acts has the rest of the body. But he who rests from perverse thoughts also has the rest of the mind.

And well do they that recline upon the green grass feed upon the nourishments of God, who by the pursuit of abstinence, the allurements of the flesh being trodden down, bestow their pains upon hearing and fulfilling the words of God.

203CTherefore some are by fifties, others by hundreds, because rest is first had from evil work, that afterwards there may be rest in the mind also.

VERS. 41.-- And having taken. He creates no new viands, because, being incarnate, he preaches no other things than those which had been written: but he shows that the law and the prophets are big with mysteries.

He broke. And he distributed to his disciples the things to be set before the crowds, because he laid open the sacraments to the holy teachers, who should preach them throughout the whole world.

VERS. 43.-- And they took up, etc. (BEDA.) The more secret mysteries, which the untaught cannot grasp, the more perfect diligently search out. For by the baskets, the twelve apostles: and by the apostles the teachers that follow are figured, despised without, within heaped up with the leavings of the saving food 203Dfor the feeding of humble hearts. For in baskets servile works are carried: but he filled them with the bread of the fragments, who chose the weak things of the world, that he might break or confound.

VERS. 44.-- Five thousand. (ID.) Because of the five senses of the body. These, having followed the Lord, signify those who, being still set in a secular habit, know how to use well the outward things which they possess. Who are rightly fed with five loaves: because it is needful that such be still instructed by the precepts of the law. But they who wholly renounce the world are four thousand and are refreshed with seven loaves, that is, lofty in evangelical perfection, and inwardly taught by spiritual grace.

VERS. 45.-- And immediately he constrained. He gives us an example 204Athat in all the good things which we do we should shun the recompense of human favour: lest the working of virtues bend us to the desire of temporal things. And the Lord himself, intimating this to us, when they who admired his mighty works wished to make him king, fled into the mountain to pray. He readily met his crucifiers, namely, that we may be ready to bear adversities, wary toward flatteries if they should smile upon us, and that, lest they deceive us by softening us if they abound, we should implore the Lord with frequent prayers.

To Bethsaida. (BEDA.) Bethsaida is a city in Galilee, of Andrew and of Peter and of Philip, near the lake of Genesareth. But Mark says, etc., as far as both of which are cities in Galilee beside the lake of Genesareth: which is also called of Tiberias, from the city of Tiberias 204Bbeing so named.

VERS. 46.-- He went into the mountain. (ID.) He prays well who seeks God by praying, and, advancing from earthly things to the higher, ascends the summit of a loftier court. He who concerning riches, etc., as far as he can do all that he wills, because he is our advocate and our judge; the one the office of loving-kindness, the other the mark of power.

VERS. 47.-- And he alone, etc. Who sometimes seems to have forsaken the Church altogether in her perils; whence: Why, O Lord, hast thou retired afar off (Psal. X) etc. But because he defers his help, he does not take away his succour.

VERS. 48.-- And about the fourth. (BEDA.) The military stations and watches are divided by a space of hours: it is plain therefore that they, etc., as far as for the morning star is 204Csaid to give light for three hours of the night, that is, for the whole morning watch.

And he would have passed by them. (ID.) That for an hour, namely, being troubled: but straightway set free, they might the more marvel at the miracle of their deliverance, and to their deliverer, etc., as far as When thou shalt walk in the fire, thou shalt not be burnt, and the flame shall not burn in thee;

VERS. 49.-- They thought. (BEDA.) Even yet heretics think that he is a phantasm, and that he did not take true flesh of the Virgin. For Theodorus, etc., as far as and how, with feet not sinking in, having a bodily weight and a material burden, he walked upon the moist and unstable substance.

VERS. 50.-- Fear ye not. The first relief is to drive away fear; the second, to quell the tempest by the power of his own 204Dpresence. Nor is it wonderful that, when the Lord goes up into the ship, the wind ceases: because in whatever heart he is present through the grace of his love, straightway all the wars of the vices and of the opposing world and of malignant spirits are stilled.

VERS. 52.-- For they understood not. (BEDA.) By the miracle of the loaves he showed that he was the founder of things. By walking upon the waves, that he had his whole body free from every heaviness of sin. By calming the winds and the waves he showed that he had dominion over the elements. But the disciples, being yet carnal, were astonished indeed at the greatness of the mighty works, but did not know the truth of the divine majesty.

VERS. 54.-- They knew him. By report, not by his face nor by 205Athe greatness of his signs: he was also known by his countenance to very many.

VERS. 56.-- The hem of his garment. (BEDA.) The least commandment, which whosoever shall have transgressed shall be called least in the kingdom of heaven: or the assumption of flesh through which we come to the Word of God.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Et conveniunt. (ID.) Patet quam vera sit confessio Domini ad Patrem qua dicitur: Quia abscondisti haec a sapientibus et prudentibus, et revelasti ea parvulis (Matth. XI). Indocti veniunt, infirmos adducunt, ut vel fimbriam ejus contingant, et ideo cupita salute patiuntur. Pharisaei vero et Scribae, qui doctores populi esse debebant, non ad verbum 205Baudiendum, non quasi ad medicum, sed ad quaestionum pugnas concurrunt.

VERS. 2.-- Communibus manibus. (HIERON.) Immundis, vel communibus manibus communionem gentium significat. Munditia Pharisaeorum sterilis est, communio apostolorum non tota extendit palmites suos usque ad mare. Non lotis. De non lotis corporis manibus vituperant, cum in eorum operibus nihil immunditiae inveniatur. Ipsi aqua exterius loti, conscientiae livore intus sunt polluti.

VERS. 3.-- Pharisaei. (BEDA.) Superstitiosa hominum traditio crebrius lavari, ob manducandum et hujusmodi. Sed necessaria doctrina veritatis eos jubet, qui panem vitae qui de coelo descendit manducare desiderant, crebro eleemosynarum, aliorumque 205Cjustitiae fructuum lamento sua opera purgare.

VERS. 4.-- Baptismata. Frustra vasorum baptismata servant, qui cordium suorum et corporum sordes negligunt. Non enim hoc de materialibus manibus dictum est, sed pro mentium emundatione, et operum castigatione, et animarum sanctimonia et salute.

VERS. 5.-- Quare discipuli tui, etc. Mira stultitia! Filium Dei arguunt, quia non servat praecepta hominum. Manus autem, id est, opera non corporis sed animae lavandae sunt, ut fiat in illis Verbum Dei.

VERS. 6.-- Bene prophetavit. (HIERON.) Latratum Pharisaeorum reprimit furca rationis, id est, Moysi et Isaiae increpatione, ut et nos haereticos verbis 205DScripturae vincere possimus.

VERS. 9.-- Bene irritum. Falsam calumniam vera ratione confutat, quasi, Vos propter traditionem hominum praecepta Dei contemnitis et negligitis, quare ergo meos discipulos arguitis, qui jussa seniorum parvipendunt, ut custodiant mandata Dei?

VERS. 11.-- Vos autem dicitis. (BEDA.) Consulens Dominus imbecillitati, vel aetatum vel penuriae parentum, praecepit, etc., usque ad qui verus Pater est, oblatio Domini praeponatur. Tibi profuerit. (ISID.) In tuos usus consumitur, quod sacrilegium est. Vel interrogative. Quasi dicat: Proderit tibi? Non debet: Hoc metuentes patres afficiebantur inedia.

VERS. 15.-- Nihil est extra, etc. (BEDA.) Nec 206Aidolothytum in quantum cibus est, in quantum Dei creatura est, sed daemoniorum invocatio hoc facit immundum. Unde: Non potestis bibere calicem Domini et calicem daemoniorum (I Cor. X).

Judaei se partem Dei jactant, communes cibos vocant, quibus omnes homines utuntur, ut ostrea, lepores, et hujusmodi animalia, quae ungulam non findunt, nec ruminant, nec squamosa in piscibus sunt.

VERS. 18.-- Et vos imprudentes. Corripiuntur, quia quae per se patent, mystica putant. Ex quo advertimus vitiosum esse auditorem, qui obscura manifeste, aut manifeste dicta obscure vult intelligere.

VERS. 19.-- Et in secessum. (BEDA.) Hinc calumniantur quidam haeretici, quod Dominus physicae 206Bignarus putet omnes cibos, etc., usque ad per occultos meatus (quos Graeci poros vocant) ad inferiora dilabitur et in secessum vadit.

(AUG.) Quaedam sic accedunt ut etiam mutent et mutentur, sicut et ipse cibus amittens speciem suam, in corpus nostrum vertitur, et nos refecti in robur mutamur.

VERS. 21.-- De corde hominum. (BEDA.) Animae locus principalis non juxta Platonem in cerebro, sed juxta Christum in corde est. Arguuntur etiam, etc., usque ad ut si pulchram mulierem nos crebro viderit inspicere, intelligit nos amare.

VERS. 24.-- Et inde surgens. Relictis Scribis et Pharisaeis calumniatoribus, in partes Tyri et Sidonis secessit, ut Tyrios Sidoniosque curaret. Neminem. 206C(BEDA.) Quamvis latere non potuerit, non tamen factum est quod voluit.
