Sed exemplum, etc., usque ad ut filiam Syrophoenissae a daemonio liberaret et per fidem gentilis feminae Scribarum et Pharisaeorum perfidiam argueret.

VERS. 25.-- Mulier enim. Marcus dicit Dominum fuisse in domo, cum venit ad eum mulier. Matthaeus dicit quod clamat post nos (Matth. XV). Per quod innuit quod post ambulantem preces emiserit. Venit ergo ad eum mulier in domum, sed quia Matthaeus ait: Non respondit ei verbum, dedit intelligere quod ambo tacuerunt, et cum silentio ingressi sunt. Et ita caetera contexuntur, quae in nullo dissentiunt.

(HIER.) Mulier est mater nostra Romana Ecclesiae. Nata, daemoniaca occidentalis barbaries, cujus 206Dfides fecit de cane ovem. Micas intellectus quaerit, non panem infractum litterae cupit.

(BEDA.) Mulier gentilis, sed cum fide ad Dominum veniens Ecclesiam significat de gentibus collectam, quae pro filia, etc., usque ad qui priscas suae perfidiae mansiones relinquunt, atque in domum Dei, id est Ecclesiam, pia se devotione transferunt.

VERS. 27.-- Sine prius saturari filios. Quasi dicat: Futurum est ut et vos gentes salutem consequamini: sed prius oportet Judaeos, qui antiqua electione filii Dei nominantur, pane coelesti refici, et sic tandem gentibus vitae pabula ministrari. Filiorum. (BEDA.) Mira conversio: nam Israel quondam filius, nos canes. Pro diversitate fidei ordo nominum mutatur. 207ADe illis dicitur: Circumdederunt me canes multi (Psal. XXI). De nobis: Alias oves habeo, quae non sunt ex hoc ovili (Joan. X).

VERS. 28.-- De micis. Non integros cibos digna sum recipere, reliquiis catellorum contenta sum, ut sic veniam ad panem integrum. (BEDA.) De micis, etc. Mensa, scriptura, unde: Parasti in conspectu meo mensam, etc. (Psal. XXII). Micae puerorum, etc., usque ad ut ad speranda superna quae a Domino promissa sunt praemia in coelo, merito se humilitatis erigant.

VERS. 29.-- Propter hunc sermonem. (BEDA.) Hic datur exemplum catechizandi et baptizandi infantes, quia per fidem et confessionem parentum in baptismo liberantur a daemonio parvuli, qui necdum per se sapere vel aliquid agere boni possunt aut mali.

207BVERS. 31.-- Tyri. (HIERON.) Tyrus angustia significans Judaeam, cui Dominus: Coangustatum est stratum et pallium breve, utrumque operire non potest, ut transferat se ad alias gentes (Isai. XVIII). Sidonem. (HIERON.) Sidon, venatio, fera indomita, nostra regio vel natio est. Decapoleos. (BEDA.) Regio decem urbium trans Jordanem ad Orientem. Quod autem dicitur: Venit ad mare Galilaeae inter medios fines Decapoleos, non ipsos eum intrasse significat, neque enim mare transnavigasse dicitur, sed usque ad mare venisse, atque ad locum qui medios fines Decapoleos longe trans mare positos respiciebat.

(HIERON.) Salvator ad salvandas gentes ab apostolis deducitur, et docet quod adolescenti interroganti respondit: Nosti mandata, haec fac et vives (Luc. XVIII).

207CVERS. 32.-- Surdum et mutum. (BEDA.) Qui scilicet, nec aures verbis Dei audiendis, nec os pro loquendis aperit. Quales necesse est ut hi qui audire et loqui verbum Dei longo usu didicerunt, Domino sanandos offerant, ut eos quos humana fragilitas nequit, gratiae dextera salvet.

(HIERON.) Genus humanum tanquam unus homo varia peste absumptus in protoplasto, caecatur dum videt, surdus fit dum audit, dum odorat emungitur, obmutescit dum loquitur, mancus fit dum manus erigit, incurvatur dum erigitur, hydropicus fit dum concupiscit, claudus dum progreditur, lepra suffunditur dum mundatur, daemone impletur dum divinitatem appetit, moritur morte dum audacter excusat. 207DEt deprecabantur. Incarnationem Domini prophetae et patriarchae cupiebant.

VERS. 33.-- Et apprehendens eum, etc. Seorsum a turbulentis cogitationibus et actibus inordinatis sermonibusque incompositis educitur qui sanari meretur. Digiti qui in aures mittuntur, verba Spiritus sancti, de quo dicitur: Digitus Dei est hic (Exod. VIII). Et: Opera digitorum tuorum sunt coeli (Psal. VIII). Tetigit linguam, etc. Spuma de carne Domini, divina sapientia, quae solvit ignorantiam humani generis, ut dicat: Credo in Deum Patrem omnipotentem, etc. Seorsum. (BEDA.) Prima salutis via est de turba educi. De turba educit Dominus infirmum, cum mentem peccatis, etc., usque ad exspuens ergo 208Alinguam muti ut loqui valeat, tangit, cum ora diu bruta ad verba sapientiae proferenda contactu suae pietatis informat.

VERS. 34.-- Et suscipiens, etc. (HIERON.) Ingemuit. Gemere nos docuit, et in coelum thesaurum nostri cordis erigere, quod per compunctionem cordis a frivola carnis laetitia purgatur, ut dicitur: Rugiebam a gemitu cordis mei; Domine, ante te omne desiderium meum, etc. Et ait illi: Ephpheta, quod est Adaperire. Corde creditur ad justitiam (Rom. X), etc. Et statim apertae, etc. Aures apertae sunt ad hymnos et cantica et psalmos. Solvitur lingua, ut eructet verbum bonum, quod non possunt nec minae nec verba cohibere vel prohibere. Unde Paulus: Ego vinctus sum, sed verbum Dei non est alligatum in me (I Tim. II).

208BVERS. 36.-- Et praecepit illis ne cui dicerent. Non in virtutibus gloriandum esse docuit, sed in cruce et in humiliatione. Quanto autem eis, etc. Civitas in monte posita undique circumspecta abscondi non potest (Matth. XV): humilitas semper praecedit gloriam.

Adaperire. Ad aures proprie. Aures enim ad audiendum aperiendae, lingua ut loqui possit erat solvenda. Unde sequitur: Statim apertae, etc.

VERS. 35.-- Et loquebatur recte. (BEDA.) Ille solus recte loquitur vel Dominum confitendo, vel aliis praedicando, cujus auditum ut coelestibus possit auscultare et obsecundare mandatis divina gratia referat. Cujus linguam Dominus tactu sapientiae, quae ipse est, ad loquendum instruit. Hic talis dicere potest: 208CDomine, labia mea aperies (Psal. V), etc.

VERS. 36.-- Quanto autem eis praecipiebat, etc. Sciebat ille qui omnia novit antequam fiant, quod magis praedicarent, et hoc praecipiendo pigris voluit ostendere, quanto studiosius quantoque frequentius [ferventius] praedicare debeant, quibus jubet ut praedicarent: quando illi qui prohibentur tacere non poterant.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- And they come together. (ID.) It is plain how true is that confession of the Lord to the Father in which it is said: Because thou hast hidden these things from the wise and prudent, and hast revealed them to little ones (Matth. XI). The unlearned come, they bring the sick, that they may touch even his hem, and therefore, having desired health, they suffer. But the Pharisees and the Scribes, who ought to have been the teachers of the people, run together not to 205Bhear the word, not as to a physician, but to the battles of questionings.

VERS. 2.-- With common hands. (HIERON.) By unclean, or common, hands he signifies the communion of the Gentiles. The cleanness of the Pharisees is barren; the communion of the apostles does not wholly stretch out its branches as far as the sea. Not washed. They find fault over the unwashed hands of the body, though in their works nothing of uncleanness is found. They themselves, washed outwardly with water, are inwardly polluted with the rancour of conscience.

VERS. 3.-- The Pharisees. (BEDA.) The superstitious tradition of men, to wash the oftener, for the sake of eating and the like. But the necessary teaching of truth bids those who desire to eat the bread of life which came down from heaven to cleanse their works by the frequent lament of alms and of the other 205Cfruits of justice.

VERS. 4.-- Washings. In vain do they keep the washings of vessels, who neglect the filth of their own hearts and bodies. For this was not said of material hands, but for the cleansing of minds, and the chastisement of works, and the holiness and salvation of souls.

VERS. 5.-- Why do thy disciples, etc. Wondrous folly! They accuse the Son of God because he does not keep the precepts of men. But the hands, that is, the works not of the body but of the soul, are to be washed, that the Word of God may be wrought in them.

VERS. 6.-- Well did he prophesy. (HIERON.) The fork of reason represses the barking of the Pharisees, that is, by the rebuke of Moses and of Isaias, that we too may be able to overcome heretics by the words 205Dof Scripture.

VERS. 9.-- Well do you make void. He refutes the false calumny by true reasoning, as if, You, for the tradition of men, despise and neglect the precepts of God, why then do you accuse my disciples, who make little of the commands of the elders, that they may keep the commandments of God?

VERS. 11.-- But you say. (BEDA.) The Lord, taking counsel for the weakness whether of age or of the poverty of parents, commanded, etc., as far as to him who is the true Father let the oblation of the Lord be preferred. It shall profit thee. (ISID.) It is consumed for thy own uses, which is sacrilege. Or interrogatively. As if he said: Will it profit thee? It ought not: fearing this, parents were afflicted with hunger.

VERS. 15.-- There is nothing outside, etc. (BEDA.) Nor 206Ais it the meat offered to idols, in so far as it is food, in so far as it is a creature of God, but the invocation of demons, that makes this unclean. Whence: You cannot drink the cup of the Lord and the cup of demons (I Cor. X).

The Jews boast themselves the portion of God, and call those foods common which all men use, such as oysters, hares, and animals of this kind, which do not divide the hoof, nor chew the cud, nor, among fishes, are scaly.

VERS. 18.-- And you also are without understanding. They are rebuked because they think those things mystical which are plain of themselves. From which we perceive him to be a faulty hearer who wishes to understand obscure things plainly, or things plainly said obscurely.

VERS. 19.-- And into the draught. (BEDA.) Hence certain heretics cavil that the Lord, ignorant of natural 206Bphilosophy, supposes all foods, etc., as far as it slips away through hidden passages (which the Greeks call poros) to the lower parts, and goes into the draught.

(AUG.) Some things so come to us that they both change and are changed, just as food itself, losing its own appearance, is turned into our body, and we, refreshed, are changed into strength.

VERS. 21.-- From the heart of men. (BEDA.) The chief seat of the soul is not, according to Plato, in the brain, but, according to Christ, in the heart. They are also reproved, etc., as far as so that, if he has often seen us gaze upon a beautiful woman, he understands that we love her.

VERS. 24.-- And rising up from thence. Leaving the Scribes and Pharisees, those calumniators, he withdrew into the parts of Tyre and Sidon, that he might heal the Tyrians and the Sidonians. No man. 206C(BEDA.) Although he could not be hid, yet what he willed was not done. But an example, etc., as far as that he might free the daughter of the Syrophoenician woman from the demon, and by the faith of a Gentile woman convict the perfidy of the Scribes and the Pharisees.

VERS. 25.-- For a woman. Mark says that the Lord was in the house when the woman came to him. Matthew says that she crieth after us (Matth. XV). By which he hints that she poured out her prayers behind him as he walked. The woman therefore came to him into the house, but because Matthew says: He answered her not a word, he gave it to be understood that both were silent, and that they entered in silence. And so the rest is woven together, and in nothing do they disagree.

(HIER.) The woman is our Roman mother of the Church. The daughter is the demon-possessed barbarism of the West, whose 206Dfaith made a sheep out of a dog. She seeks the crumbs of understanding; she does not desire the unbroken bread of the letter.

(BEDA.) The woman, a Gentile, but coming to the Lord with faith, signifies the Church gathered out of the Gentiles, who for her daughter, etc., as far as who leave the ancient dwellings of their perfidy, and betake themselves with pious devotion into the house of God, that is, the Church.

VERS. 27.-- Suffer first the children to be filled. As if he said: It shall come to pass that you Gentiles also obtain salvation: but first the Jews, who by the ancient election are named the sons of God, must be refreshed with the heavenly bread, and so at last the food of life be ministered to the Gentiles. Of the children. (BEDA.) A wondrous turning about: for Israel was once the son, we the dogs. According to the diversity of faith the order of the names is changed. 207AOf them it is said: Many dogs have compassed me (Psal. XXI). Of us: Other sheep I have, that are not of this fold (Joan. X).

VERS. 28.-- Of the crumbs. I am not worthy to receive whole meats; I am content with the leavings of the whelps, that so I may come to the whole bread. (BEDA.) Of the crumbs, etc. The table is Scripture, whence: Thou hast prepared a table before me, etc. (Psal. XXII). The crumbs of the children, etc., as far as that by the merit of humility they may raise themselves up to hope for the things above, the rewards which have been promised by the Lord in heaven.

VERS. 29.-- For this saying. (BEDA.) Here is given an example of catechizing and baptizing infants, because by the faith and confession of their parents little ones are freed from the demon in baptism, who cannot yet of themselves have understanding, or do anything of good or of evil.

207BVERS. 31.-- Of Tyre. (HIERON.) Tyre, meaning straitness, signifies Judea, to which the Lord says: The bed is straitened and the covering short: the one cannot cover both, so that he may betake himself to other nations (Isai. XVIII). Sidon. (HIERON.) Sidon, a hunting, an untamed wild beast, is our own region or nation. Of Decapolis. (BEDA.) The region of ten cities beyond the Jordan toward the east. But where it is said: He came to the sea of Galilee, through the midst of the coasts of Decapolis, this does not signify that he entered them; for neither is he said to have sailed across the sea, but to have come as far as the sea, and to a place which looked toward the midst of the coasts of Decapolis, set far beyond the sea.

(HIERON.) The Saviour is led by the apostles to the saving of the Gentiles, and teaches what he answered to the young man who questioned him: Thou knowest the commandments; do these things, and thou shalt live (Luc. XVIII).

207CVERS. 32.-- One deaf and dumb. (BEDA.) One, namely, who opens neither his ears to hear the words of God, nor his mouth to speak them. Such men they who have learned by long use to hear and to speak the word of God must needs offer to the Lord to be healed, that those whom human frailty cannot [heal], the right hand of grace may save.

(HIERON.) The human race, as one man consumed by divers plagues in the first-formed man, is blinded while it sees, is made deaf while it hears, its nose is wiped while it smells, it is struck dumb while it speaks, it is made maimed while it lifts up its hands, it is bowed down while it is raised up, it becomes dropsical while it lusts, lame while it goes forward, it is overspread with leprosy while it is cleansed, it is filled with a demon while it aspires to divinity, it dies the death while it boldly excuses itself. 207DAnd they besought him. The prophets and the patriarchs desired the incarnation of the Lord.

VERS. 33.-- And taking him, etc. He who deserves to be healed is led apart from turbulent thoughts, from disordered deeds, and from ill-composed discourses. The fingers which are put into the ears are the words of the Holy Spirit, of whom it is said: This is the finger of God (Exod. VIII). And: The heavens are the works of thy fingers (Psal. VIII). He touched his tongue, etc. The foam from the flesh of the Lord is the divine wisdom, which looses the ignorance of the human race, so that it may say: I believe in God the Father almighty, etc. Apart. (BEDA.) The first way of salvation is to be led out from the crowd. The Lord leads the sick man out from the crowd, when the mind, by sins, etc., as far as therefore, spitting, he touches 208Athe tongue of the dumb man that it may be able to speak, when by the touch of his loving-kindness he forms mouths long brutish to utter the words of wisdom.

VERS. 34.-- And looking up, etc. (HIERON.) He groaned. He taught us to groan, and to lift up to heaven the treasure of our heart, which is purified by compunction of heart from the frivolous gladness of the flesh, as it is said: I roared with the groaning of my heart; Lord, all my desire is before thee, etc. And he said to him: Ephpheta, which is, Be thou opened. With the heart we believe unto justice (Rom. X), etc. And immediately they were opened, etc. The ears are opened to hymns and canticles and psalms. The tongue is loosed, that it may utter forth a good word, which neither threats nor words can restrain or forbid. Whence Paul: I am bound, but the word of God is not bound in me (I Tim. II).

208BVERS. 36.-- And he charged them that they should tell no man. He taught that we must glory not in mighty works, but in the cross and in humiliation. But the more he charged them, etc. A city set on a mountain and looked upon from every side cannot be hid (Matth. XV): humility always goes before glory.

Be thou opened. Properly, to the ears. For the ears were to be opened for hearing, and the tongue was to be loosed that it might be able to speak. Whence it follows: Immediately they were opened, etc.

VERS. 35.-- And he spoke right. (BEDA.) He alone speaks rightly, whether by confessing the Lord or by preaching to others, whose hearing divine grace brings back, that he may be able to hearken and be obedient to the heavenly commandments. Whose tongue the Lord instructs to speak by the touch of wisdom, which he himself is. Such a one can say: 208CO Lord, thou wilt open my lips (Psal. V), etc.

VERS. 36.-- But the more he charged them, etc. He who knows all things before they come to pass knew that they would preach the more; and by so charging them he willed to show to the slothful how much more diligently and how much more frequently [ferventius] they ought to preach, whom he commands to preach: since those who were forbidden could not hold their peace.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 2.-- Misereor super turbam. Quia verus homo miseretur et compatitur affectu humanae fragilitatis. Quod de septem panibus et paucis pisciculis quatuor millia hominum satiavit, divinae potentiae opus est contra Eutichetis errorem.

Triduo sustinent. Quare triduo sustinerent Matthaeus 208Ddicit sic (Matth. XV): Et ascendens in montem sedebat ibi, et accesserunt ad eum, etc. Turba triduo Dominum sustinet propter sanationem infirmorum suorum, cum electi quique fide sanctae Trinitatis lucidi Domino, pro suis suorumque peccatis, animae peccantis, scilicet, languoribus instanter supplicant.

Turba multa triduo Dominum sustinet, cum multitudo fidelium peccata sua per poenitentiam declinans ad Dominum se in opere, in locutione et cogitatione convertit.

(BEDA.) Hoc miraculo designatur, quod viam praesentis saeculi, aliter incolumes transire nequimus, alimento verbi sui Christus nos reficiat. Hoc vero typice, etc., usque ad ipse ab infimis delectationibus 209Aabstractam pane coeli reficit, atque ad appetitum supernae suavitatis cibi spiritualis dato pignore accendit.

VERS. 3.-- Si dimisero eos. Quia conversi peccatores in praesentis vitae via deficiunt, si in sua conscientia sine doctrinae pabulis dimittantur.

De longe. Moraliter. Qui nihil carnalis corruptionis expertus, ad servitutem Dei festinat, de longinquo non venit; qui etiam nulla impudicitia, nullis flagitiis inquinatus, solum autem conjugium expertus, nec iste de longinquo. Alii post multa flagitia veniunt: et ideo de longinquo.

VERS. 5.-- Septem. (BEDA.) Mysterium est Novi Testamenti, in quo septiformis Spiritus gratia plenius creditur et datur. Nec sunt panes hordeacei, 209Bsicut illi quinque de quibus quinque millia hominum satiata sunt, ne, sicut in lege, vitalis animae alimentum corporalibus sacramentis tegeretur. Hordei enim medulla tenacissima tegitur palea.

(HIER.) Septem panes, septem dona Spiritus sancti. Quatuor millia, annus Novi Testamenti cum quatuor temporibus. Septem sportae, septem primae Ecclesiae. Fragmenta panum mystici intellectus primae septimanae sunt. Pisciculi benedicti: libri Novi Testamenti, quomodo piscis assi partem resurgens postulat, et piscem superpositum prunis discipulis in captura piscium porrigit.

VERS. 6.-- Super terram. (BEDA.) In refectione quinque panum turba supra fenum discubuit; quae autem septem panibus reficienda est, super terram 209Cdiscumbere praecipitur: quia per legem carnis, etc., usque ad hic autem remota omni cupiditate carnali convivas Novi Testamenti spei permanentis firmamentum tanquam ipsius montis soliditas nullo feno interposito continet.

VERS. 7.-- Pisciculos. (ID.) Sanctos illius temporis, per quos eadem condita est Scriptura: vel quorum ipsa Scriptura, fidem, vitam et passiones continet; qui de fluctibus saeculi erepti, et divina benedictione consecrati refectionem nobis, ne in hujus mundi excursu deficiamus, vitae suae et mortis suae exemplo praebuere.

VERS. 8.-- Manducaverunt. (ID.) Sunt multi qui quamvis omnia sua relinquere, et altiora hujus mundi nequeant implere, tamen esurientes et sitientes justitiam 209Dsaturantur, cum audientes mandata Dei ad vitam perveniunt aeternam.

Quod superaverat. (ID.) Altiora praecepta vel exhortamenta, et consilia, quae generalis fidelium multitudo nequit attingere, sicut hic: Si vis perfectus esse, vade et vende omnia quae habes (Matth. XIX), etc.

Septem sportas. Septem sportae, Spiritus sancti gratia septiformi repleti. Fiunt enim sportae de junco, et palmarum foliis. Juncus nascitur super aquas, palma victorem coronat. Sancti quoque ne ab humore aeternitatis arescant, in ipso vitae fonte se collocant, et aeternae retributionis palmam exspectant.

VERS. 10.-- Et statim. Pro hoc in Matthaeo habemus: Ascendit in naviculam, et venit in fines Magedan. 210ASed non est dubitandum eumdem locum esse sub utroque nomine. Nam plerique codices habent Magedan etiam secundum Marcum.

VERS. 11.-- Quaerentes, etc. Quasi ea quae viderant, non fuissent signa. Sed quid quaerant per hoc determinatur, de coelo, vel in morem Eliae ignem de sublimi, vel ad similitudinem Samuelis aestivo tempore mugire tonitrua, vel fulgura vel imbres, quasi non possent et haec calumniari qui ea calumniabantur quae oculis viderant, manu tangebant, utilitate sentiebant. Vel signa de coelo, manna, ut qui paucis panibus multa hominum millia satiavit, nunc sicut Moyses manna coelitus misso populum omnem passim reficiat. Unde Joannes post edulium panum turbas quaesisse dicit: Quid operaris? Patres nostri manducaverunt 210Bmanna in deserto (Joan. VI), etc.

VERS. 12.-- Si dabitur. (AUG.) De concordia evangelistarum. In Marco ita scriptum esse dicit: et signum non dabitur ei.

(BEDA.) Generationi isti, id est tentantium Deum et contradicentium verbis ejus, non dabitur signum de coelo, quod tentantes, etc., usque ad ad impositionem manuum apostolorum gratiam sancti Spiritus de coelo plurimis credentibus infudit.

VERS. 13.-- Ascendit navim. (ID.) Septem sportis modo impletis, statim ascenderunt in naviculam, et venerunt in fines Magedan, et ibi navigantibus dictum est quod caverent a fermento Pharisaeorum et sadducaeorum. Sed Scriptura dicit quod obliti sunt panes tollere, in quo ostenditur quod modicam curam 210Ccarnis haberent intenti Domini praesentiae.

VERS. 17.-- Quid cogitatis. (BEDA.) Accepta occasione, docet quid significent quinque panes et septem, et pauci pisciculi, quinque millia hominum aut quatuor. Si enim fermentum Pharisaeorum et Herodis traditiones perversas et haeretica significat dogmata, quare cibi quibus nutritus est populus Dei non veram doctrinam integramque significat?

VERS. 23.-- Et exspuens. (ID.) Sputum ab intus a capite Domini procedit. Manus vero membra corporis exterius posita. Exspuens ergo in oculos caeci, manus suas imponit, ut videat: quia caecitatem humani generis et per invisibilia divinae pietatis dona et per ostensa a foris sacramenta assumpta humanitatis abstersit.

210DQuem uno verbo totum simul curare poterat, paulatim curat, ut magnitudinem humanae caecitatis ostendat: et quae vix et quasi per gradus ad lucem redeat, et gratiam suam nobis indicat, per quam singula perfectionis incrementa adjuvant.

VERS. 24.-- Video homines, etc. (HIER.) Omnes aestimat sibi superiores, se indignum judicans: ut David se vocari hominem, sed canem mortuum et pulicem unum. Primo autem, etc., usque ad huic Dominus secunda impositione manuum, lucem omnia clara videndi restituit: scilicet quid credendum, quomodo vivendum, quae praemia sunt speranda.

VERS. 25.-- Deinde iterum, etc. (HIER.) Ut clare videret omnia, id est per opera visibilia invisibilia 211Aintelligeret, et quae oculus non vidit, et clarum, animae suae statum post rubiginem peccati mundi cordis oculo contemplaretur. Beati enim mundo corde, quoniam ipsi Deum videbunt (Matth. V).

VERS. 26.-- Et misit. (HIER.) Ut videret in se quod ante non vidit. Non enim putat desperans homo de salute omnino posse se quod facere illuminatus per spem potest perficere.

Mystice. Admonet Dominus omnes qui agnitione veritatis illustrantur, ut ad cor redeant, et quantum sibi donatum fuerat sollicita mente perpendant, et beneficiis Dei digna operatione respondeant.

Nemini dixeris. (BEDA.) Quod sanationem silere jubet, exemplum est suis, ne si miranda faciant, favorem quaerant, sed divinis aspectibus sint placere 211Bcontenti.

VERS. 27.-- Quem me dicunt? Pulchre primo sententiam hominum interrogat, ne confessio eorum vulgi opinione, sed veritatis agnitione videatur infirmata: ne sicut Herodes de auditis haesitare putentur. Unde Petro secundum Matthaeum dicit: Caro et sanguis non revelavit tibi (Matth. XVI), id est doctrina hominum.

Homines. Pulchre qui hominum more diversam de Domino sententiam ferunt, homines dicuntur. Nam qui veritatem potentiae ejus pia mente cognoscunt, non homines, sed dii appellari merentur. Unde dicit illis: Vos vero, etc. Quasi dicat: Illi, homines, humana opinantes; sed vos, dii, quem me esse dicitis?

VERS. 29.-- Tu es Christus. (BEDA.) Omnia complectitur 211Cqui et naturam et nomen exprimit, in quo summa virtutum. Paulus dicit, etc., usque ad non licet tamen nescire generationis fidem.

VERS. 30.-- Et comminatus est. (BEDA.) Noluit se ante passionem et resurrectionem praedicari: quia completo sanguinis sacramento apostolis opportunius dicit: Euntes docete omnes gentes, baptizantes eos in nomine Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti (Matth. XVIII). Quia non prodesset eum publice praedicari, quem flagellatum sunt visuri, et crucifixum multa pati.

VERS. 31.-- Quoniam oportet, etc. (HIER.) Sicut gubernator peritus tempestatem in tranquillitate praecavens, nautas paratos vult esse suos. Sicut alibi: Si quis vult venire post me, abneget se (Matth. XVI), etc.

VERS. 32.-- Et apprehendens. (BEDA.) Amantis affectu 211Ddicens: Absit a te, Domine. Vel ut melius in Graeco habetur: Propitius esto, etc., usque ad apprehenditque eum in affectum suum, vel separatim ducit, ne praesentibus caeteris magistrum videatur arguere.

VERS. 33.-- Vade retro. (ID.) Multi putant quod non Petrus, sed adversarius Spiritus sit correctus, quo suggerente hoc Apostolus dicebat: Sed mihi error Apostoli de pietatis affectu veniens, nunquam incentivum videbitur diaboli. Diabolo dicitur: Vade retro, Satanas. Petro: Vade retro me, etc., id est sequere sententiam meam.

Quae Dei sunt, etc. Mea est voluntas et Patris, ut pro salute hominum moriar: tu non vis granum frumenti 212Ain terram cadere, ut multum fructum afferat.

VERS. 34.-- Si quis vult, etc. (BEDA.) Postquam mysterium suae passionis et resurrectionis ostendit, hortatur omnes ad imitandum passionis exemplum, et omnibus salutem promittit animarum. Sed perfectioribus, quanta passurus quod a mortuis resurrecturus esset aperuit, formam tribuens praedicandi, ut scilicet, pro suo quisque modo instruatur, neque infirmis altiora committantur.

Deneget. Non valet apprehendere quod ultra ipsum est si nescierit mactare quod est. Nisi quis a semetipso deficiat, ad Deum, qui supra ipsum est, non appropinquat. Tollat crucem. Vel per abstinentiam macerando corpus, vel per compassionem animum. De corpore dicit Paulus: Castigo corpus meum, et in 212Bservitutem redigo (II Cor. IX), etc. Idem de compassione animi: Quis infirmatur, et ego non infirmor? (II Cor. XI.)

VERS. 38.-- Qui enim. (BEDA.) Duo tempora Ecclesia significat, aliud persecutionis, quando ponenda est anima; aliud pacis, quando frangenda sunt desideria terrena.

Qui enim me, etc. Quia multi avaritiam vincunt, labentia despiciunt, sed fidei rectitudinem quam habent verecundia impediti, non exprimunt voce. Addit: Qui enim me, etc.

VERS. 39.-- Amen dico. (BEDA.) Cum de futura vita loqueretur, ideo necessarium fuit ut hoc promitteret de poenitenti vita, ut rudes adhuc discipuli hac consolatione confortarentur, sicut de Judaico 212Cpopulo dicitur: Dedit illis regiones gentium et labores populorum possederunt, ut custodiant justificationes ejus, et legem ejus requirant (Psal. CIV).

Regnum Dei, etc. Id est, Ecclesiam contra mundi hujus gloriam erectam. Vel futuram in coelis beatitudinem quam in monte viderunt. Quod pia provisione factum est, ut aeternum gaudium, tametsi ad breve momentum praelibatum, ad adversa ferenda fortius animaret.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 2.-- I have compassion on the crowd. Because as true man he has compassion and suffers with them in the affection of human frailty. That with seven loaves and a few little fishes he filled four thousand men is the work of divine power, against the error of Eutyches.

Three days they continue with me. Why they should continue three days Matthew 208Dtells thus (Matth. XV): And going up into a mountain he sat there, and they came to him, etc. The crowd continues with the Lord three days for the healing of their sick, when all the elect, made bright to the Lord by faith in the holy Trinity, make instant supplication for their own sins and for those of their people, that is, for the languors of the sinning soul.

A great crowd continues with the Lord three days, when the multitude of the faithful, turning aside from their sins by penance, turns itself to the Lord in deed, in speech, and in thought.

(BEDA.) By this miracle it is signified that we cannot otherwise pass unharmed the way of the present world: that Christ refresh us with the food of his word. But this typically, etc., as far as he himself refreshes with the bread of heaven [the soul] drawn away from the lowest delights, 209Aand kindles it, by the pledge given of spiritual food, to a longing for the sweetness above.

VERS. 3.-- If I shall send them away. Because converted sinners faint in the way of the present life, if they are dismissed into their own conscience without the food of doctrine.

From afar. Morally. He who, having known nothing of carnal corruption, hastens to the service of God, does not come from afar; he too who, defiled by no immodesty and by no shameful deeds, has known wedlock only, neither does he come from afar. Others come after many shameful deeds: and therefore from afar.

VERS. 5.-- Seven. (BEDA.) It is the mystery of the New Testament, in which the sevenfold grace of the Spirit is more fully believed and given. Nor are they barley loaves, 209Blike those five with which five thousand men were filled, lest, as in the law, the food of the living soul should be covered over with bodily sacraments. For the pith of barley is covered with a most clinging husk.

(HIER.) The seven loaves are the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit. The four thousand are the year of the New Testament with its four seasons. The seven baskets are the seven first Churches. The fragments of the loaves are the mystical understandings of the first week. The blessed little fishes are the books of the New Testament: as when, rising again, he asks for a piece of broiled fish, and, at the catch of fishes, offers the disciples a fish laid upon the coals.

VERS. 6.-- Upon the ground. (BEDA.) At the refreshment of the five loaves the crowd sat down upon the grass; but that which is to be refreshed with seven loaves is bidden to sit down upon the ground 209C: because by the law of the flesh, etc., as far as but here, all carnal desire being removed, the firm ground of an abiding hope, as it were the solidity of the mountain itself, holds the guests of the New Testament, with no grass laid between.

VERS. 7.-- Little fishes. (ID.) The saints of that time, by whom that same Scripture was composed; or whose faith, life and passions Scripture itself contains: who, snatched out of the waves of the world and consecrated by the divine blessing, furnished us refreshment by the example of their life and of their death, lest we faint in the course of this world.

VERS. 8.-- They did eat. (ID.) There are many who, although they cannot leave all their goods and fulfil the higher things of this world, yet, hungering and thirsting after justice, 209Dare filled, when, hearing the commandments of God, they come to life eternal.

That which was over. (ID.) The higher precepts or exhortations and counsels, which the general multitude of the faithful cannot attain, as here: If thou wilt be perfect, go and sell all that thou hast (Matth. XIX), etc.

Seven baskets. The seven baskets are those filled with the sevenfold grace of the Holy Spirit. For baskets are made of rushes and of the leaves of palms. The rush is born upon the waters; the palm crowns the victor. The saints too, lest they wither away from the moisture of eternity, set themselves in the very fountain of life, and look for the palm of the eternal reward.

VERS. 10.-- And immediately. For this we have in Matthew: He went up into a boat, and came into the coasts of Magedan. 210ABut it is not to be doubted that the same place is meant under either name. For very many codices have Magedan in Mark also.

VERS. 11.-- Seeking, etc. As though the things they had seen had not been signs. But what they seek is determined by this: from heaven, either fire from on high after the manner of Elias, or, after the likeness of Samuel, that thunders should bellow in summer time, or lightnings, or showers, as if they could not cavil at these too, who were cavilling at the things they had seen with their eyes, touched with their hand, and felt by their profit. Or signs from heaven, manna: that he who with a few loaves filled many thousands of men should now, like Moses, refresh the whole people everywhere with manna sent down from heaven. Whence John says that after the eating of the loaves the crowds asked: What dost thou work? Our fathers did eat 210Bmanna in the desert (Joan. VI), etc.

VERS. 12.-- If a sign shall be given. (AUG.) On the Concord of the Evangelists. He says that in Mark it is written thus: and a sign shall not be given to it.

(BEDA.) To this generation, that is, of those tempting God and contradicting his words, there shall not be given a sign from heaven, which they that tempt him, etc., as far as at the imposition of the hands of the apostles he poured out from heaven the grace of the Holy Spirit upon very many that believed.

VERS. 13.-- He went up into the ship. (ID.) The seven baskets being but now filled, immediately they went up into the boat and came into the coasts of Magedan; and there it was said to them as they sailed that they should beware of the leaven of the Pharisees and of the Sadducees. But the Scripture says that they had forgotten to take bread, wherein it is shown that they had little care 210Cof the flesh, being intent upon the presence of the Lord.

VERS. 17.-- Why do you reason. (BEDA.) Taking the occasion, he teaches what the five loaves and the seven signify, and the few little fishes, and the five thousand men or the four. For if the leaven of the Pharisees and of Herod signifies perverse traditions and heretical dogmas, why do the foods with which the people of God was nourished not signify true and entire doctrine?

VERS. 23.-- And spitting. (ID.) The spittle proceeds from within, from the head of the Lord. But the hands are members of the body set without. Spitting therefore into the eyes of the blind man, he lays his hands upon him, that he may see: because he wiped away the blindness of the human race both by the invisible gifts of the divine loving-kindness and by the sacraments of the humanity he assumed, shown forth from without.

210DHim whom he could have healed wholly and at once with a single word, he heals little by little, that he may show the greatness of human blindness: and that it scarcely and as it were by degrees comes back to the light, and he makes known to us his grace, by which the several increases of perfection lend their aid.

VERS. 24.-- I see men, etc. (HIER.) He esteems all men above himself, judging himself unworthy: as David [refused] to be called a man, but a dead dog and a single flea. But first, etc., as far as to him the Lord, by a second imposition of hands, restored the light of seeing all things clearly: namely, what is to be believed, how one is to live, what rewards are to be hoped for.

VERS. 25.-- Then again, etc. (HIER.) That he might see all things clearly, that is, that through visible works he might understand the invisible, 211Aand the things which the eye hath not seen, and might contemplate with the eye of a clean heart the clear state of his own soul after the rust of sin. For blessed are the clean of heart, for they shall see God (Matth. V).

VERS. 26.-- And he sent him. (HIER.) That he might see in himself what before he did not see. For a man despairing of salvation does not think that he can at all do that which, being enlightened, he is able to accomplish through hope.

Mystically. The Lord admonishes all who are enlightened by the acknowledging of the truth to return to their heart, and with a careful mind to weigh how much had been granted them, and to answer the benefits of God with worthy working.

Tell it to nobody. (BEDA.) That he bids the healing be kept silent is an example to his own, that, if they do things to be wondered at, they should not seek favour, but be content to please 211Bthe divine gaze.

VERS. 27.-- Whom do men say that I am? Fitly does he first ask the opinion of men, lest their confession should seem weakened by the opinion of the crowd, but by the acknowledging of the truth: lest, like Herod, they be thought to waver about the things they had heard. Whence he says to Peter, according to Matthew: Flesh and blood hath not revealed it to thee (Matth. XVI), that is, the teaching of men.

Men. Fitly are they called men who after the manner of men bring forth a divergent opinion concerning the Lord. For they who with a pious mind acknowledge the truth of his power deserve to be called, not men, but gods. Whence he says to them: But you, etc. As if he said: They, being men, opine the things of men; but you, being gods, whom do you say that I am?

VERS. 29.-- Thou art the Christ. (BEDA.) He embraces all things 211Cwho expresses both the nature and the name, in which is the sum of the virtues. Paul says, etc., as far as yet it is not permitted to be ignorant of the faith of his generation.

VERS. 30.-- And he charged them. (BEDA.) He would not have himself preached before the passion and the resurrection: because, the sacrament of his blood being accomplished, he says to the apostles more opportunely: Going, teach ye all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost (Matth. XVIII). Because it would not profit that he be publicly preached whom they were about to see scourged, and crucified, and suffering many things.

VERS. 31.-- That it behoveth, etc. (HIER.) As a skilled pilot, guarding against a storm while it is calm, wills his own sailors to be ready. As elsewhere: If any man will come after me, let him deny himself (Matth. XVI), etc.

VERS. 32.-- And taking him. (BEDA.) With the affection of one that loves, 211Dsaying: Far be it from thee, Lord. Or, as it is better had in the Greek: Be thou merciful, etc., as far as and he takes hold of him into his own affection, or leads him aside, lest he seem to rebuke his master while the rest are present.

VERS. 33.-- Go behind. (ID.) Many think that it was not Peter, but the adversary spirit, that was corrected, at whose suggestion the Apostle said this: But to me the Apostle's error, coming from the affection of piety, will never seem the devil's prompting. To the devil it is said: Go behind, Satan. [Vulg. Matt. 4:10 reads Vade Satana, "Go, Satan," without the adverb] To Peter: Go behind me, etc., that is, follow my judgment.

The things that are of God, etc. It is my will and the Father's that I should die for the salvation of men: thou wilt not have the grain of wheat 212Afall into the ground, that it may bring forth much fruit.

VERS. 34.-- If any man will, etc. (BEDA.) After he had shown the mystery of his passion and of his resurrection, he exhorts all to imitate the example of the passion, and to all he promises the salvation of souls. But to the more perfect he disclosed how great things he was to suffer, and that he was to rise again from the dead, giving thereby a form of preaching: namely, that each be instructed according to his own measure, and that higher things be not committed to the weak.

Let him deny himself. He is not able to lay hold of what is beyond himself, if he has not known how to slay what he is. Unless a man fail to his own self, he does not draw near to God, who is above him. Let him take up his cross. Either by wearing down the body through abstinence, or the mind through compassion. Of the body Paul says: I chastise my body, and bring it into 212Bsubjection (II Cor. IX), etc. The same, of compassion of mind: Who is weak, and I am not weak? (II Cor. XI.)

VERS. 38.-- For he that shall be ashamed. (BEDA.) The Church signifies two times: one of persecution, when the soul must be laid down; the other of peace, when earthly desires must be broken.

For he that shall be ashamed of me, etc. Because many overcome avarice and despise things that pass away, but, hindered by bashfulness, do not utter with the voice the rightness of faith which they hold. He adds: For he that shall be ashamed of me, etc.

VERS. 39.-- Amen I say. (BEDA.) Since he was speaking of the life to come, it was therefore necessary that he should promise this concerning the penitent life, that the disciples, as yet untaught, might be strengthened by this consolation, as it is said of the Jewish 212Cpeople: He gave them the lands of the Gentiles, and they possessed the labours of the peoples, that they might keep his justifications, and seek after his law (Psal. CIV).

The kingdom of God, etc. That is, the Church raised up against the glory of this world. Or the blessedness to come in heaven, which they saw upon the mountain. Which was done by a merciful provision, that the eternal joy, though foretasted for a brief moment, might the more strongly hearten them to bear adversities.

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 1.-- Et post dies, etc. (HIER.) Post comminationem crucis resurrectionis gloria ostenditur, unde sequitur:

Et post dies, etc. Ut non timerent opprobria crucis, qui oculis suis viderant gloriam futurae resurrectionis.

212D(BEDA.) Et post dies sex. Lucas: Factum est autem post haec verba fere dies octo, etc., usque ad ne in octavo retributionis tempore ab irato judice corripiamur. In montem excelsum. In montem ut oraret, etc., usque ad tunc aeterna ejus visione merebuntur laetari.

Transfiguratus. Non veram substantiam carnis amisit, sed gloriam futurae suae, vel nostrae resurrectionis ostendit. Talis post judicium cunctis apparebit electis: in judicio, in forma servi et bonis et malis, ut impii quem sprevere, quem Judaei negavere, milites quem crucifixere, Pilatus Herodesque quem judicavere nequeant judicem agnoscere.

213AVERS. 2.-- Et vestimenta. Vestimenta Domini, sancti. Unde: Qui in Christo baptizati estis Christum induistis (Gal. III). Haec Domino in mundo manente videbantur despecta, sed ipso montem petente novo candore refulgent. Quia nunc quidem filii sumus Dei, sed nondum apparuit quid erimus. Cum ergo apparuerit, similes ei erimus, quia videbimus eum sicuti est (I Joan. III). Unde subditur.

VERS. 3.-- Et apparuit, etc. Moyses sanctus mortuus, Elias vivus in coelum raptus, visi in majestate cum Domino (ut Lucas dicit) futuram in illo omnium sanctorum gloriam significant: qui tempore judicii vel vivi in carne reperientur vel mortui.

Elias cum. (BEDA.) Legislator et eximius prophetarum apparent: loquuntur cum Domino in carne 213Bvivente, ostendentes ipsum esse quem lex et prophetae promiserunt; non in infimis, sed in monte cum illo, quia qui terrena desideria mente transeunt solvi majestatem Scripturae sanctae, quae in Domino impleta est, perspiciunt.

VERS. 4.-- Rabbi. Quamvis stupefactus, etc., usque ad unum habentia tabernaculum, id est Ecclesiam Dei. Bonum est. Multo ergo melius choris sanctorum interesse, Deitatis perfrui visione: si sic delectat humanitas Christi transfigurata, et duorum societas ad punctum ostensa.

VERS. 6.-- Nubes obumbrans. Unde quia quaesivit materiale tabernaculum, nubis accepit umbraculum, ut discat in resurrectione non tegmine domorum, sed Spiritus sancti sanctos esse protegendos. 213CHic est. Quasi dicat: Hic vere est ille quem Moyses iste vobis promisit, etc., usque ad ipsaque illustrabitur gratia et in perpetuum protegetur.

VERS. 7.-- Et statim. (BEDA.) Cum fieret vox super Filium hominis, inventus est ipse solus. Quia cum manifestaverit seipsum electis, erit Deus omnia in omnibus: imo ipse cum suis unus per omnia Christus, id est, caput cum corpore splendebit, unde: Nemo ascendit in coelum, nisi qui descendit de coelo, Filius hominis, qui est in coelo (Joan. III).

VERS. 8.-- Praecepit illis. Non vult gloriam regni futuri et triumphi quam in monte ostenderat publice praedicari: ne et incredibile esset pro magnitudine, et post tantam gloriam apud rudes animos sequens crux scandalum faceret.

213DVERS. 9.-- Verbum continuerunt. (HIER.) Id est, cum absorpta mors fuerit in victoria, non erunt in memoria priora, cum abstulerit Dominus sordem filiae Sion, auferens omnem lacrymam ab oculis sanctorum.

VERS. 10.-- Quid ergo. Tradunt scribae, secundum Malachiam, quod Elias veniet ante adventum Salvatoris, et reducet corda patrum ad patres eorum, et restituet omnia in antiquum statum. Existimant ergo discipuli hanc adventus gloriam esse, quam viderant in monte, et dicunt: Si jam venisti in gloria, quare praecursor tuus non apparet? maxime quia vident recessisse Eliam.

VERS. 11.-- Restituet, etc. Scilicet convertet cor 214Apatrum ad filios, et cor filiorum ad patres. Restituet enim morti quod debet et diu vivendo sustulit, unde addit: Quomodo scriptum. Quasi dicat: Sicut de Christi passione prophetae multifarie scripserunt: sic et Elias multa passurus est. Restituet ergo omnia. Primo, corda hominum illius temporis ad credendum in Christo, resistendum Antichristo. Deinde, ponet animam pro Christo.

VERS. 13.-- Turbam magnam. (HIER.) Non est requies sub sole: semper occidit parvulos invidia, magnos percutiunt fulgura montes, alii discentes cum fide, alii invidentes cum fastu, ad Ecclesiam veniunt. Conquirentes. (BEDA.) Quid conquirant Evangelista non dicit, sed potest intelligi de hoc quaestionem motam fuisse, quare discipuli daemoniacum 214Bqui in medio erat, non possent liberare: hoc enim ex sequentibus concipi potest.

VERS. 15.-- Et interrogavit. (ID.) Nota quod loca rebus congruunt. In monte orat, transformatur, discipulis arcana suae majestatis aperit, in imo a turba excipitur, miserorum fletu pulsatur. Sursum discipulis mysteria regni reserat, deorsum turbis peccata infidelitatis exprobrat. Sursum vocem Patris his qui audire poterant pandit, deorsum malos spiritus expellit. Nunc pro meritorum qualitate aliis ascendere, aliis non desistit descendere. Et interrogavit. (HIER.) Scit et interrogat, ut confessio pariat salutem, et murmur cordis nostri sermonibus solvatur prius ut est: Dic iniquitates tuas prius, ut justificeris.

VERS. 17.-- Et dixi discipulis. Latenter culpam 214Cobliquat in discipulos, cum saepe curatio non curantium, sed curandorum impediatur vitio.

VERS. 18.-- O generatio. Non homini irascitur, sed vitio. Non taedio affectuum hoc dicit, sed ut medicus aegro contra praecepta agenti. Et arguit per unum Judaeos incredulitatis.

VERS. 19.-- Statim spiritus. Quia dum post peccatum ad Dominum converti volumus, magis a diabolo impugnamur, ut vel excutiat virtutem, vel vindicet suam expulsionem.

VERS. 20.-- Ab infantia. (HIER.) Significat gentilem populum cui nativitate increvit cultus idolorum, ut stulte immolaret filios suos daemoniis, unde sequitur:

VERS. 21.-- Et frequenter eum, etc. Alii enim 214Dignem, alii aquam venerabantur. Adjuva. (HIER.) Credulitas nostra, ut rostrata lingua infirma est, nisi innixa adjutorio Dei. Fides cum lacrymis optata vota capit, ut est: Fiat tibi secundum fidem tuam (Matth. XV).

VERS. 25.-- Discerpens, etc. (HIER.) Discerpit diabolus appropinquantes ad salutem, quod est ei esca dilecta, quos in ventrem suum trahere desiderat per terrores et damna, ut Job: Factus, etc. (ISID., RAB.) etc. Sanatis enim dicitur: Mortui enim estis, et vita vestra abscondita est cum Christo in Deo (Col. III). Unde infirmitas Christianorum non est mors, sed mortis similitudo.

VERS. 28.-- Hoc genus in nullo. (HIER.) Stultitia 215Aad luxuriam carnis pertinet et jejunio sanatur. Ira et ignavia oratione depellitur. Medicina cujusque vulneris adhibenda est ei. Non sanat oculum quod calcaneum, jejunio passiones corporis, oratione sanantur pestes mentis.

VERS. 30.-- Occident eum, etc. Prosperis miscet tristia, ut cum venerint, non impraemeditatis ferantur animis. Si contristat eos, quia occidendus est, debet laetificare quia die tertia resurrecturus est.

VERS. 31.-- Ignorabant. Non tam ingenii tarditate quam amore, quia Deum verum cognoverunt, moriturum credere nequibant. Et quia eis in figuris loqui consueverunt, horrentes ejus mortem, in his quoque eum loqui figurate putabant.

VERS. 32.-- Capharnaum. (HIER.) Villa consolationis: 215Bcongruit praedictae sententiae: Occisus die tertia resurget (Joan. XII). Granum frumenti moritur, ut multiplicius colligatur: si non moritur, solum manet. Quid in via, etc. In via tractabant de principatu: similis tractatio loco. Principatus enim vi ingreditur, sic deseritur, et dum tenetur labitur: et incertum in qua mansione, id est, in qua die finiatur; unde dicit: Qui vult esse primus, etc.

VERS. 33.-- Inter se disputaverunt. (BEDA.) Hinc exorta videtur disputatio, quia viderant Petrum et Jacobum et Joannem seorsum ductos in montem, et aliquod secretum ibi esse creditum eis, sed et Petro, sicut Matthaeus dicit, claves regni coelorum promissas, et Ecclesiam super petram fidei, a qua ipse nomen acceperit, aedificandam. Putabant ergo 215Cvel illos tres caeteris, vel omnibus Petrum esse praelatum.

VERS. 34.-- Et residens, etc. Illi euntes disputabant de principatu, et ipse sedens docet humilitatem. Principes enim laborant, humiles quiescunt. Desiderium gloriae vult humilitate sanare, et primo simplici monet imperio, mox innocentiae puerilis exemplo.

VERS. 35.-- Complexus. Significat humiles dignos esse suo complexu, qui jure possunt gloriari et dicere: Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me (Cant. II).

VERS. 36.-- Quisquis unum. Vel simpliciter pauperes Christi ab his qui velint esse majores, pro 215Dejus honore ostendit esse recipiendos. Vel ipsos malitia parvulos esse suadet, ut tanquam parvuli simplices sint. Et quicunque. Quia in pueris se recipi docebat, ut caput in membris, ne putaretur hoc solum esse quod videbatur, adjunxit: Et quicunque me susceperit, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Respondit illi. Quia dixerat: Quisquis unum ex hujusmodi pueris receperit, me recipit, intelligit Joannes quod in nomine ejus eos non recipiant, qui non sincere ambulant; unde ait: Magister, vidimus quemdam. Quasi dicat: Hic in nomine tuo non debet suscipi. Et prohibuimus eum. Putavit eum excludendum a beneficio qui non utitur officio, sed docetur neminem a bono quod ex 216Aparte habet arcendum, sed ad hoc potius quod non habet provocandum: unde: Nolite prohibere.

VERS. 38.-- Nolite prohibere. (BEDA.) Hinc Apostolus: Sed sive occasione sive veritate Christus annuntietur, etc., usque ad detestari et prohibere debemus.

VERS. 41.-- Et quisquis scandalizaverit. Quanquam hoc generale sit, potest tamen secundum consequentiam sermonis contra apostolos dici, qui de primatu disputabant et eos quos ad fidem vocabant, exemplo suo perdere poterant. (BEDA.) Nota quod in bono opere nostro aliquando cavendum est scandalum proximi, aliquando pro nihilo habendum: in quantum sine peccato possumus, vitare proximorum scandalum debemus. Si autem pro veritate 216Bscandalum sumitur, utilius nasci permittitur quam veritas relinquatur.

Mola asinaria. (HIER.) Secundum morem provinciae, etc., usque ad tolerabilior utcunque eum inferni poena cruciaret.

VERS. 42.-- Et si scandalizaverit. Quia supra docuit, ne scandalizemus eos qui credunt in eum, admonet quantum debemus eos cavere qui scandalizant. Σκάνδαλον Graece offendiculum vel ruina vel impactio pedis. Vel, ut alii, scrupulus. Ille ergo scandalizat fratrem qui ei dicto vel facto occasionem ruinae praebet. Manus tua. Amicus, cujus ope et consilio indigemus quotidie. Sed si hic laedere in causa animae voluerit, excludendus est a nostra societate, ne si cum perdito in hac vita partem habere 216Cvolumus, simul cum illo pereamus.

Quam duas manus. (HIER.) Duae manus principatus, humilitas et superbia. Abscinde superbiam, tene humile principatum.

VERS. 43.-- Ubi vermis. (BEDA.) Fetor vermium de corruptione carnis et sanguinis, ideoque caro recens sale conditur, ut exsiccato humore sanguineo, vermis esse nequeat. Caro ergo et sanguis vermes creant, quia delectatio carnalis cui condimentum continentiae non resistit, poenam luxuriosis generat aeternam. Debemus ergo corpus continentiae sale, et mentem condimento sapientiae ab erroris et vitiorum labe castigare.

VERS. 48.-- Omnis enim igne. Quia tertio mentionem fecit ignis et vermis, ostendit quomodo utrumque 216Dvaleamus vitare. Omnis enim igne, mire dictum est: Quod enim sale salitur, vermis putredinem arcet. Quod vero igne, id est, in igne, asperso sale carnem quoque consumit. Haec secundum legem in hostiis fiebant quae in altari cremabantur, ubi in omni victima et sacrificio sal offerri praeceptum est. Omnis victima sale salietur. Ille enim vere Domini victima est qui corpus et animam, etc., usque ad dilectos et proximos nobis abnegare. Omnis victima sale salietur. (HIER.) Victima genus humanum, quod hic sapientiae sale vel ratione salitur, dum corruptio sanguinis, custodia putredinis, et mater vermium hic consumitur, et post purgatorio igne examinabitur.

217AVERS. 49.-- Quod si sal, etc. Id est, si quis semel condimentis veritatis refectus ad apostasiam redierit: quo alio doctore corrigitur, qui eam quam gustavit sapientiam, vel adversis territus, vel prosperis illectus respuit? Unde: Quis medebitur incantatori a serpente percusso? (Eccli. XII.) In hoc Judam et socios ejus significare creditur, qui phylargyria corruptus, et apostolatum perdere, et Dominum tradere non dubitavit. Quia autem sunt multi quos dum major scientia erigit, ab aliorum societate disjungit, et quo plus sapiunt, eo plus a concordiae virtute desipiunt. subjungit: Habete in vobis sal et pacem. (BEDA.) Habere sal sine pace, non virtutis est donum, sed damnationis argumentum. Quo enim quisque melius sapit, eo deterius delinquit.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- And after days, etc. (HIER.) After the threatening of the cross the glory of the resurrection is shown, whence there follows:

And after days, etc. That they might not fear the reproaches of the cross, who had seen with their own eyes the glory of the resurrection to come.

212D(BEDA.) And after six days. Luke: And it came to pass, about eight days after these words, etc., as far as lest in the eighth time of retribution we be seized upon by an angry judge. Into a high mountain. Into a mountain, that he might pray, etc., as far as then shall they deserve to rejoice in the eternal vision of him.

Transfigured. He did not lose the true substance of the flesh, but showed the glory of his own resurrection to come, or of ours. Such will he appear to all the elect after the judgment: at the judgment, in the form of a servant, to the good and to the wicked alike, so that the impious may not be able to recognize as judge him whom they spurned, whom the Jews denied, whom the soldiers crucified, whom Pilate and Herod judged [nequeant; read queant, "may be able"].

213AVERS. 2.-- And his garments. The garments of the Lord are the saints. Whence: You who have been baptized in Christ have put on Christ (Gal. III). These, while the Lord remained in the world, seemed despised; but when he sought the mountain they shine again with a new whiteness. Because now indeed we are the sons of God, but it hath not yet appeared what we shall be. When therefore he shall appear, we shall be like to him, because we shall see him as he is (I Joan. III). Whence it is subjoined.

VERS. 3.-- And there appeared, etc. Moses, a holy man dead, and Elias, caught up alive into heaven, seen in majesty with the Lord (as Luke says), signify the glory of all the saints to come in him: who at the time of judgment shall be found either alive in the flesh or dead.

Elias with. (BEDA.) The lawgiver and the chief of the prophets appear: they speak with the Lord living in the flesh, 213Bshowing that he is the one whom the law and the prophets promised; not in the lowest places, but on the mountain with him, because they who pass beyond earthly desires in mind perceive that the majesty of Holy Scripture, which has been fulfilled in the Lord, is unfolded.

VERS. 4.-- Rabbi. Although astonished, etc., as far as having one tabernacle, that is, the Church of God. It is good. Much better therefore is it to be among the choirs of the saints, and to enjoy the vision of the Godhead: if the humanity of Christ transfigured so delights, and the company of two shown for a moment.

VERS. 6.-- A cloud overshadowing. Whence, because he sought a material tabernacle, he received the shade of a cloud, that he might learn that in the resurrection the saints are to be protected, not by the covering of houses, but by the Holy Spirit. 213CThis is. As if he said: This is truly he whom that Moses promised you, etc., as far as and it shall be enlightened by the same grace, and shall be protected for ever.

VERS. 7.-- And immediately. (BEDA.) When the voice came over the Son of man, he alone was found. Because when he shall have manifested himself to the elect, God will be all in all: nay rather, he himself with his own, one Christ throughout all, that is, the head with the body, shall shine forth; whence: No man hath ascended into heaven, but he that descended from heaven, the Son of man, who is in heaven (Joan. III).

VERS. 8.-- He charged them. He wills not that the glory of the kingdom to come and of the triumph which he had shown on the mountain be publicly preached: lest both it should be past belief for its greatness, and, after so great a glory, the cross that followed should make a scandal among untaught minds.

213DVERS. 9.-- They kept the word. (HIER.) That is, when death shall have been swallowed up in victory, the former things shall not be in memory, when the Lord shall have taken away the filth of the daughter of Sion, wiping away every tear from the eyes of the saints.

VERS. 10.-- What then. The scribes hand down, according to Malachy, that Elias shall come before the coming of the Saviour, and shall bring back the hearts of the fathers to their fathers, and shall restore all things to their ancient state. The disciples therefore judge this to be the glory of his coming which they had seen on the mountain, and they say: If thou hast already come in glory, why does thy forerunner not appear? especially because they see that Elias has withdrawn.

VERS. 11.-- He shall restore, etc. That is, he shall turn the heart 214Aof the fathers to the children, and the heart of the children to the fathers. For he shall restore to death what he owes and what by living long he has withheld; whence he adds: How it is written. As if he said: As the prophets wrote in many ways of the passion of Christ, so also Elias is to suffer many things. He shall therefore restore all things. First, the hearts of the men of that time, to believe in Christ and to resist Antichrist. Then, he shall lay down his life for Christ.

VERS. 13.-- A great crowd. (HIER.) There is no rest under the sun: envy always slays the little ones, lightnings strike the great mountains; some learning with faith, others envying with pride, come to the Church. Questioning. (BEDA.) What they question about the Evangelist does not say, but it may be understood that a question had been raised about this: why the disciples could not free the demoniac 214Bwho was in their midst: for this can be gathered from what follows.

VERS. 15.-- And he asked. (ID.) Note that the places agree with the things. On the mountain he prays, is transformed, opens to the disciples the secrets of his majesty, in the low place he is received by the crowd, and is beaten upon by the weeping of the wretched. Above he unlocks to the disciples the mysteries of the kingdom, below he upbraids the crowds with the sins of unbelief. Above he lays open the voice of the Father to those who were able to hear, below he drives out evil spirits. Now, according to the quality of their merits, he ceases not to ascend for some and to descend for others. And he asked. (HIER.) He knows, and he asks, that confession may bring forth salvation, and that the murmur of our heart may first be loosed in words, as it is: Tell thy iniquities first, that thou mayest be justified.

VERS. 17.-- And I spoke to the disciples. Secretly he turns the blame 214Caside upon the disciples, whereas often the cure is hindered by the fault, not of those who heal, but of those to be healed.

VERS. 18.-- O generation. He is angry not with the man, but with the vice. He says this not out of weariness of feeling, but as a physician to a sick man who acts against his orders. And through one he convicts the Jews of unbelief.

VERS. 19.-- Immediately the spirit. Because while, after sin, we will to be converted to the Lord, we are the more assailed by the devil, that he may either shake out our virtue, or avenge his own expulsion.

VERS. 20.-- From his infancy. (HIER.) It signifies the Gentile people, in whom from birth the worship of idols grew, so that it foolishly sacrificed its own sons to demons; whence there follows:

VERS. 21.-- And oftentimes he, etc. For some 214Dworshipped fire, others water. Help. (HIER.) Our belief, like a beaked tongue, is weak, unless it lean upon the help of God. Faith with tears obtains the prayers it desired, as it is: Be it done to thee according to thy faith (Matth. XV).

VERS. 25.-- Tearing him, etc. (HIER.) The devil tears those who draw near to salvation, which is his beloved food, whom he desires to drag into his own belly by terrors and by losses, as in Job: He was made, etc. (ISID., RAB.) etc. For to the healed it is said: For you are dead, and your life is hid with Christ in God (Col. III). Whence the infirmity of Christians is not death, but the likeness of death.

VERS. 28.-- This kind by nothing. (HIER.) Folly 215Abelongs to the luxury of the flesh, and is healed by fasting. Anger and sloth are driven off by prayer. The medicine of each wound must be applied to it. What heals the heel does not heal the eye; by fasting the passions of the body, by prayer the plagues of the mind, are healed.

VERS. 30.-- They shall kill him, etc. He mingles sad things with prosperous, that when they come they may not be borne with unprepared minds. If it saddens them that he is to be killed, it ought to gladden them that on the third day he is to rise again.

VERS. 31.-- They knew not. Not so much from slowness of wit as from love: because they had known him to be true God, they could not believe that he would die. And because they were accustomed to speak to them in figures, shuddering at his death, they thought that in these things also he spoke figuratively.

VERS. 32.-- Capharnaum. (HIER.) The village of consolation: 215Bit agrees with the foregoing sentence: Being slain, on the third day he shall rise again (Joan. XII). The grain of wheat dies, that it may be gathered more manifoldly: if it does not die, it remains alone. What in the way, etc. In the way they were treating of the primacy: a treating suited to the place. For a princedom is entered by force, and so is forsaken, and while it is held it slips away: and it is uncertain in what lodging, that is, on what day, it shall be ended; whence he says: He that will be first, etc.

VERS. 33.-- They had disputed among themselves. (BEDA.) Hence the dispute seems to have arisen, because they had seen Peter and James and John led apart into the mountain, and that some secret had there been entrusted to them; and to Peter also, as Matthew says, the keys of the kingdom of heaven promised, and the Church to be built upon the rock of faith, from which he himself received his name. They thought therefore 215Ceither that those three were set before the rest, or that Peter was set before them all.

VERS. 34.-- And sitting down, etc. They as they went were disputing about the primacy, and he, sitting, teaches humility. For princes toil, the humble are at rest. He wills to heal the desire of glory by humility, and first admonishes by a simple command, then by the example of a child's innocence.

VERS. 35.-- Having embraced him. He signifies that the humble are worthy of his embrace, who can rightly glory and say: His left hand is under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me (Cant. II).

VERS. 36.-- Whosoever shall receive one. Either he shows simply that the poor of Christ are to be received by those who would be greater, for 215Dhis honour. Or he persuades them to be themselves little ones in malice, that they may be simple as little ones. And whosoever. Because he was teaching that he is received in the children, as the head in the members, lest this only should be thought to be what appeared, he added: And whosoever shall receive me, etc.

VERS. 37.-- He answered him. Because he had said: Whosoever shall receive one of such children, receiveth me, [Vulg. Mark 9:36 reads receperit in nomine meo, "shall receive in my name"] John understands that they who do not walk sincerely do not receive them in his name; whence he says: Master, we saw a certain man. As if he said: This man ought not to be received in thy name. And we forbade him. He thought that one who does not use the office was to be shut out from the benefit; but he is taught that no one is to be barred from the good which he has in 216Apart, but rather to be urged on to that which he has not: whence: Forbid him not.

VERS. 38.-- Forbid him not. (BEDA.) Hence the Apostle: But whether by occasion or by truth Christ be preached, etc., as far as we ought to detest and to forbid.

VERS. 41.-- And whosoever shall scandalize. Although this be general, yet according to the sequence of the discourse it can be said against the apostles, who were disputing about the primacy and could destroy by their own example those whom they were calling to the faith. (BEDA.) Note that in our good work the scandal of a neighbour is sometimes to be guarded against, sometimes to be counted as nothing: in so far as we can without sin, we ought to avoid the scandal of our neighbours. But if scandal is taken for the sake of truth, 216Bit is more useful that it be suffered to arise than that the truth be forsaken.

An ass's millstone. (HIER.) According to the custom of the province, etc., as far as a more tolerable punishment of hell would in any case torment him.

VERS. 42.-- And if it shall scandalize thee. Because above he taught that we should not scandalize those who believe in him, he admonishes how much we ought to beware of those who scandalize. Σκάνδαλον in Greek is a stumbling-block, or a fall, or the striking of the foot. Or, as others say, a sharp stone. He therefore scandalizes his brother who by word or by deed affords him occasion of falling. Thy hand. A friend, of whose help and counsel we have need daily. But if he should be willing to wound us in the cause of the soul, he must be shut out from our fellowship, lest, if we will to have part with a lost man in this life, 216Cwe perish together with him.

Than having two hands. (HIER.) The two hands of princedom are humility and pride. Cut off pride; hold a humble princedom.

VERS. 43.-- Where the worm. (BEDA.) The stench of worms comes of the corruption of flesh and of blood; and therefore fresh meat is seasoned with salt, so that, the bloody humour being dried out, there can be no worm. Flesh therefore and blood breed worms, because carnal delight, which the seasoning of continence does not withstand, begets eternal punishment for the lustful. We ought therefore to chastise the body with the salt of continence, and the mind with the seasoning of wisdom, from the stain of error and of vices.

VERS. 48.-- For every one shall be salted with fire. Because for the third time he made mention of fire and of the worm, he shows how we may be able to avoid 216Dboth. For every one with fire, it is wondrously said: for what is salted with salt keeps off the rottenness of the worm. But what is salted with fire, that is, in fire, when salt has been sprinkled upon it, consumes the flesh as well. These things were done according to the law in the victims that were burned upon the altar, where it was commanded that salt be offered in every victim and sacrifice. Every victim shall be salted with salt. For he is truly a victim of the Lord who [offers] body and soul, etc., as far as to deny those dear and near to us. Every victim shall be salted with salt. (HIER.) The victim is the human race, which here is salted with the salt of wisdom or of reason, while the corruption of blood, the keeper of rottenness and the mother of worms, is here consumed, and afterwards shall be tried by the fire of purgatory.

217AVERS. 49.-- But if the salt, etc. That is, if a man once refreshed with the seasonings of truth return to apostasy: by what other teacher is he corrected, who has spat out the wisdom he tasted, either terrified by adversities or enticed by prosperity? Whence: Who shall heal a charmer struck by a serpent? (Eccli. XII.) [Vulg. Eccli. 12:13 reads miserebitur, "shall have pity on"] In this it is believed that Judas and his fellows are signified, who, corrupted by love of money, did not hesitate both to lose his apostleship and to betray the Lord. But because there are many whom, while greater knowledge lifts them up, it severs from the fellowship of others, and the more they savour, the more they lose their savour of the virtue of concord. He subjoins: Have salt in you, and peace. (BEDA.) To have salt without peace is not the gift of virtue, but a proof of damnation. For the better a man savours, the worse he offends.

CAPUT X.

  • 217B

VERS. 1.-- Et inde exsurgens. Hucusque ea quae in Galilaea fecit et docuit: hinc narrat quae in Judaea fecit, et docuit, et passus est. Et primo quae trans Jordanem ad orientem: deinde quae cis Jordanem, quando venit Jericho, et Bethaniam, et Jerusalem. Cum enim omnis Judaeorum provincia generaliter ad distinctionem aliarum gentium Judaea dicta sit, specialius tamen meridiana plaga dicitur Judaea ad distinctionem Galilaeae Decapolis, et caeterarum in eadem provincia regionum.

VERS. 2.-- Pharisaei interrogabant. Magna distantia inter turbas et Pharisaeos. Hae conveniunt, ut doceantur, et infirmi curentur, sicut Matthaeus 217Caperte dicit: Illi ut tentando decipiant. Has enim devotio pietatis: illos adducit stimulus livoris. Si licet viro. (BEDA.) Cornuto syllogismo tentavit, etc., usque ad sed peccantium necessitate concessa est.

VERS. 5.-- Ad duritiam cordis. (ID.) Nunquid Deus contrarius sibi est, etc., usque ad per odia et homicidia perseverare nuptiarum copulam.

VERS. 8.-- Et erunt, etc. Praemium nuptiarum est e duobus unam carnem fieri. Castitas juncta spiritui unus spiritus efficitur.

VERS. 9.-- Homo non separet. Desiderio secundae uxoris. Deus separat: qui conjungit, quando ex consensu utriusque propter servitutem Dei (eo quod tempus breve sit), sic habent uxores quasi non habentes.

217DVERS. 10.-- Et in domo, etc. (HIER.) Non discipuli interrogaverunt prius, etc., usque ad ut panis cor hominis confirmet, ut est: Si quis vult post me venire (Luc. IX), etc.

VERS. 11.-- Quicunque dimiserit. (BEDA.) Matthaeus plenius: Quicunque dimiserit uxorem suam, nisi ob fornicationem, et aliam duxerit, maechatur (Matth. V). Una ergo solummodo causa est carnalis, fornicatio: una spiritualis, timor Dei, ut uxor dimittatur, sicut multi religionis causa fecisse leguntur. Nulla autem causa est Dei lege praescripta, ut alia ducatur vivente ea quae est relicta.

VERS. 13.-- Discipuli autem comminabantur offerentibus. Non quia nollent eis et manu et voce Salvatoris 218Abenedici, sed nondum plenam fidem habentes putabant eum more hominum offerentium importunitate lassari.

VERS. 14.-- Talium enim est regnum Dei. Talium significanter dicit, non istorum. Quasi dicat: Mores regnant, non aetas. His promittitur regnum coelorum qui similem habent simplicitatem et innocentiam, quibus congruit illud apostolis: Nolite effici parvuli sensibus, sed malitia parvuli estote (I Cor. XIV).

VERS. 15.-- Quisquis non receperit regnum. Qui non perseverat in ira, laesus non meminit, videns pulchram mulierem non concupiscit, non aliud habet in ore, aliud in corde. Quasi dicat: Si tales non fueritis, non intrabitis in regnum coelorum. Regnum Dei. Id est, 218Bdoctrinam Evangelii sicut parvuli accipere jubemur, id est, sicut parvulus doctoribus non contradicit, rationes adversus eos non componit, sed fideliter suscipit, cum metu obtemperat et quiescit: ita et nos obediendo simpliciter, et sine omni contradictione verba Domini debemus suscipere.

VERS. 17.-- Procurrens quidam genu. (BEDA.) Audierat, credo, iste a Domino tantum eos qui volunt parvulis esse similes introitu regni coelestis esse dignos, et ideo certior esse desiderat, non per parabolas, sed aperte: quibus operum meritis vitam aeternam consequi possit.

VERS. 18.-- Quid me dicis. (ID.) Quia magistrum vocaverat bonum, etc., usque ad sed absque Deo, nullum bonum esse testatur.

218CVERS. 19.-- Ne adulteres. (ID.) Haec est puerilis innocentiae castitas, quae nobis imitanda proponitur, si regnum Dei intrare velimus. Notandum sane, quod justitia legis suo tempore custodita, non solum bona temporalia, sed etiam vitam conferebat aeternam.

VERS. 20.-- Magister. (ID.) Non putandus est voto tentantis, etc., usque ad unde addit: Vade, quaecunque habes vende, et da pauperibus (Matth. V), etc.

VERS. 21.-- Et veni, sequere me. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Post contemptas divitias Salvatorem imitare, id est, relictis malis fac bona. Facilius saccus contemnitur quam concupiscentia vel voluptas.

VERS. 23.-- Quam difficile. (ID.) Qui multiplicandis divitiis incumbunt, etc., usque ad intrare possint 218Dregnum Dei.

VERS. 25.-- Facilius est camelum. Quomodo ergo in Evangelio Matthaeus et Zachaeus, et Joseph et in Veteri Testamento quam plurimi divites intravere? Forte quia divitias pro nihilo habuere vel ex toto contemnere didicerunt, unde David: Unicus et pauper sum ego (Psal. XXIV). Et idem: Divitiae si affluant, nolite cor apponere (Ibid. LXI). Non ait, nolite suscipere.

VERS. 26.-- Et quis potest. Cum plures sint pauperes quam divites, non hoc dixissent nisi in divitum numero intellexissent cunctos qui divitias adipisci vellent.

VERS. 27.-- Apud Deum. Qui a cupiditate terrenorum 219Aad charitatem aeternorum convertere potest, et superbos facere humiles.

VERS. 28.-- Ecce nos dimisimus. Grandis fiducia: piscator erat, dives non erat, victum manu et arte quaerebat, et tamen confidenter dicit: Ecce nos reliquimus omnia. Et secuti sumus. Quia non sufficit sic dimittere, jungit quod perfectum est, et secuti sumus te. Quasi dicat: Fecimus quod jussisti: quid ergo dabis praemii?

VERS. 30.-- Centies tantum. (BEDA.) Hac occasione quidam Judaicam mille annorum fabulam post resurrectionem justorum dogmatizant, etc., usque ad patriae coelestis, quae per dexteram significatur, omnium pariter electorum verissima dilectione fruuntur.

VERS. 31.-- Multi autem. Quia multi incipiunt sed non perficiunt, terribilis sententia subditur: 219BMulti autem, etc. Latro in cruce confessor eodem die quo pro peccatis suis crucifixus est, gratia fidei cum Christo in paradiso gaudet. Quotidie quoque videmus multos laicos magis vitae meritis excellere, et alios a prima aetate spirituali studio ferventes, ad extremum otio torpescere.

VERS. 32.-- Et stupebant. Ideo scilicet quia meminerant quod se passurum multa a summis sacerdotibus et scribis praedixerat, et occidendum. Metuebant ergo ne vel ipsi cum eo occiderentur, vel ille, cujus vita et magisterio gaudebant, occideretur.

Et assument. (BEDA.) Praevidens discipulorum animos ex passione sua perturbandos, etc., usque ad locum mortis quasi intrepidus adit.

219CVERS. 35.-- Et accedunt. Audita resurrectione, immemores secundi adventus putant eum more hominum regnare. Matthaeus dicit matrem postulasse, sed Marcus dicit ipsos, desiderium eorum volens aperire quorum consilio mater petebat.

VERS. 38.-- Aut baptismo. Idem quod calix. Unde alibi de passione sua dicit: Baptismo habeo baptizari: et quomodo coarctor usque dum perficiatur.

VERS. 39.-- Calicem quidem. (BED.) Jacobus ab Herode capite truncatur, Joannes in ferventis olei dolium missus. Inde ad coronam martyrii athleta Christi processit, statimque in Pathmos relegatus. Itaque et martyrio animo non defuit, et calicem confessionis bibit quem biberunt in camino tres pueri, licet persecutor sanguinem non fuderit.

219DVERS. 40.-- Sedere autem. Quasi, Vultis ut hoc tribuam vobis? Sed regnum coelorum non est dantis, sed accipientis. Non enim personarum acceptio est apud Deum (Act. X), sed quicunque dignus fuerit, accipiet quod non personae sed vitae paratum. Vobis. Adhuc superbis, quasi dicat: Si vultis accipere, nolite esse quod estis: aliis paratum est, et vos alii estote, et vobis paratum est, quod est, prius humiliamini qui jam vultis exaltari.

VERS. 41.-- Indignati sunt. Non de matre, sed de filiis, qui ignorantes mensuram suam immodica cupiditate ardebant.

VERS. 42.-- Jesus autem vocans eos. Humilis magister, et pius et mitis, nec cupiditatis immodicae 220Aduos arguit, nec decem indignationis increpat et livoris, sed docet majorem esse qui minor fuerit, et dominum qui servus sit. Frustra igitur, aut illi ad majora suspirant aut hi super desiderio eorum dolent, cum ad summitatem virtutum non potentia sed humilitas ducat. Suis denique proponit exemplum, ut si dicta parvipenderent, ad opera erubescerent. Nam et Filius hominis, etc.

VERS. 46.-- Et veniunt Jericho. (HIER.) Jericho luna vel anathema, quod congruit appropinquanti passioni. Defectus carnis Christi, praeparatio est coelestis Jerusalem, unde Jericho deserentes, Jerusalem, id est visioni pacis, appropinquant. Et proficiscente eo. (BEDA.) Quia ascendente ad coelos Domino et fidelibus sequentibus, etc., usque ad eosque ad 220Billuminationem omnium populorum in mundum dispersit. Bartimaeus caecus. (HIER.) Caecitas Judaeorum quae illustrabitur in fine in adventu Eliae et Enoch.
