VERS. 47.-- Qui cum audisset. Populus gentium audita fama Christi, cujus particeps quaerebat fieri, contradicebant multi, primo Judaei post etiam gentiles, ne illuminandus sanandusque mundus Christum invocaret, nec tamen ad vitam praeordinatos aeternam poterat impugnantium furor prohibere.

Fili David. (HIER.) Per merita patriarcharum illuminatur Judaicus populus, cui adest misericors Deus et miserator illuminans caecos et erigens elisos.

VERS. 48.-- At illo multo magis. Ingravescente bello vitiorum, manus levandae sunt ad lapidem adjutorii 220Ccum clamore, id est, ad Jesum.

(HIER.) Conveniens ordo salutis, etc., usque ad apostolos in altis considerans.

VERS. 49.-- Et vocant caecum. (BEDA.) Allegorice, caecum clamantem Dominus vocat, dum populo gentium scientiam veritatis desideranti, per sanctos praedicatores verbum fidei committit. Qui vocantes caecum animaequiorem esse et surgere, ad eumque venire, praecipiunt, cum praedicando spem salutis habere, de vitiorum torpore surgere, atque ad virtutum studia quibus illuminari mereatur, accingi jubent quasi dicat: Accedite ad eum et illuminamini (Psal. XXXIII). Et, surge qui dormis (Ephes. V), etc.

VERS. 50.-- Qui, projecto vestimento suo. Id est, 220Dabjectis mundi curis, expedito mentis gressu ad datorem lucis properat. Exsiliens. Promptam voluntatem completione desiderii remunerat, unde: Quodcunque petieritis in oratione credentes accipietis.

VERS. 51.-- Rabboni, et videam. Non aurum quaerit, sed lumen. Caecus enim divitias habere potest, sed non videt quod habet. Exemplo hujus non falsas divitias quaeramus, sed lucem quam cum solis angelis videre possumus ad quam via fides est. Unde illuminato caeco dicitur: Vade, fides tua salvum te fecit.

VERS. 52.-- Vidit et sequebatur. (BEDA.) Videt et sequitur qui quod bene intelligit operatur. Sequi, imitari. Unde: Si quis mihi ministrat, me sequatur (Joan. XII).

221AIn via. Consideremus qua via graditur, et sequamur per humilitatem, per labores. Via quae dicit: Ego sum via, veritas et vita (Joan. XIV). Haec est via angusta quae ducit ad ardua, Hierosolymae et Bethaniae ad montem Olivarum, qui est mons luminis et consolationis.

CHAPTER X.

  • 217B

VERS. 1.-- And rising up from thence. Thus far the things which he did and taught in Galilee: from here he relates what he did in Judea, and taught, and suffered. And first the things beyond the Jordan toward the east: then those on this side of the Jordan, when he came to Jericho, and to Bethany, and to Jerusalem. For although the whole province of the Jews is generally called Judea, to distinguish it from the other nations, yet more particularly the southern tract is called Judea, to distinguish it from Galilee, from the Decapolis, and from the other regions in the same province.

VERS. 2.-- The Pharisees asked. Great is the distance between the crowds and the Pharisees. These come together that they may be taught, and that the sick may be healed, as Matthew 217Cplainly says: those, that by tempting they may deceive. For the devotion of piety brings these: the goad of spite leads those. Whether it is lawful for a man to put away his wife. (BEDA.) He tempted him with a horned syllogism, etc., as far as but it was granted because of the necessity of those who sin.

VERS. 5.-- For the hardness of heart. (ID.) Is God contrary to himself, etc., as far as that the bond of wedlock should persevere through hatreds and murders.

VERS. 8.-- And they shall be, etc. The reward of marriage is that of two there be made one flesh. Chastity joined to the spirit is made one spirit.

VERS. 9.-- Let not man put asunder. By the desire of a second wife. God separates: he who joins, when by the consent of both, for the service of God (in that the time is short), they so have wives as though not having them.

217DVERS. 10.-- And in the house, etc. (HIER.) It was not the disciples who asked first, etc., as far as that bread may strengthen the heart of man, as it is: If any man will come after me (Luc. IX), etc.

VERS. 11.-- Whosoever shall put away his wife. (BEDA.) Matthew more fully: Whosoever shall put away his wife, save for fornication, and shall marry another, committeth adultery (Matth. V). There is therefore one carnal cause only, fornication: one spiritual, the fear of God, that a wife be put away, as many are read to have done for religion's sake. But no cause is prescribed by the law of God, that another be married while she lives who has been left.

VERS. 13.-- But the disciples rebuked them that brought them. Not because they were unwilling that these should be blessed by the Saviour's 218Ahand and voice, but, not yet having full faith, they thought that after the manner of men he was wearied by the importunity of those who brought them.

VERS. 14.-- For of such is the kingdom of God. He says "of such" with meaning, not "of these." As if he said: Manners reign, not age. The kingdom of heaven is promised to those who have the like simplicity and innocence, to whom that word to the apostles is fitting: Be not made little ones in understanding, but in malice be little ones (I Cor. XIV) [Vulg. 1 Cor. 14:20 nolite pueri effici sensibus, "be not children in understanding"].

VERS. 15.-- Whosoever shall not receive the kingdom. He who does not persevere in anger, who when hurt does not remember it, who seeing a fair woman does not covet, who has not one thing in his mouth and another in his heart. As if he said: If you shall not be such, you shall not enter into the kingdom of heaven. The kingdom of God. That is, 218Bwe are bidden to receive the teaching of the Gospel as little ones do, that is, as a little one does not contradict his teachers, does not frame arguments against them, but faithfully receives, obeys with fear and is quiet: so we also, obeying simply and without any contradiction, ought to receive the words of the Lord.

VERS. 17.-- A certain man running up, on bended knee. (BEDA.) He had heard, I believe, from the Lord that only those who wish to be like little ones are worthy of entrance into the heavenly kingdom, and therefore he desires to be more certain, not through parables but openly: by what merits of works he might be able to obtain eternal life.

VERS. 18.-- Why callest thou me good. (ID.) Because he had called him good master, etc., as far as but he testifies that apart from God there is no good.

218CVERS. 19.-- Thou shalt not commit adultery. (ID.) This is the chastity of childlike innocence, which is set before us to be imitated, if we would enter the kingdom of God. And it is to be well noted that the justice of the law, kept in its own time, conferred not only temporal goods but also eternal life.

VERS. 20.-- Master. (ID.) He is not to be thought [to have asked] with the intent of one tempting, etc., as far as whence he adds: Go, sell whatsoever thou hast, and give to the poor (Matth. V), etc.

VERS. 21.-- And come, follow me. (ID.) As if he said: After riches have been despised, imitate the Saviour, that is, evils being left behind, do good things. A money-bag is more easily despised than concupiscence or pleasure.

VERS. 23.-- How hardly. (ID.) They who apply themselves to the multiplying of riches, etc., as far as they may be able to enter 218Dthe kingdom of God.

VERS. 25.-- It is easier for a camel. How then in the Gospel did Matthew and Zacchaeus, and Joseph, and in the Old Testament very many rich men enter in? Perhaps because they held riches for nothing, or learned to despise them altogether, whence David: I am alone and poor (Psal. XXIV). And the same: If riches abound, set not your heart upon them (Ibid. LXI). He does not say, Do not receive them.

VERS. 26.-- And who can be saved. Since there are more poor than rich, they would not have said this unless they had understood in the number of the rich all who would wish to obtain riches.

VERS. 27.-- With God. Who is able to turn men from the greed of earthly things 219Ato the love of eternal things, and to make the proud humble.

VERS. 28.-- Behold, we have left all. Great confidence: he was a fisherman, he was not rich, he sought his living by hand and craft, and yet he says confidently: Behold, we have forsaken all things. And we have followed. Because it is not enough so to leave, he joins that which is perfect, and we have followed thee. As if he said: We have done what thou didst command: what reward therefore wilt thou give?

VERS. 30.-- A hundredfold. (BEDA.) On this occasion certain men lay down as doctrine the Jewish fable of the thousand years after the resurrection of the just, etc., as far as they enjoy the most true love of the heavenly fatherland, which is signified by the right hand, and of all the elect alike.

VERS. 31.-- But many. Because many begin but do not finish, a terrible sentence is subjoined: 219BBut many, etc. The thief on the cross, a confessor on the same day on which he was crucified for his own sins, by the grace of faith rejoices with Christ in paradise. Daily also we see many laymen excel rather by the merits of their life, and others, fervent from their earliest age in spiritual study, grow torpid at the last in idleness.

VERS. 32.-- And they were astonished. For this reason, namely, because they remembered that he had foretold that he should suffer many things from the chief priests and the scribes, and be slain. They feared therefore lest either they themselves should be slain with him, or he, in whose life and teaching they rejoiced, should be slain.

And they shall take. (BEDA.) Foreseeing that the minds of the disciples would be troubled by his passion, etc., as far as he approaches the place of death as one undaunted.

219CVERS. 35.-- And they come. Having heard of the resurrection, forgetful of the second coming, they think that he will reign after the manner of men. Matthew says that the mother made the request, but Mark says that they themselves did, wishing to lay open the desire of those by whose counsel the mother asked.

VERS. 38.-- Or with the baptism. The same as the cup. Whence elsewhere he says of his passion: I have a baptism wherewith to be baptized: and how am I straitened until it be accomplished.

VERS. 39.-- The cup indeed. (BED.) James is beheaded by Herod, John is cast into a cauldron of boiling oil. Thence the athlete of Christ went forth to the crown of martyrdom, and was at once banished to Patmos. And so he both was not wanting to martyrdom in mind, and drank the cup of confession which the three children drank in the furnace, although the persecutor did not shed his blood.

219DVERS. 40.-- But to sit. As if to say, Do you wish that I should grant this to you? But the kingdom of heaven is not the giver's but the receiver's. For there is no respect of persons with God (Act. X), but whosoever shall be worthy shall receive that which is prepared not for the person but for the life. To you. Still proud, as if he said: If you wish to receive, be not what you are: it is prepared for others, and be you others, and it is prepared for you, which is, be humbled first, you who already wish to be exalted.

VERS. 41.-- They were indignant. Not about the mother, but about the sons, who, not knowing their own measure, burned with immoderate greed.

VERS. 42.-- But Jesus calling them. A humble master, and kind and gentle, he neither reproves the two for immoderate greed 220Anor rebukes the ten for indignation and spite, but teaches that he is the greater who shall have been the less, and lord who is servant. In vain therefore do either those sigh after greater things or these grieve over their desire, since not power but humility leads to the summit of the virtues. Lastly he sets before his own an example, that if they made light of words, they might blush at deeds. For the Son of man also, etc.

VERS. 46.-- And they come to Jericho. (HIER.) Jericho is moon or anathema, which agrees with him drawing near to the passion. The failing of Christ's flesh is the preparation of the heavenly Jerusalem, whence, forsaking Jericho, they draw near to Jerusalem, that is, to the vision of peace. And as he went forth. (BEDA.) Because, the Lord ascending into the heavens and the faithful following, etc., as far as and he scattered them into the world for 220Bthe enlightening of all peoples. Blind Bartimaeus. (HIER.) The blindness of the Jews, which shall be enlightened at the end at the coming of Elias and Enoch.

VERS. 47.-- Who when he had heard. The people of the Gentiles, having heard the fame of Christ, of whom it sought to be made partaker, many contradicted, first the Jews and afterwards the Gentiles also, lest the world, which was to be enlightened and healed, should call upon Christ, nor yet could the fury of those who assailed it hinder those foreordained to eternal life.

Son of David. (HIER.) Through the merits of the patriarchs the Jewish people is enlightened, to whom God is present, merciful and compassionate, enlightening the blind and raising up them that are cast down.

VERS. 48.-- But he much the more. As the war of vices grows heavier, hands must be lifted up to the stone of help 220Cwith a cry, that is, to Jesus.

(HIER.) A fitting order of salvation, etc., as far as beholding the apostles on high.

VERS. 49.-- And they call the blind man. (BEDA.) Allegorically, the Lord calls the blind man crying out, while to the people of the Gentiles, desiring the knowledge of the truth, he commits the word of faith by holy preachers. These, calling the blind man, bid him be of better courage and rise and come to him, when by preaching they command him to have hope of salvation, to rise from the torpor of vices, and to gird himself to those pursuits of the virtues by which he may deserve to be enlightened, as if he said: Come ye to him and be enlightened (Psal. XXXIII). And, rise thou that sleepest (Ephes. V), etc.

VERS. 50.-- Who, casting away his garment. That is, 220Dthe cares of the world being cast off, with the step of his mind unhindered he hastens to the giver of light. Leaping up. He rewards a ready will with the fulfilling of its desire, whence: Whatsoever you shall ask in prayer, believing, you shall receive.

VERS. 51.-- Rabboni, and let me see. He does not seek gold, but light. For a blind man can have riches, but he does not see what he has. By his example let us seek not false riches, but the light which we can see with the angels alone, to which the way is faith. Whence it is said to the blind man when enlightened: Go, thy faith hath made thee whole.

VERS. 52.-- He saw and followed. (BEDA.) He sees and follows who does that which he well understands. To follow is to imitate. Whence: If any man minister to me, let him follow me (Joan. XII).

221AIn the way. Let us consider by what way he walks, and let us follow through humility, through labours. The way which says: I am the way, the truth and the life (Joan. XIV). This is the narrow way which leads to the steep places, of Jerusalem and of Bethany to the mount of Olives, which is the mount of light and of consolation.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Bethaniae. (BEDA.) Bethania villa sive civitas in latere montis Oliveti, etc., usque ad duos propter veritatis scientiam et operis munditiam. Duos ex discipulis. (HIER.) Bini vocantur, bini mittuntur, etc., usque ad inter duo cherubim Dominum cognoscant, spiritu mente psallentes.

VERS. 2.-- Et statim. (BEDA.) Introeuntes mundum praedicatores invenerunt populum nationum, etc., 221Busque ad qui autem mundus et sanctus est, solius Dei est.

VERS. 4.-- Pullum ligatum. (HIER.) Pullus ligatus et indomitus, quem solvunt et domant, populus gentilis est. Ante januam fidei, cum vinculis peccatorum suorum in bivio stat: in libertate arbitrii dubitat inter mortem et vitam.

VERS. 6.-- Et dimiserunt. (BEDA.) Qui in solvendo contradicebant, etc., usque ad quo Christo sessore fiant digna operiunt.

VERS. 7.-- Et sedit. (HIER.) Coepit regnare ut non regnet peccatum in mortali vel lasciva carne: sed justitia et pax et gaudium in Spiritu sancto.

VERS. 8.-- Multi autem straverunt. Quia multi martyres se propriae carnis amictu exuentes, simplicioribus 221Cviam suo sanguine parant, ut inoffensi ad supernam civitatem, ad quam ducit Jesus, incedant.

(HIER.) Multi, etc. Pedes sunt extremi quos ad jungendum constituit Apostolus, qui etsi non sunt dorsum in quo sedit Dominus, tamen cum militibus a Joanne instruuntur.

Alii autem. (ID.) Justi ut palma florebunt, angusti in radicibus, lati in floribus et fructibus: quoniam bonus odor Christi sunt, et sternunt viam mandatorum Dei bona fama. (BEDA.) Alii autem frondes vel ramos de arboribus caedunt, qui in doctrina veritatis verba et sententias Patrum de eorum excerpunt libris. Et haec in via Dei ad animum auditoris venientis humili praedicatione submittunt.

VERS. 9.-- Et qui praeibant. Praecessit Judaicus 221Dpopulus, secutus est gentilis. Et quia omnes qui fideles sunt vel fuerunt in Christum crediderunt et credunt, et qui praeeunt, et qui sequuntur, hosanna clamant: quod Salva nos, Latine dicitur: ab ipso enim omnes priores etiam salutem quaesierunt.

VERS. 10.-- Benedictus qui venit. Quoniam una fides, una spes omnium. Illi exspectabant et venturum credebant, et nos venisse credimus.

(BEDA.) Per hoc quod jungitur: hosanna in excelsis: quod est salus, significat quod adventum Christi non solum hominum salus est, sed totius mundi terrena jungens coelestibus, ut ei omne genu flectatur, coelestium, terrestrium et infernorum (Philip. II).

(HIER.) Hoc Gabrieli consonat: qui ait: Hic erit 222Amagnus, et filius Altissimi vocabitur, etc., usque ad per ipsum verbum obsecrationis.

VERS. 11.-- Et introivit. (ID.) Jam appropinquante passione in loco passioni praefinito ante saecula vult esse, etc., usque ad jam sacramentum beatae immolationis inchoavit.

In templum. Exemplum dedit nobis, ut quocunque veniremus, primum ad domum orationis si ibi sit divertamus: et cum nos per orationis studium Deo commendaverimus, ad ea propter quae venimus agenda secedamus. Et circumspectis, etc. Non semel hoc fecit, sed per omnes quinque dies. Per diem in templo docebat, noctibus exiens in monte Oliveti morabatur, sicut Lucas ait. Docendo enim incredulis officium correctionis sedulus impendebat, manendo 222Bapud fideles gratiam benignitatis propitius exhibebat.

Cumque vidisset. (BEDA.) Sicut per parabolas loquitur, ita facit, etc., usque ad miracula faciendo, et non invenit: ideo damnavit.

VERS. 14.-- Jam non amplius. (ID.) Tu quoque si non vis audire in judicio a Christo, Discedite a me, mali, in ignem aeternum, quia esurivi et non dedistis mihi, manducandum (Matth. XXV), arbor sterilis esse caveto, sed potius Christo pauperi et esurienti fructum pietatis quo indiget offer.

VERS. 15.-- Coepit ejicere. In ipsa re ostendit quod per figuram in ficu fecit. Ficus enim non peccavit si ante tempus fructum non habuit, sed sacerdotes.

Vendentes. (BEDA.) Ea scilicet quae qui de longinquo 222Cvenerunt ab indigenis offerenda emebant, etc., usque ad semper privantur sacerdotio.

VERS. 16.-- Et non sinebat. Futuri judicii exemplum praemittit, quando omnes reprobos ab Ecclesia repellit: et ne ultra ad eam turbandam intrent aeterno verbere compescit.

Vos autem fecistis. Ad hoc enim in templo erant, ut vel non dantes corporaliter persequerentur, vel dantes spiritualiter necarent. Templum et domus Dei mens est et conscientia fidelium, quae in laesione proximi perversas cogitationes profert, quasi in spelunca latrones resident, et simpliciter gradientes interficiunt: et sic mens jam non domus Dei, sed spelunca est latronum.

VERS. 20.-- Aridam factam. (BEDA.) A radicibus 222Darefacta est ficus, ut ostenderetur gens impia non ad tempus, vel ex parte corripienda externorum incursibus, et per poenitentiam liberanda, sicut saepe factum est, sed omni et aeterna damnatione ferienda. Aliter. Arefacta est a radicibus, ut ostendatur non solum humano extrinsecus, sed divino intus favore funditus destituenda: nam et vitam perdidit in coelis et patriam in terris.

VERS. 21.-- Recordatus Petrus. Petrus agnoscit aridam, et abscissam radicem cui succedit oliva pulcherrima fructifera vocata a Domino. Unde sequitur: Amen, dico vobis, etc.

VERS. 23.-- Quicunque dixerit. (BEDA.) Solent quidam dicere, quod nostri nondum plenam fidem habuerunt, 223Aetc., usque ad quanto a piorum laesione se expulsum gemit. (HIER.) Sed hoc factum est: quando dixerunt apostoli: Digne transferimur ad gentes, quia vos indignos judicatis (Matth. XXI). (BEDA.) Notanda est distinctio deprecantium, etc., usque ad si tamen in se peccantibus primo dimittunt.

VERS. 25.-- Et cum stabitis. (HIER.) Marcus suo more, septem versus orationis Dominicae una oratione comprehendit. Is namque cui dimissa sunt omnia, quid amplius rogabit, nisi quod perseveret in eo quod obtinuit?

VERS. 28.-- In qua potestate. De Dei dubitant potestate, et volunt subintelligi diaboli esse quod facit.

VERS. 29.-- Interrogabo. Poterat aperta responsione calumniam tentatorum confutare: sed prudenter 223Binterrogat, ut vel silentio suo vel sententia condemnentur.

VERS. 31.-- Si dixerimus. (HIER.) De lucerna mundi obscurantur, unde dicit: Paravi lucernam Christo meo: inimicos ejus induam confusione (Psal. CXXXI).

Quare ergo, etc. Quasi dicat: Quem confitemini de coelo habuisse prophetiam, mihi testimonium perhibuit et ab illo audistis in qua potestate haec facio.

VERS. 32.-- Si dixerimus. Quodlibet horum respondeant: vident se in laqueum ruituros, timent lapidationem: sed magis confessionem veritatis.

VERS. 33.-- Neque ego dico vobis. (BED.) Duabus de causis scientia veritatis occultatur quaerentibus, cum aut hic cui quaerit minus intelligit, aut odio aut contemptu veritatis indignus est qui debeat aperiri. 223CPropter alterum dicitur: Adhuc multa habeo vobis dicere, quae non potestis portare modo. Propter alterum, Nolite sanctum dare canibus.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- Of Bethany. (BEDA.) Bethany is a village or city on the side of Mount Olivet, etc., as far as two, for the knowledge of the truth and the cleanness of the work. Two of the disciples. (HIER.) Two by two they are called, two by two they are sent, etc., as far as they may know the Lord between the two cherubim, singing with the spirit, with the mind.

VERS. 2.-- And straightway. (BEDA.) The preachers, entering the world, found the people of the nations, etc., 221Bas far as but that which is clean and holy belongs to God alone.

VERS. 4.-- A colt tied. (HIER.) The colt tied and untamed, which they loose and tame, is the Gentile people. Before the door of faith it stands with the bonds of its own sins where two ways meet: in the freedom of choice it wavers between death and life.

VERS. 6.-- And they let them go. (BEDA.) They who at the loosing spoke against it, etc., as far as they cover them with that whereby, with Christ as their rider, they may be made worthy.

VERS. 7.-- And he sat. (HIER.) He began to reign, that sin might not reign in mortal or in wanton flesh: but justice and peace and joy in the Holy Ghost.

VERS. 8.-- But many spread their garments. Because many martyrs, stripping themselves of the covering of their own flesh, prepare a way with their blood for the simpler sort, 221Cthat these may go unhindered to the city on high, to which Jesus leads.

(HIER.) Many, etc. The feet are the outermost members, which the Apostle appointed for the joining together, which, although they are not the back on which the Lord sat, yet are instructed along with the soldiers by John.

And others. (ID.) The just shall flourish as the palm tree, narrow in the roots, broad in flowers and fruits: since they are the good odour of Christ, and they strew the way of the commandments of God with good report. (BEDA.) And others cut down leaves or branches from the trees, who in the teaching of the truth cull out the words and sentences of the Fathers from their books. And these they lay down in the way of God, by humble preaching, before the mind of the hearer as he comes.

VERS. 9.-- And they that went before. The Jewish 221Dpeople went before, the Gentile followed. And because all who are or have been faithful believed and believe in Christ, both they who go before and they who follow cry hosanna: which in Latin is rendered, Save us: for from him also all the former sought salvation.

VERS. 10.-- Blessed is he that cometh. Since there is one faith, one hope of all. They looked for him and believed that he would come, and we believe that he has come.

(BEDA.) By this which is joined to it: hosanna in the highest: which is salvation, it signifies that the coming of Christ is the salvation not of men only, but of the whole world, joining earthly things to heavenly, that to him every knee should bow, of those that are in heaven, on earth and under the earth (Philip. II).

(HIER.) This is in accord with Gabriel: who says: He shall be 222Agreat, and shall be called the Son of the Most High, etc., as far as through the word itself of entreaty.

VERS. 11.-- And he entered in. (ID.) Now, the passion drawing near, he wills to be in the place appointed for the passion before the ages, etc., as far as he has already begun the sacrament of the blessed immolation.

Into the temple. He gave us an example, that wheresoever we should come, we should first turn aside to the house of prayer if there be one there: and when by the practice of prayer we have commended ourselves to God, we should withdraw to do those things for which we came. And having looked about, etc. He did this not once, but through all the five days. By day he taught in the temple, by night, going out, he abode on Mount Olivet, as Luke says. For by teaching he diligently bestowed on the unbelieving the office of correction, by abiding 222Bamong the faithful he graciously showed forth the grace of kindness.

And when he had seen. (BEDA.) As he speaks by parables, so he does, etc., as far as by working miracles, and he found none: therefore he condemned it.

VERS. 14.-- Now no more. (ID.) Thou also, if thou wilt not hear in the judgment from Christ, Depart from me, ye evil ones, into everlasting fire, for I was hungry and you gave not to me, to eat (Matth. XXV) [Vulg. Matt. 25:41 maledicti, "ye cursed"], beware to be a barren tree, but rather offer to Christ, poor and hungry, the fruit of piety which he lacks.

VERS. 15.-- He began to cast out. In the thing itself he shows what he did by a figure in the fig tree. For the fig tree did not sin if before the time it had no fruit, but the priests did.

Them that sold. (BEDA.) Those things, namely, which they who came from 222Cafar bought from the natives to be offered, etc., as far as they are always deprived of the priesthood.

VERS. 16.-- And he suffered not. He sends before an example of the judgment to come, when he drives all the reprobate from the Church: and lest they enter any more to trouble her, he restrains them with an everlasting scourging.

But you have made. For to this end were they in the temple, that they might either bodily persecute those who did not give, or spiritually slay those who did give. The temple and house of God is the mind and conscience of the faithful, which in the hurting of a neighbour brings forth perverse thoughts, as robbers sit in a den, and slay those who walk in simplicity: and so the mind is now not the house of God, but a den of thieves.

VERS. 20.-- Withered away. (BEDA.) The fig tree was withered from 222Dthe roots, that it might be shown that the impious nation was not to be chastised for a time, or in part, by the inroads of foreigners, and to be set free through penance, as has often been done, but to be smitten with an entire and everlasting damnation. Otherwise. It was withered from the roots, that it might be shown to be utterly forsaken not only of human favour from without, but of divine favour within: for it lost both life in heaven and country on earth.

VERS. 21.-- Peter remembering. Peter acknowledges the tree withered, and the root cut off, to which succeeds the most beautiful fruitful olive, so called by the Lord. Whence there follows: Amen, I say to you, etc.

VERS. 23.-- Whosoever shall say. (BEDA.) Certain men are wont to say that our own had not yet full faith, 223Aetc., as far as by how much he groans that he has been cast out from the hurting of the godly. (HIER.) But this was done: when the apostles said: Worthily are we transferred to the Gentiles, because you judge yourselves unworthy (Matth. XXI). (BEDA.) The distinction of those who pray is to be noted, etc., as far as if yet they first forgive those who sin against them.

VERS. 25.-- And when you shall stand. (HIER.) Mark after his manner comprises the seven petitions of the Lord's Prayer in one prayer. For he to whom all things are forgiven, what more shall he ask, save that he persevere in that which he has obtained?

VERS. 28.-- By what authority. They doubt of the power of God, and wish it to be understood underneath that what he does is the devil's.

VERS. 29.-- I will ask. He could have confuted the calumny of the tempters by an open answer: but he prudently 223Basks, that they may be condemned either by their own silence or by their own sentence.

VERS. 31.-- If we shall say. (HIER.) By the lamp of the world they are darkened, whence he says: I have prepared a lamp for my Christ: his enemies I will clothe with confusion (Psal. CXXXI).

Why then, etc. As if he said: He whom you confess to have had his prophecy from heaven bore witness to me, and from him you have heard by what authority I do these things.

VERS. 32.-- If we shall say. Whichever of these they answer: they see that they will fall into a snare, they fear stoning: but more the confession of the truth.

VERS. 33.-- Neither do I tell you. (BED.) For two causes the knowledge of the truth is hidden from those who seek it, when either he to whom he seeks understands too little, or by hatred or by contempt of the truth he is unworthy that it should be opened to him. 223CFor the one it is said: I have yet many things to say to you, which you cannot bear now. [Vulg. Jo. 16:12 sed non potestis portare modo, "but you cannot bear them now"] For the other, Give not that which is holy to dogs.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- Lacum, sive torcular aut altare, aut illa torcularia quorum titulo quidam psalmi praenotantur. Peregre profectus. Dans liberum arbitrium operandi non mutatione loci, quia ubique Deus, et omnia complet.

VERS. 6.-- Quia reverebuntur. Non hoc ignorando dixit, quia omnia novit, sed semper ambigere dicitur Deus, ut libera voluntas homini reservetur.

(BEDA.) Interrogamus Arium, et Eunomium, ecce pater dicitur ignorare, et sententiam temperat, et quantum in nobis est dicitur esse mentitus. Quidquid 223Dpro patre responderint, hoc intelligant pro filio, qui se dicit ignorare consummationis diem.

VERS. 7.-- Coloni autem. (ID.) Probat Judaeorum principes non per ignorantiam Christum crucifixisse, etc., usque ad et suam justitiam quae ex lege est, praeferre nitebantur.

VERS. 8.-- Et ejecerunt. Notat pertinaciam eorum: qui nec crucifixo ac resuscitato Domino ad praedicationem apostolorum credere voluerunt, sed quasi vile cadaver projecerunt: quia quantum in se erat, a suis finibus excludentes gentibus suscipiendum dederunt.

(BEDA.) Cuicunque fidelium mysterium baptismi, etc., usque ad Dominum crucifigere et ostentatui habere gaudebit.

224AVERS. 10.-- Lapidem quem reprobaverunt. (HIER.) Lapis reprobus, quem gessit angulus, conjungens in coena agnum cum pane, finiens Vetus, Novum inchoans Testamentum, hic praestat mira in oculis nostris.

VERS. 13.-- Et mittunt ad eum. (BEDA.) Turbam timebant, atque quod per se non poterant, etc., usque ad et regnum et omnia perdere maluerint, quam esse tributarii.

VERS. 14.-- Magister, scimus. (ID.) Blanda et fraudulenta interrogatio ad hoc provocat respondentem, ut magis Deum quam Caesarem timeat, et dicat non debere tributa solvi, ut audientes Herodiani seditionis contra Romanos auctorem teneant.

VERS. 15.-- Qui sciens versutiam. Qui putant interrogationem Salvatoris ignorationem esse, non dispensationem, 224Bex hoc loco discant, quod potuit scire cujus imago esset. Sed interrogat, ut ad sermonem eorum competenter respondeat. Denarium. Denarius genus nummi qui pro decem nummis computatur, et habet imaginem Caesaris.

VERS. 17.-- Reddite ergo quae sunt Caesaris. Hoc ipse fecit: pro se et pro Petro solvendo tributa. Quae sunt Dei Deo. Hoc quoque fecit Patris implens voluntatem. Aliter: Reddite quae sunt Caesaris Caesari. Impressionem suae imaginis. Quae sunt Dei Deo. Animam lumine vultus ejus illustratam, Unde: Signatum est super nos lumen vultus tui (Psal. IV), etc.

VERS. 18.-- Sadducaei, etc. Duae haereses erant in Judaeis, Pharisaeorum et Sadducaeorum. Pharisaei traditionum et observationum (quas illi δευτερώσεις 224Cvocant) justitiam praeferebant: unde et divisi vocantur a populo. Sadducaei autem, id est, justi, et corporis et animae resurrectionem negabant.

Sadducaei, etc. (BEDA.) Qui resurrectionem corporum esse negant, vel non credunt animas judicantes interire cum corporibus; recte hujusmodi fabulam fingunt, quae deliramenti arguat eos qui asserunt resurrectionem corporum. Potuit autem et in gente eorum aliquando hoc accidere.

VERS. 20.-- Septem ergo, etc. (ID.) Turpitudinem fabulae opponunt, ut resurrectionem negent. Sed mystice, septem fratres sine filiis defuncti, omnibus reprobis congruunt: qui per totam vitam (quae septem diebus volvitur) a bono opere steriles sunt, quibus viritim morientibus ad ultimum et ipsa mundana 224Dconversatio moritur, id est, transit, quasi uxor infecunda, quam illi sine fructu boni operis exegerunt. HIER. Mulier sterilis, nec relinquens semen ex septem fratribus, etc., usque ad quia initium sapientiae timor Domini.

VERS. 25.-- Neque nubent, neque, etc. (BEDA.) Graeco idiomati Latina consuetudo non respondet: nubere enim proprie mulieres dicuntur: et viri ducere. Sed nos simpliciter dictum intelligamus, neque nubent viri, neque nubentur mulieres.

VERS. 26.-- Ego sum Deus, etc. (BED.) Cum multa apertiora testimonia de resurrectione posset proferre, etc., usque ad quae cum animabus vel bona vel mala gesserunt.

225AVERS. 28.-- Interrogavit eum. (HIER.) Quid sibi vult haec quaestio, cum hoc sciant omnes periti in lege? Sed diverse in Exodo et Levitico et Deuteronomio ordinantur mandata. De his enim duobus uberibus super pectora sponsae levatis alitur nostra infantia.

Primum omnium, etc. Maximum quod ante omnia debemus in corde, quasi unicum pietatis fundamentum locare, hoc est, scilicet cognitio atque confessio divinae unitatis cum exsecutione divinae operationis, quae in dilectione Dei et proximi perficitur: haec est fides quae per dilectionem operatur.

VERS. 32.-- Bene magister. (BEDA.) Ostendit scriba in hac responsione inter scribas et Pharisaeos gravem quaestionem diu versatam esse, etc., usque ad nemo autem absque fide et dilectione in qua sententia 225Bscriba iste declarat se fuisse.

VERS. 34.-- Non longe es a regno Dei, etc. (ISID.) Quamvis ad tentandum venerit: longior namque est ignorantia quam scientia, ut Sadducaeis dicitur: Erratis nescientes Scripturas neque virtutem Dei (Matth. XXII). (BEDA.) Matthaeus dicit, quia tentando quaerebat, etc., usque ad secundum illud: Qui facile credit levis est corde, et minorabitur (Eccl. XIX).

Et nemo. Quia in sermonibus confutantur, ultra non interrogant; sed aperte comprehensum Romanae potestati tradunt, unde patet venena invidiae superari posse, sed difficile quiescere.

VERS. 36.-- Sede a dextris. Nam in hoc infirmitatem filii, sed quia alter in altero operatur ostendit. Nam et filius subjicit inimicos Patri, et Deum Patrem 225Cglorificat super terram.

VERS. 38.-- Cavete. (BEDA.) Nota, quia non vetat eos qui hujus officii sunt, etc., usque ad simulatione seducamur, vel aemulatione ad idem inflammemur.

VERS. 40.-- Qui devorant domos viduarum. Quasi patroni in judicio futuri, ab infirmis et peccatorum conscientia turbatis pecuniam accipere non dubitant: cum commendet Deo orationem manus porrecta ad dandum, non collecta ad accipiendum: quibus illud congruit: Oratio ejus fiat in peccatum.

VERS. 41.-- Gazophylacium. (BEDA.) Φυλάττειν Graece, servare Latine. Gaza, Persice, divitiae, Gazophylacium locus quo divitiae servantur, et arca, scilicet, in qua populi donaria congregabantur ad usus templi. Unde in libro Regnum: Tulit Joiada pontifex 225Dgazophylacium unum, aperuitque foramen desuper (IV Reg. XII), etc., et porticus in quibus servabantur. Unde: Haec verba locutus est Jesus in gazophylacio, docens in templo.

Aspiciebat quomodo. Sicut appetitores primatus et vanae gloriae cavendos esse dixit, et simulatione orantibus prolixius judicium praedixit, sic etiam offerentes justo examine discernit, ut retribuat singulis, secundum cor et opera, quod et hodie facit in Ecclesia.

Divites. (BEDA.) Divites, Judaei de justitia legis elati, etc., usque ad cunctis superborum Judaeorum operibus, praestant.

VERS. 42.-- Vidua pauper. (HIER.) Haec paupercula me et similes signat, qui mitto quod possum 226Aet desidero quod non possum vobis explanare. Non quantum, sed ex quanto desiderat Deus. Unusquisque quadrantem potest offerre. Haec est torta panis in Levitico, quae est voluntas prompta. Quadrans, quia ex tribus consistit, cogitatu, verbo et facto.

Quadrans. Quadrantem vocant calculatores quartam partem cujusque rei, scilicet, loci, temporis, pecuniae. Forsitan ergo hic quartam partem sicli, id est, quinque obolos significat.

VERS. 44.-- Omnes enim. (BEDA.) Judaeus ex abundante mittit in munera, etc., usque ad Deus meus misericordia ejus praeveniet me (Psal. LVIII).

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- A vat, that is, a winepress or an altar, or those winepresses by whose title certain psalms are inscribed. Having gone into a far country. Giving free choice of working, not by a change of place, because God is everywhere, and fills all things.

VERS. 6.-- Because they will reverence. He said this not in ignorance, because he knows all things, but God is always said to be in doubt, that free will may be reserved to man.

(BEDA.) We put the question to Arius and Eunomius, behold, the father is said to be ignorant, and he tempers his sentence, and, so far as is in us, he is said to have lied. Whatever 223Dthey shall answer for the father, let them understand the same for the Son, who says that he does not know the day of the consummation.

VERS. 7.-- But the husbandmen. (ID.) He proves that the princes of the Jews crucified Christ not through ignorance, etc., as far as and they strove to prefer their own justice which is of the law.

VERS. 8.-- And they cast him out. He marks their obstinacy: who, not even with the Lord crucified and raised again, were willing to believe at the preaching of the apostles, but cast him forth like a worthless corpse: because, so far as in them lay, shutting him out from their own borders they gave him to the Gentiles to be received.

(BEDA.) To whichever of the faithful the mystery of baptism, etc., as far as he will rejoice to crucify the Lord and to hold him up for a show.

224AVERS. 10.-- The stone which they rejected. (HIER.) The rejected stone, which the corner bore, joining at the supper the lamb with the bread, ending the Old and beginning the New Testament, this shows forth wonders in our eyes.

VERS. 13.-- And they send to him. (BEDA.) They feared the crowd, and what they could not do of themselves, etc., as far as they would rather lose both the kingdom and all things than be tributaries.

VERS. 14.-- Master, we know. (ID.) The smooth and deceitful question provokes him who answers to this, that he should fear God rather than Caesar, and should say that tributes ought not to be paid, so that the Herodians, hearing it, might hold him the author of sedition against the Romans.

VERS. 15.-- Who knowing their craftiness. Let those who think that the Saviour's questioning is ignorance and not dispensation 224Blearn from this place that he could know whose image it was. But he asks, that he may fittingly answer their speech. A penny. The denarius is a kind of coin which is reckoned for ten coins, and it has the image of Caesar.

VERS. 17.-- Render therefore the things that are Caesar's. This he himself did: paying tribute for himself and for Peter. The things that are God's, to God. This also he did, fulfilling the will of the Father. Otherwise: Render the things that are Caesar's to Caesar. The impress of his own image. The things that are God's, to God. The soul enlightened by the light of his countenance, Whence: The light of thy countenance is signed upon us (Psal. IV), etc.

VERS. 18.-- The Sadducees, etc. There were two heresies among the Jews, that of the Pharisees and that of the Sadducees. The Pharisees set forth the justice of traditions and observances (which they 224Ccall δευτερώσεις): whence they are also called the divided, apart from the people. But the Sadducees, that is, the just, denied the resurrection both of the body and of the soul.

The Sadducees, etc. (BEDA.) They who deny that there is a resurrection of bodies, or who do not believe it, judging that souls perish together with the bodies; rightly do they frame a fable of this kind, which may convict of raving those who assert the resurrection of bodies. But this could also have befallen at some time in their own nation.

VERS. 20.-- Seven therefore, etc. (ID.) They set against him the baseness of a fable, that they may deny the resurrection. But mystically, the seven brothers dead without children agree with all the reprobate: who through their whole life (which is rolled round in seven days) are barren of good work, and when these die one by one, at the last the very worldly 224Dconversation dies also, that is, passes away, like a barren wife, whom they wore out without the fruit of a good work. HIER. The barren woman, leaving no seed from the seven brothers, etc., as far as because the fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom.

VERS. 25.-- Neither shall they marry, neither, etc. (BEDA.) The Latin usage does not answer to the Greek idiom: for it is properly of women that marrying is said: and of men, the taking of a wife. But let us understand it as simply said, neither shall they marry, the men, neither shall they be married, the women.

VERS. 26.-- I am the God, etc. (BED.) Although he could bring forward many plainer testimonies concerning the resurrection, etc., as far as the things which, whether good or evil, they wrought together with their souls.

225AVERS. 28.-- He asked him. (HIER.) What does this question mean, since all who are skilled in the law know it? But the commandments are ordered differently in Exodus and in Leviticus and in Deuteronomy. For our infancy is nourished from these two breasts raised upon the bosom of the bride.

The first of all, etc. The greatest thing, which before all we ought to set in the heart as the one foundation of piety, is this, namely, the knowledge and confession of the divine unity together with the carrying out of the divine working, which is perfected in the love of God and of the neighbour: this is the faith which worketh by love.

VERS. 32.-- Well, master. (BEDA.) The scribe shows in this answer that a weighty question had long been debated among the scribes and the Pharisees, etc., as far as but no one without faith and love, in which judgment this 225Bscribe declares himself to have been.

VERS. 34.-- Thou art not far from the kingdom of God, etc. (ISID.) Although he had come to tempt: for ignorance is farther off than knowledge, as it is said to the Sadducees: You err, not knowing the Scriptures nor the power of God (Matth. XXII). (BEDA.) Matthew says that he asked by way of tempting, etc., as far as according to that word: He that believeth easily is light of heart, and shall be made less (Eccl. XIX).

And no man. Because they are confuted in words, they question him no more; but, having openly seized him, they deliver him to the Roman power, whence it appears that the poisons of envy can be overcome, but hardly be quiet.

VERS. 36.-- Sit on my right hand. For in this he shows the weakness [infirmitatem; read non infirmitatem, "not the weakness"] of the Son, but that the one works in the other. For the Son both subjects his enemies to the Father, and glorifies God the Father 225Cupon earth.

VERS. 38.-- Beware. (BEDA.) Note that he does not forbid those who are of this office, etc., as far as we be led astray by their pretence, or be inflamed by emulation to the same.

VERS. 40.-- Who devour the houses of widows. As men who are to be patrons in the judgment, they do not hesitate to take money from the weak and from those troubled by the conscience of their sins: whereas it is the hand stretched out to give, and not closed to receive, that commends prayer to God: to whom that word is fitting: Let his prayer be turned to sin.

VERS. 41.-- The treasury. (BEDA.) Φυλάττειν in Greek, to keep in Latin. Gaza, in Persian, riches, Gazophylacium the place in which riches are kept, and the chest, namely, in which the offerings of the people were gathered for the uses of the temple. Whence in the book of Kings: Joiada the high priest took 225Done chest, and opened a hole in the top (IV Reg. XII), etc., and the porches in which they were kept. Whence: These words Jesus spoke in the treasury, teaching in the temple.

He beheld how. As he said that those who seek after primacy and vainglory are to be avoided, and foretold a more grievous judgment to those who pray in pretence, so also he discerns those who offer with a just examination, that he may render to each according to the heart and the works, which he does also today in the Church.

The rich. (BEDA.) The rich, the Jews puffed up over the justice of the law, etc., as far as they surpass all the works of the proud Jews.

VERS. 42.-- A poor widow. (HIER.) This poor little woman signifies me and such as me, who cast in what I can 226Aand desire what I cannot to expound to you. God desires not how much, but out of how much. Every one can offer a farthing. This is the twist of bread in Leviticus, which is a ready will. A farthing, because it consists of three, in thought, in word and in deed.

A farthing. Reckoners call a farthing the fourth part of any thing, namely, of place, of time, of money. Perhaps therefore here it signifies the fourth part of a sicle, that is, five oboli.

VERS. 44.-- For all. (BEDA.) The Jew casts into the gifts out of that which abounds to him, etc., as far as My God, his mercy shall prevent me (Psal. LVIII).

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Et cum egrederetur. (BEDA.) Recedent de templo Domino, etc., usque ad erat luitura, et 226Baedificia ruitura.

Vides has, etc. Divinitus autem procuratum est, ut, patefacta per orbem fidei evangelicae gratia, ipsum templum cum caeremoniis tolleretur: ne forte aliquis parvulus adhuc, ac lactens in fide, si videret manere illa a prophetis facta a Domino instituta admirando sanctum saeculare, paulatim ad Judaismum relaberetur. Aufertur ergo umbra, et palmam tenet veritas per orbem declarata.

(HIER.) Praenuntiat cladem novissimi temporis, id est destructionem templi cum plebe et littera sua. De qua: Lapis super lapidem non relinquetur (Luc. XXI): id est, testimonia prophetarum super eos in quos Judaei retorquebant ea ut in Esdram, in Zorobabel, in Machabaeos.

226CVERS. 3.-- Et cum, etc. (BED.) In sanctis quietus manet: dum superborum detestatur amentiam. Mons enim Olivarum fructiferam Ecclesiae celsitudinem significat: mons ille non infructuosas arbores, sed oliveta gignit, quibus lumen alitur, requies lassis tribuitur, infirmitas solvitur.

VERS. 5.-- Et respondens. (ID.) A tempore Dominicae passionis in populo Judaeorum qui latronem seditiosum elegerunt, Christum Salvatorem abjecerunt, nec bella hostium nec seditiones civium cessaverunt; sed apostoli ne his adventantibus terreantur, et ne Hierosolymam Judaeamque deserant, admonentur, quia non statim finis. In quadragesimum enim annum desolatio provinciae et ultimum urbis ac templi excidium prolatum est.

226DVERS. 6.-- Multi enim venient. Imminente excidio multi venerunt qui se esse Christos, et jam tempus libertatis adesse mentirentur. Multi etiam in Ecclesia temporibus apostolorum inter caetera diem Domini instare minati sunt. Multi in nomine Christi venere Antichristi, quorum primus Simon Magus, cui auscultabant omnes qui erant in Samaria a minimo usque ad maximum dicentes: Hic est virtus Dei quae vocatur magna: eo quod multo tempore magicis artibus dementasset eos.

VERS. 8.-- Exsurget enim. (BEDA.) Haec omnia ante ultimos et acerbissimos dolores, etc., usque ad qui contra se invicem dimicantes, Ecclesiae victoriam faciunt.

227AVERS. 9.-- Videte autem. (BEDA.) Quare haec omnia inferantur, dicit Videte, etc. Ea enim vel sola maxima causa est excidii, quia post occisionem Domini, nominis quoque ac fidei praecones similes et confessores impia crudelitate vexabant.

VERS. 10.-- Et in omnes gentes. (ID.) Quia noverat Dominus corda discipulorum de perditione suae gentis tristanda, hoc modo consolatur, ut sciant amissis Judaeis se socios regni coelestis ex toto orbe habituros. Sicut enim ecclesiasticae historiae testantur multo ante excidium Judaeae, omnes apostoli ad praedicandum Evangelium per totum orbem sunt dispersi, exceptis Jacobo Zebedaei, et Jacobo fratre Domini, qui prius in Judaea pro verbo Evangelii sanguinem fuderunt.

227BVERS. 14.-- Cum autem videritis, etc. (ID.) Potest abominatio intelligi, vel Antichristus, vel imago Caesaris quam Pilatus in templo posuit, etc., usque ad semper parebat imperio Romanorum.

VERS. 17.-- Vae autem praegnantibus, etc. Quia vel uteri pondere vel filiorum sarcina gravatae fugere non poterunt. Unde in libro Historiarum legitur uxorem Jonathae turbatam fuga, filium sinu suo lapsum recepisse perpetuo claudum.

VERS. 21.-- Et tunc si quis. (BED.) Quidam hoc ad captivitatem Judaicam referunt, quando multi Christos se esse dicentes, populum post se deceptum trahebant. Sed ibi nullus erat fidelis, quem Deus exhortaretur, ne perversos magistros sequeretur. Omnes et obsidentes et obsessi alieni a Christo obdurabant, 227Cunde melius de haereticis accipiendum, qui contra Ecclesiam venientes, se Christos esse mentiuntur: quorum primus Simon Magus; extremus Antichristus.

VERS. 24.-- Sed in illis diebus, etc. (ID.) Sidera in judicio videbuntur obscura, etc., usque ad tunc fiet quod idem propheta alibi dicit: Et erit lux lunae sicut sol, et lux solis erit septempliciter sicut lux septem dierum (Isa. III).

VERS. 25.-- Decidentes. A lumine quando pene deerit semen Abrahae, cui assimilatae sunt stellae.

Et virtutes quae, etc. (BEDA.) Quid mirum tremere homines, cum aspectu judicii tremant angelicae potestates? Unde Job: Columnae coeli contremiscunt, et pavent ad nutum ejus (Job. XXVI). Et quid patitur 227Dvirgula deserti, ubi concutitur cedrus paradisi? Movebuntur. (HIER.) Ad iram vindictae quando mittentur a Filio hominis veniente in nubibus coeli cum virtute, quae prius sicut pluvia in vellus descendit in humilitate.

VERS. 27.-- A quatuor ventis. Ab oriente et occidente, austro, aquilone, et non tantum ab his; sed a summo terrae usque ad summum coelum, id est, ab extremis finibus terrae per directum usque ad ultimos terminos ejus, ubi longe aspicientibus circulus coeli terrae videtur insidere.

VERS. 28.-- A ficu autem. Quasi dicat, quomodo cum fuerint in ficu teneri cauliculi, et gemma in florem erumpit, intelligitis adventum aestatis et veris 228Aet favonii; sic cum haec quae scripta sunt videritis, non putetis jam adesse finem mundi, sed quasi praecursores venire, qui ostendant prope esse.

(BEDA.) Ficus Synagoga, quae quia ad se veniente Domino fructum justitiae non habuit, etc., usque ad et aestatem verae lucis et pacis.

(HIER.) Fici parabola, id est, prophetia. Folia nata, verba sunt praesentia, aestas vero proxima dies est judicii, in quo unaquaeque arbor manifestabit quod intus habuit an aridum ad comburendum, an viride ad plantandum in Eden cum ligno vitae cujus folia in salutem gentium, id est, verba quibus dicetur, Venite, benedicti Patris mei, etc.

VERS. 31.-- Coelum et terra transibunt. Coelum aereum, a quo aves coeli et nubila coeli, unde Petrus: 228BCoeli autem qui nunc sunt, et terra eodem verbo repositi sunt et igni reservati (II Pet. III), aperte docens, quia alii coeli sunt igne perituri quam aqua perditi, id est, inania et nebulosa. Aqua enim diluvii quae tantum quindecim cubitis cacumina montium transcendit ultra aeris aetherisque confinia non pervenit. Ecclesiastes (Cap. I): Generatio praeterit, et generatio advenit: terra vero in aeternum stat. Sic ergo coelum et terra transeunt, secundum imaginem, manent in aeternum secundum essentiam.

VERS. 32.-- Neque filius. -- In eo enim sunt omnes thesauri sapientiae et scientiae absconditi. Ideo absconditi, quia nobis scire non expedit, unde ait: Non est vestrum nosse tempora vel momenta quae Pater 228Cposuit in sua potestate (Act. I). In quo ostendit quod sciat, sed nosse apostolis non expediat, ut semper incerti de adventu judicis, sic quotidie vivant quasi alia die judicandi. Scit ergo filius sibi, sed non nobis, ut semper simus solliciti, unde: videte, vigilate, etc.

(HILAR.) Filius et Spiritus sanctus, quia non sunt a se, de die illa nesciunt a se: Pater autem, quia a se est, scit a se.

VERS. 34.-- Et janitori, etc. (HIER.) Ei, scilicet, qui praedicat. Cui dicitur: Si non annuntiaveris iniquo iniquitatem suam, sanguinem ejus de manu tua requiram (Ezech. III).

Vigilate ergo. Praemisso patrisfamilias exemplo, cur consummationis diem reticeat, dicit: vigilate ergo. Qui dormit, non corpora, sed phantasias videt, 228Dcum evigilat nihil habet de his quae viderat. Sic sunt quos mundi amor rapit in vita, deserit post vitam.

VERS. 36.-- Quod autem vobis. Non solum rectores Ecclesiae, sed et omnes vigilare praecipimur, januas cordium custodientes; ne antiqui hostis mala suggestio subintret, ne nos Dominus dormientes inveniat, unde Paulus: Vigilate, justi, et nolite peccare.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- And as he was going out. (BEDA.) They shall withdraw from the temple, the Lord, etc., as far as it was to pay the penalty, and 226Bthe buildings to fall in ruin.

Seest thou these, etc. But it was divinely provided that, when the grace of the evangelical faith had been laid open throughout the world, the temple itself with its ceremonies should be taken away: lest perchance one still a little child, and a suckling in the faith, if he saw those things abide which were made by the prophets and instituted by the Lord, in admiring a worldly holy thing, should slip back little by little into Judaism. The shadow therefore is taken away, and the truth, declared throughout the world, holds the palm.

(HIER.) He foretells the calamity of the last time, that is, the destruction of the temple together with its people and its letter. Of which: There shall not be left a stone upon a stone (Luc. XXI): that is, the testimonies of the prophets upon those against whom the Jews turned them back, as upon Esdras, upon Zorobabel, upon the Machabees.

226CVERS. 3.-- And when, etc. (BED.) He abides quiet among the saints: while he detests the madness of the proud. For the Mount of Olives signifies the fruitful loftiness of the Church: that mountain brings forth not unfruitful trees, but olive groves, by which light is fed, rest is granted to the weary, weakness is loosed.

VERS. 5.-- And answering. (ID.) From the time of the Lord's passion, among the people of the Jews who chose the seditious robber and cast away Christ the Saviour, neither the wars of enemies nor the seditions of citizens have ceased; but the apostles are warned not to be terrified at the coming of these things, and not to forsake Jerusalem and Judea, because the end is not at once. For to the fortieth year was deferred the desolation of the province and the last destruction of the city and of the temple.

226DVERS. 6.-- For many shall come. When the destruction was at hand, many came who would falsely claim that they were Christs, and that the time of liberty was already at hand. Many also in the Church, in the times of the apostles, threatened among other things that the day of the Lord was pressing on. Many antichrists came in the name of Christ, of whom the first was Simon Magus, to whom all who were in Samaria gave ear from the least to the greatest, saying: This is the power of God which is called great: because for a long time he had bewitched them with magic arts.

VERS. 8.-- For there shall rise. (BEDA.) All these things before the last and bitterest sorrows, etc., as far as who, fighting one against another, make the victory of the Church.

227AVERS. 9.-- But look to yourselves. (BEDA.) Why all these things are brought in, he says Look ye, etc. For this alone is the greatest cause of the destruction, that after the slaying of the Lord they harassed with impious cruelty the heralds likewise of his name and of the faith, and the confessors like him.

VERS. 10.-- And unto all nations. (ID.) Because the Lord knew that the hearts of the disciples were to be saddened at the perdition of their own nation, he comforts them in this manner, that they may know that, the Jews being lost, they will have companions of the heavenly kingdom out of the whole world. For as the ecclesiastical histories testify, long before the destruction of Judea all the apostles were scattered through the whole world to preach the Gospel, save James the son of Zebedee, and James the brother of the Lord, who first in Judea shed their blood for the word of the Gospel.

227BVERS. 14.-- But when you shall see, etc. (ID.) The abomination may be understood either as Antichrist, or as the image of Caesar which Pilate set in the temple, etc., as far as it was always obedient to the empire of the Romans.

VERS. 17.-- But woe to them that are with child, etc. Because, weighed down either by the burden of the womb or by the load of their children, they will not be able to flee. Whence in the book of Histories it is read that the wife of Jonathan, troubled in flight, took back her son, fallen from her bosom, lame for ever.

VERS. 21.-- And then if any man. (BED.) Some refer this to the Jewish captivity, when many, saying that they were Christs, drew the deceived people after them. But there was no faithful man there whom God might exhort not to follow perverse masters. All, both the besiegers and the besieged, being strangers to Christ, were hardened, 227Cwhence it is better to be taken of heretics, who, coming against the Church, falsely say that they are Christs: of whom the first was Simon Magus; the last, Antichrist.

VERS. 24.-- But in those days, etc. (ID.) The stars in the judgment shall be seen dark, etc., as far as then shall come to pass what the same prophet says elsewhere: And the light of the moon shall be as the sun, and the light of the sun shall be sevenfold, as the light of seven days (Isa. III).

VERS. 25.-- Falling down. From their light, when the seed of Abraham, to which the stars were likened, shall almost fail.

And the powers which, etc. (BEDA.) What wonder is it that men should tremble, when at the sight of the judgment the angelic powers tremble? Whence Job: The pillars of heaven tremble, and dread at his beck (Job. XXVI). And what does 227Dthe little rod of the desert suffer, where the cedar of paradise is shaken? They shall be moved. (HIER.) To the wrath of vengeance, when they shall be sent by the Son of man coming in the clouds of heaven with power, who before came down in humility like rain upon a fleece.

VERS. 27.-- From the four winds. From the east and the west, the south, the north, and not only from these; but from the utmost part of the earth to the utmost part of heaven, that is, from the farthest bounds of the earth straight on to its last limits, where to those who look from afar the circle of heaven seems to rest upon the earth.

VERS. 28.-- But from the fig tree. As if he said, as when there are tender shoots upon the fig tree, and the bud breaks out into flower, you understand the coming of summer and of spring 228Aand of the west wind; so when you shall see these things which are written, think not that the end of the world is already at hand, but that they come as forerunners, to show that it is near.

(BEDA.) The fig tree is the Synagogue, which, because when the Lord came to her she had not the fruit of justice, etc., as far as and the summer of the true light and peace.

(HIER.) The parable of the fig tree, that is, the prophecy. The leaves that are born are the present words, but the summer near at hand is the day of judgment, in which every tree shall make manifest what it had within, whether dry, for the burning, or green, for the planting in Eden with the tree of life, whose leaves are unto the health of the nations, that is, the words in which it shall be said, Come, ye blessed of my Father, etc.

VERS. 31.-- Heaven and earth shall pass away. The airy heaven, from which are the birds of heaven and the clouds of heaven, whence Peter: 228BBut the heavens which now are, and the earth, by the same word are laid up in store and reserved unto fire (II Pet. III), plainly teaching that the heavens which are to perish by fire are other than those which were destroyed by water, that is, the empty and cloudy ones. For the water of the flood, which passed the tops of the mountains by fifteen cubits only, did not come beyond the borders of the air and of the ether. Ecclesiastes (Cap. I): One generation passeth away, and another generation cometh: but the earth standeth for ever. So therefore heaven and earth pass away according to their image, they remain for ever according to their essence.

VERS. 32.-- Neither the Son. -- For in him are all the treasures of wisdom and of knowledge hidden. Therefore hidden, because it is not expedient for us to know, whence he says: It is not for you to know the times or the moments which the Father 228Chath put in his own power (Act. I). In which he shows that he knows, but that it is not expedient for the apostles to know, so that, being always uncertain of the coming of the judge, they may live daily as though they were to be judged on the morrow. The Son therefore knows for himself, but not for us, that we may always be watchful, whence: see ye, watch, etc.

(HILAR.) The Son and the Holy Ghost, because they are not of themselves, do not know of themselves concerning that day: but the Father, because he is of himself, knows of himself.

VERS. 34.-- And to the porter, etc. (HIER.) To him, namely, who preaches. To whom it is said: If thou declare not to the wicked man his iniquity, I will require his blood at thy hand (Ezech. III).

Watch ye therefore. The example of the father of the household being set before, why he keeps silence about the day of the consummation, he says: watch ye therefore. He who sleeps sees not bodies, but phantasms, 228Dand when he awakes he has nothing of those things which he had seen. Such are they whom the love of the world snatches away in life, and forsakes after life.

VERS. 36.-- And what I say to you. Not only the rulers of the Church, but all of us are bidden to watch, keeping the doors of our hearts; lest the evil suggestion of the ancient enemy steal in, lest the Lord find us sleeping, whence Paul: Watch, ye just, and sin not. [Vulg. 1 Cor. 15:34 Evigilate, "Awake"]

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Erat autem pascha. (HIER.) Pascha, transitus interpretatur, phase vero immolatio, etc., usque ad et armati virtute comedamus dicentes: Etenim Pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus (Matth. XXVI).

(BEDA.) Pascha, quod Hebraice phase, a transitu, 229Anon a passione, etc., usque ad iter scilicet admonet subire virtutum. Et quaerebant summi sacerdotes. (HIER.) A principibus egressa est iniquitas, qui templum parare, et vasa, et se purificare secundum legem ad esum agni debuerant.

VERS. 2.-- Non in die festo. (ID.) Vitant diem festum, quod convenit illis: non est festivitas eis qui vitam et misericordiam perdiderunt.

VERS. 3.-- Et cum esset Bethaniae. (ID.) Hinnulus cervorum semper ad lectulum suum redit, id est, Filius obediens Patri usque ad mortem: obedientiam a nobis petit. Simon enim obediens dicitur. Simonis leprosi. Simon leprosus mundum infidelem primo, postea fidelem significat. Et recumberet, venit, etc. Recumbente seipso, id est, humiliante se, ut eum tangeret 229Bfides peccatricis: quae de pedibus ascendit ad caput, a capite descendit per fidem, id est, a Christo ad membra ejus.

Mulier. (BEDA.) Maria Magdalena soror Lazari quem suscitavit Dominus, etc., usque ad ut Matthaeus et Marcus perhibent, oleo sancto perfudit.

Alabastrum. Alabastrum marmor candidum variis guttis distinctum unguenta incorrupta servat. Nardi spicati. Pistica nardus dicitur mista, quia non solum de radice, sed de spicis et de foliis compositum erat: quod est pretiosius.

Et fracto. (HIER.) Domus impleta odore, coelum et terra est. Fractum alabastrum carnale est desiderium, quod frangitur ad caput: ex quo omne corpus compaginatum est. (BED.) Devotio Mariae, fidem et 229Cpietatem designat Ecclesiae, etc., usque ad pia praedicatione et devotis veneratur obsequiis.

VERS. 4.-- Erant autem quidam. (ID.) Per synecdochen plurale pro singulari posuit, etc., usque ad cujus hac de causa furandi consuetudinem intimare curavit. Ut quid perditio ista. (HIER.) Perditus de salute perditionem invenit, ut in ficu fructifera mortis laqueum nancisceretur.

VERS. 5.-- Poterat enim unguentum. (ID.) Sub praetextu avaritiae mysterium loquitur fidei, etc., usque ad spolia dividamus ad vesperam.

VERS. 6.-- Bonum opus operata. (ID.) Qui credit in Deum, reputatur ei in opus justitiae. Aliud enim est credere ei, aliud est credere in eum, id est, totum se injicere in illum.

229DVERS. 7.-- Me autem non semper. (BEDA.) Corporali praesentia et familiaritate conjunctum sicut nunc. Unde Apostolus: Etsi noverimus Christum secundum carnem, sed nunc jam novimus (II Cor. V). Spiritualiter autem semper est nobiscum, unde: Ecce ego vobiscum sum usque ad consummationem saeculi omnibus diebus. (HIER.) Ita intelligendum est: Me autem non semper habebitis in corporali praesentia, ut prius in convictu et familiaritate.

VERS. 8.-- Quod habuit. Quod putatis perditionem esse unguenti, officium sepulturae est. Nec mirum si mihi dedit odorem fidei bonum, cum ego pro ea fusurus sum sanguinem meum.

VERS. 9.-- Amen dico vobis. (BEDA.) Nota in Christo 230Anotitiam futurorum, passuros post paucos dies praescit Evangelium suum toto orbe praedicandum. Notandum, quod sicut Maria toto orbe quo est Ecclesia diffusa gloriam meruit de pio obsequio, quod Domino devote exhibuit: sic et Judas qui ei detraxit, perfidiae nota longe lateque infamatur. Sed Dominus bonum pia laude remunerat, futura impii contumelias tacet.

VERS. 10.-- Uno de duodecim. (HIER.) Unus numero, non merito, nomine non numine, corpore non animo, unde sponsus ad sponsam: Vulnerasti cor meum, soror mea, in uno oculorum tuorum, et in uno crine colli tui (Cant. IV). Oculus et crinis sapientia est et virtus, quoniam Judae cum caeteris dicitur: Vobis datum est nosse mysterium regni Dei (Matth. XIII). Et: 230BDedi vobis potestatem (Luc. X), etc.

Abiit ad, etc. Ille abiit ad principes: et postquam exivit, intravit in eum Sathanas. Hic nox nocti indicat scientiam. Unumquodque animal ad sibi simile jungitur (Matth. XXVIII). Maria currit ad apostolos ut dies diei eructet verbum; Judas ad Judaeos, ut nox nocti indicet scientiam.

VERS. 11.-- Et quaerebat quomodo illum, etc. (HIER.) Promittit se tradere, ut magister ejus ante dicebat: Tlbi dabo hanc potestatem universam. BEDA. Multi hodie scelus Judae exhorrent nec tamen cavent, etc., usque ad ut amotis arbitris mendacio veritatem, crimine mutent virtutem.

VERS. 12.-- Et prima die azymorum. (Id.) Quarta 230Cdecima luna, quando abjecto fermento agnus occidebatur ad vesperum, etc., usque ad ipsius immolationis, id est, suae passionis exordium sacravit.

(ISID.) Cum amaritudine comeditur azyma, quae est redemptio nostra. Amaritudo enim est passio Domini.

VERS. 13.-- Ite in civitatem. (BED.) Indicium est praescientiae divinitatis, etc., usque ad et ad tollenda mundi crimina vivifici fontis baptisma consecretur. (HIER.) Civitas est Ecclesia quae muro fidei cingitur: homo occurrens primitivus populus est, amphora aquae lex litterae. Homo lagenam aquae. (BEDA.) Consulte et aquae bajuli, et Domini domus tacita sunt vocabula, ut omnibus verum pascha celebrare volentibus, id est sacramentis Christi imbui, eumque 230Dsuae mentis hospitio suscipere quaerentibus, facultas danda signetur.

Sequimini. (HIER.) Qui ducit in altum ubi refectio Christi, unde Raab exploratoribus mandat, ut non per ima sed per excelsa irent.

VERS. 15.-- Coenaculum grande. (ID.) Ecclesia magna, quae narrat nomen Domini, strata varietate virtutum et linguarum, unde: Circumamicta varietate (Psal. XLIV) virtutum, in qua paratur Domino pascha. Dominus domus Petrus apostolus cui Dominus domum suam credidit: ut sit una fides sub uno pastore. (BED.) Coenaculum lex spiritualis, quae de angustiis litterae egrediens in sublimi loco recipit Salvatorem, etc., usque ad quia cuncta paschae sacramenta 231Aet caetera legis decreta ejus esse sacramenta cognoscit.

VERS. 17.-- Vespere autem. (HIER.) Vespera diei vesperam indicat mundi. Circa undecimam namque horam veniunt novissimi: qui primi denarium accipiunt vitae aeternae: quia ante crucem Abraham erat in inferno, et post crucem latro in paradiso.

VERS. 18.-- Amen dico, etc. Sicut de passione praedixerat: ita de proditore praedicit, dans locum poenitendi, ut cum intellexisset cogitationes suas praesciri a Deo, poeniteret eum facti sui. Non tamen ex nomine designat, ne aperte redargutus impudentior fiat. Mittit crimen in numerum: ut conscius poenitentiam agat. Unus ex vobis. Dum falsitas arguitur, veritas comprobatur et impletur. Qui edebat panes 231Bmeos, magnificavit super me supplantationem (Psal. XL). Omnes tanguntur ut fiat harmonia in cithara. Omnes nervi bene suspensi consona voce respondent: Nunquid ego sum, Domine? Unus remissus et pecuniae amore affectus dixit: Nunquid ego sum, Rabbi?

VERS. 19.-- Contristari. Sicut undecim quia nihil mali contra Dominum cogitaverant: sed plus credunt magistro quam sibi, et timentes fragilitatem suam, tristes de peccato suo interrogant, cujus non habebant conscientiam.

VERS. 20.-- Unus ex duodecim. (HIER.) Separat seorsum ovem lupus quam cupit. Ovis quae de ovili egreditur, lupi patet morsibus. Qui intingit. (BEDA.) Mira Domini patientia. Prius dixit: Unus ex vobis tradet me, perseverat proditor in malo. Apertius arguit: 231Cet tamen non proprie designatur. Judas aliis contristatis et manum retrahentibus a cibis temeritate et impudentia qua Dominum tradit; etiam manum cum magistro in catino mittit ut audacia bonam conscientiam mentiretur.

VERS. 21.-- Vae autem. (ID.) In sempiternum vae homini illi qui ad mensam Domini indigne accedit, etc., usque ad poena praedicitur ut qui pudore non vincitur, timore corrigatur.

VERS. 22.-- Manducantibus. (ID.) Finito veteri pascha quod in commemoratione liberationis populi Dei ab Aegypto agebatur, etc., usque ad ideoque velocius a morte resuscitandum. Accepit. (HIER.) Figurans corpus suum in pane, etc., usque ad exstinguuntur inimici quae sunt mysteria Ecclesiae Christi. 231D(BEDA.) Panis qui confirmat cor hominis, etc., usque ad vel nos sine illius passione salvari.

VERS. 23.-- Gratias. Gratias egit et benedixit jam proximus passioni, qui poenam alienae iniquitatis suscipit: qui nihil dignum passione egit: ut ostendat quam aequanimiter unusquisque propriae culpae flagella sustinere debeat.

Biberant. (HIER.) Ebrietas felix: salutaris satietas, etc., usque ad sanguis enim Novi Testamenti qui pro multis effunditur non omnes emundat.

VERS. 25.-- Jam non bibam. (ID.) Hic mutat sacrificium, sed non mutat tempus; ut nos nunquam coenam Jesu ante quartam decimam lunam faciamus. Qui facit in quarta decima resurrectionem, in 232Aundecima coenam Domini facit, quod nunquam inventum est nec in Novo nec in Vetere Testamento. Vitis. (BEDA.) Vitis vel vinea Domini Synagoga, etc., usque ad de salute ejusdem populi baptismo regenerati novo vobiscum gaudio perfundar.

VERS. 26. Et hymno dicto. (ID.) Potest intelligi hymnus quem secundum Joannem Patri gratias agens decantabat, etc., usque ad et charismata sancti Spiritus quibus in corde perungamur, debere conscendere. In montem Olivarum. (HIER.) In monte Oliveti tenetur Jesus: et inde ascendit ad coelos: ut sciamus quia inde ascendimus ad coelos unde vigilamus, et oramus, et ligamur, nec repugnamus in terra.

Omnes scandalizabimini. (BEDA.) Praedicit quid passuri sint: ut cum passi fuerint non desperent, sed 232Bpoeniteant.

VERS. 27.-- In nocte ista. Qui ebrii sunt, nocte ebrii sunt (I Thess. V), et qui scandalizantur, nocte scandalizantur, id est, mente obscurati. (HIER.) Omnes cadunt, sed non omnes jacent. Nunquid quia dormit non adjiciet ut resurgat? (Zach. XIII.) Carnale est cadere: diabolicum est jacere. Percutiam pastorem. Percute pastorem. Propheta postulat passionem Domini: Pater respondet: Percutiam pastorem. Precibus sanctorum Filius a Patre mittitur: id est, incarnatur et percutitur, et patitur: disperguntur oves pastore capto. Resurrectio promittitur ut spes non exstinguatur.

VERS. 29.-- Petrus autem. (ID.) Avis sine pennis in altum volare nititur: sed corpus aggravat animam ut, 232Ctimore humanae mortis, timor Domini superetur.

VERS. 30.-- Bis gallus vocem. (BEDA.) Alii simpliciter: Priusquam gallus cantet, ter me negabis, etc., usque ad sicut et illud: Qui viderit mulierem ad concupiscendum, jam moechatus est eam (Matth. V). (HIER.) Gallus cantat: Petrus negat ter, etc., usque ad et trinae negationis sordes lavit lacrymis.

VERS. 32.-- Gethsemani. (ID.) Id est, vallis pinguium, ubi tauri pingues obsederunt eum et vituli multi circumdederunt eum. Sedete hic. (ISID.) Separantur in oratione qui separantur in passione: quia ille orat, illi dormiunt pinguedine cordis oppressi. Donec orem. (BED.) Cum Dominus in monte orat, etc., usque ad unde: Humiliavit semetipsum factus obediens usque ad mortem. Et majorem hac dilectionem 232Dnemo habet ut ponat quis animam suam pro amicis suis (Phil. II; Joan. XV).

VERS. 33.-- Et coepit pavere. (HIER.) Pavere et tristari docemur ante judicium mortis, qui non possumus per nos dicere nisi per illum: Venit princeps hujus mundi et in me non habet quidquam (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 34.-- Tristis est, etc. (BED.) Non propter mortem tristis est, etc., usque ad sed a somno infidelitatis et torpore mentis.

VERS. 35.-- Et orabat. (ID.) Haec vox est sonus infirmitatis nostrae, etc., usque ad sed quia aliter non fiet, Non quod ego volo, sed quod tu.

(HIER.) Hoc contra Eutychianos qui dicunt unam tantum in Christo operationem, unam voluntatem: 233Ahic autem ostendit humanam quae per infirmitatem carnis recusat passionem, et divinam quae prompta est perficere dispensationem.

VERS. 36.-- Sed non quod. (HIER.) Usque in finem non cessat docere nos patribus obedire: et voluntatem eorum voluntati nostrae praeponere.

VERS. 37.-- Et ait Petro, etc. Qui dixerat: Et si oporteat me mori tecum, non te negabo: etsi omnes scandalizati fuerint, sed non ego (Matth. XXVI); nunc tristitiae magnitudine somnium vincere non potest.

VERS. 38.-- Ut non intretis. Non ait ut non tentemini, sed ut non intretis in tentationem, id est, ut tentatio vos non superet, non teneat intra suos casses.

(HIER.) In tentationem intrat qui orare negligit. Ter discipuli dormiunt, ter Dominus orans 233Bsuscitat. Trina dormitio, tres mortuos quos Dominus suscitavit significat. Primus in domo, secundus ad sepulcrum, tertius de sepulcro. Trina Domini vigilia tres personas nos habere in orando docet, et de praeteritis et de praesentibus, et futuris veniam rogare. Caro autem infirma. (BEDA.) Caro namque pondere suo ad ima semper trahit.

VERS. 42.-- Surgite. Postquam tertio oravit, apostolorum timorem poenitentia corrigendum docuit, securus ad passionem pergit, dicens: Surgite, eamus. Quasi dicat nos non inveniant timentes, sed ultro eamus obviam, ut passuri gaudium et confidentiam videant.

VERS. 44.-- Signum. (HIER.) Dat signum osculi cum veneno diaboli: sicut Cain obtulit sacrificium 233Csubdolum et reprobatum, unde vinum cum aceto in cruce ponunt.

VERS. 45.-- Rabbi. (BEDA.) Impudens et scelerata confidentia magistrum vocat, etc., usque ad illud etiam complet: Cum his qui oderunt pacem (Psal. CXIX), etc.

VERS. 47.-- Unus. (ID.) Petrus secundum Joannem eodem ardore mentis quo caetera. Sciebat enim quod Phinees puniendo sacrilegos, mercedem justitiae et sacerdotii perennis acceperat. Lucas ait: Quod Dominus tetigit auriculam et sanavit eam (Luc. XXII). Ipse enim pietatis nunquam obliviscitur, hostes etiam suos vulnerari non patitur. Mystice: Docens eos qui in suae mortis consensione vulnus animae contraxerunt, si digne poenituerunt salutem posse 233Dmereri.

VERS. 48.-- Tanquam ad latronem. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Stultum est cum gladiis et fustibus comprehendere, qui ultro se vobis tradit: et in nocte quasi latitantem investigare per proditorem, cum quotidie in templo doceat. Sed ideo adversum me congregamini in tenebris, quia potestas vestra in tenebris est.

VERS. 50.-- Tunc, etc. (ID.) Impletur quod dixit, quod omnes scandalizarentur, etc., usque ad et a Deo revocandi, adversariis dare didicerunt.

VERS. 52.-- At ille, etc. Sicut Joseph relicto pallio nudus de manibus impudicae dominae effugit. Qui autem vult effugere manus iniquorum, relinquens mente quae mundi sunt, post Jesum fugiat. 234AAb eis. Quorum et praesentiam detestabatur et facta. Non a Domino, cujus amorem etiam absens corpore fixum servavit in mente.

VERS. 53.-- Summum, etc. Caipham scilicet, qui secundum Joannem erat pontifex anni illius, de quo consentaneum scribit Josephus, quod pontificium sibi absque merito dignitatis emerat a principe Romano: quid ergo mirum, si iniquus pontifex inique judicat?

VERS. 54.-- A longe. (BEDA.) Quia negationi proximus, si Christo proximus fuisset non negasset: in hoc tamen admirandus est, quod Dominum non reliquit etiamsi timeat. Quod timeat naturae est, quod sequitur devotionis est: quod negat obreptionis est, quod poenitet fidei est. Atrium. (HIER.) Atrium, saecularis 234Bcircumitus est. Ministri, daemonia sunt. Ignis desiderium carnale, cum quibus qui manet, flere peccata non valet. Et calefaciebat se. (BEDA.) Est ignis charitatis; de quo dicitur, etc., usque ad moxque sui cordis arcana prunis inflammavit amoris.

VERS. 56.-- Falsum testimonium. (ID.) Falsus est qui non in eodem sensu dicta intelligit quo dicuntur, etc., usque ad ut proprie de templo Judaico videatur dixisse.

VERS. 60.-- Et exsurgens, etc. Iratus, quia non invenit locum calumniae, motu corporis insaniam mentis demonstrat. Non respondens, etc. Ad responsum provocat, ut ex qualibet occasione sermonis locum accusandi inveniat. Jesus autem non respondit, 234Cquia praevidit quidquid responderet in calumniam verti.

Tu es Christus. (HIER.) Quem exspectabat a longe non videbat prope: sicut Isaac caligantibus oculis, Jacob sub manibus non agnoscebat, sed longe post de eo futura canit.

VERS. 62.-- Et videbitis. Sacerdos interrogat Filium Dei; Jesus respondit Filium hominis; ut intelligamus eumdem Filium Dei esse et Filium hominis, ne quaternitatem faciamus in Trinitate, et homo in Deo, et Deus in homine sit. A dextris virtutis. Quia humiliavit seipsum factus obediens usque ad mortem, mortem autem crucis: propter quod et Deus exaltavit illum, et dedit illi nomen quod est super omne nomen 234D(Phil. II).

Cum nubibus coeli. (HIER.) Ascendit in nube, cum nube veniet: id est cum corpore solo suo quod assumpsit a virgine ascendit, et cum multiformi Ecclesia, quae est corpus ipsius et plenitudo ad judicium venturus est, sicut dicit Matthaeus: Cum autem venerit Filius hominis, et omnes angeli cum eo, etc.
