VERS. 63.-- Summus autem sacerdos. (BEDA.) Eadem rabies quae prius de sede excusserat, etc., usque ad cum in Lycaonia quasi dii honorarentur, fecerunt. Vestimenta sua, etc. (HIER.) Hoc est, ephod in quo Judaei habebant honorem, amiserunt, etc., usque ad ejus tamen inviolata permanet castitas 235Ausque ad consummationem saeculi in illis, quos sors electionis invenerit.

VERS. 64.-- Qui omnes, etc. (HIER.) Quo reatu suo nostrum reatum solveret, etc., usque ad hinc propheta: Quid retribuam Domino pro omnibus quae retribuit mihi? (Psal. CXV.)

VERS. 65.-- Velare faciem, etc. Non ut scelera eorum non videat, sed, ut ipsi quondam Moysi fecerunt, a se gratiam cognitionis ejus abscondant. Hoc velamentum usque hodie manet super cor eorum, quod in Christum credentibus est ablatum. Unde eo moriente velut templum scissum est, et sancta sanctorum arcana patefacta.

Alapis eum caedebant. (BEDA.) Qui tunc caesus est alapis, et nunc caeditur blasphemiis falsorum Christianorum. 235BQui consumptus salivis infidelium, nunc vesanis fidelium opprobriis exhonoratur.

VERS. 68.-- At ille, etc. Nota, quod negat Christum qui se negat ejus esse discipulum. Dominus enim non dixit: Negabis te discipulum meum, sed me negabis. Negavit ergo eum cum se negavit ejus esse discipulum.

Et exiit foras, etc. (HIER.) Petrus sine spiritu, voci ancillae cessit, etc., usque ad et foras eximus extra quod fuimus.

VERS. 70.-- Nam et Galilaeus. (BEDA.) Eadem lingua Galilaeis et Hierosolymitis est, sed tamen quaecunque provincia et regio habet proprium loquendi sonum quem mutare, etc., usque ad quia nomen illud aliter Hierosolymitae, aliter Galilaei 235Csonabant.

VERS. 72.-- Et recordatus est. (ID.) Petrus nocte negat, ad galli cantum poenitet. In die quem tertio negaverat, se tertio amare professus est. Quod enim, etc., usque ad egreditur foras (ut alii Evangelistae narrant), ut ab impiis secretus, negationis culpam liberius abluat fletibus.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Now it was the pasch. (HIER.) Pascha is interpreted a passing over, but phase an immolation, etc., as far as and armed with virtue let us eat, saying: For Christ our pasch is sacrificed (Matth. XXVI).

(BEDA.) Pascha, which in Hebrew is phase, from a passing over, 229Anot from suffering, etc., as far as it admonishes us, namely, to undertake the way of the virtues. And the chief priests sought. (HIER.) Iniquity went forth from the princes, who ought to have made ready the temple, and the vessels, and to have purified themselves according to the law for the eating of the lamb.

VERS. 2.-- Not on the festival day. (ID.) They shun the festival day, and that befits them: there is no festival for those who have lost life and mercy.

VERS. 3.-- And when he was in Bethany. (ID.) The young hart of the harts always returns to his own bed, that is, the Son obedient to the Father even unto death: he asks obedience of us. For Simon is interpreted obedient. Of Simon the leper. Simon the leper signifies the world unbelieving at first, afterwards believing. And as he was at meat, there came, etc. He himself reclining, that is, humbling himself, that the faith of the sinful woman 229Bmight touch him: which faith goes up from the feet to the head, and comes down from the head through faith, that is, from Christ to his members.

A woman. (BEDA.) Mary Magdalen, the sister of Lazarus whom the Lord raised up, etc., as far as she poured holy oil upon him, as Matthew and Mark relate.

An alabaster box. Alabaster is a white marble marked with divers drops, and it keeps ointments uncorrupted. Of spikenard. Nard is called pistic, that is, mixed, because it was compounded not of the root only, but of the spikes and of the leaves: which is the more precious.

And breaking. (HIER.) The house filled with the odour is heaven and earth. The broken alabaster is carnal desire, which is broken at the head: from which the whole body is framed together. (BED.) The devotion of Mary designates the faith and 229Cthe piety of the Church, etc., as far as she venerates him with pious preaching and with devout observances.

VERS. 4.-- And there were some. (ID.) By synecdoche he put the plural for the singular, etc., as far as whose habit of thieving he took care for this cause to make known. To what purpose is this waste. (HIER.) He that was lost found perdition out of salvation, so that in a fruitful fig tree he might light upon the noose of death.

VERS. 5.-- For this ointment might have been. (ID.) Under the pretext of avarice he speaks the mystery of faith, etc., as far as let us divide the spoils at evening.

VERS. 6.-- She hath wrought a good work. (ID.) He who believes in God, it is reputed to him unto a work of justice. For it is one thing to believe him, another to believe in him, that is, to cast oneself wholly upon him.

229DVERS. 7.-- But me you have not always. (BEDA.) Joined to you by bodily presence and familiarity as now. Whence the Apostle: Although we have known Christ according to the flesh, yet now we know him (II Cor. V) [Vulg. 2 Cor. 5:16 sed nunc jam non novimus, "but now we know him so no longer"]. But spiritually he is always with us, whence: Behold I am with you even to the consummation of the world all days. (HIER.) Thus is it to be understood: But me you shall not always have in bodily presence, as before in companionship and familiarity.

VERS. 8.-- What she had. What you think to be a waste of the ointment is the office of burial. Nor is it a wonder if she has given me the good odour of faith, since I am about to shed my blood for her.

VERS. 9.-- Amen I say to you. (BEDA.) Note in Christ 230Athe knowledge of things to come, those about to suffer after a few days, he foreknows that his Gospel is to be preached in the whole world. It is to be noted that, as Mary through the whole world in which the Church is spread abroad earned glory for the loving service which she devoutly rendered to the Lord: so also Judas, who disparaged her, is defamed far and wide with the mark of perfidy. But the Lord rewards the good with loving praise, and is silent about the coming reproaches of the wicked man.

VERS. 10.-- One of the twelve. (HIER.) One in number, not in merit, in name not in divine power, in body not in mind, whence the bridegroom to the bride: Thou hast wounded my heart, my sister, with one of thy eyes, and with one hair of thy neck (Cant. IV). The eye and the hair are wisdom and virtue, since it is said to Judas with the rest: To you it is given to know the mystery of the kingdom of God (Matth. XIII). And: 230BI have given you power (Luc. X), etc.

He went to, etc. He went away to the princes: and after he went out, Satan entered into him. Here night to night showeth knowledge. Every living thing is joined to that which is like itself (Matth. XXVIII). Mary runs to the apostles, that day to day may utter speech; Judas to the Jews, that night to night may show knowledge.

VERS. 11.-- And he sought how he might, etc. (HIER.) He promises to betray him, as his master had said before: To thee will I give all this power. BEDA. Many today shudder at the crime of Judas and yet do not beware of it, etc., as far as that, the witnesses being removed, they may exchange truth for a lie, and virtue for a crime.

VERS. 12.-- And on the first day of the unleavened bread. (Id.) On the fourteenth 230Cmoon, when, the leaven being cast away, the lamb was slain at evening, etc., as far as he consecrated the beginning of that very immolation, that is, of his own passion.

(ISID.) With bitterness is the unleavened bread eaten, which is our redemption. For the bitterness is the passion of the Lord.

VERS. 13.-- Go into the city. (BED.) It is a token of the foreknowledge of his godhead, etc., as far as and that the baptism of the life-giving font may be consecrated for the taking away of the crimes of the world. (HIER.) The city is the Church, which is girt with the wall of faith: the man who meets them is the primitive people, the pitcher of water the law of the letter. A man carrying a pitcher of water. (BEDA.) Of set purpose are the names both of the water-bearer and of the master of the house passed over in silence, that it may be signified that the opportunity is to be given to all who wish to keep the true pasch, that is, to be steeped in the sacraments of Christ, and who seek to receive him 230Dinto the lodging of their own mind.

Follow ye. (HIER.) He who leads on high, where the refreshment of Christ is, whence Rahab charges the spies to go not by the low places but by the heights.

VERS. 15.-- A large upper room. (ID.) The great Church, which declares the name of the Lord, strewn with the variety of the virtues and of tongues, whence: Surrounded with variety (Psal. XLIV) of virtues, in which the pasch is prepared for the Lord. The master of the house is Peter the apostle, to whom the Lord entrusted his house: that there may be one faith under one shepherd. (BED.) The upper room is the spiritual law, which, going out from the straits of the letter, receives the Saviour in a lofty place, etc., as far as because it knows that all the sacraments of the pasch 231Aand the other decrees of the law are his sacraments.

VERS. 17.-- And in the evening. (HIER.) The evening of the day points to the evening of the world. For about the eleventh hour come the last: who first receive the penny of eternal life: because before the cross Abraham was in hell, and after the cross the thief was in paradise.

VERS. 18.-- Amen I say, etc. As he had foretold of his passion: so he foretells of the betrayer, giving place for repentance, that when he had understood that his thoughts were foreknown by God, he might repent of his deed. Yet he does not point him out by name, lest, being openly refuted, he become more shameless. He casts the crime into the number: that he who is conscious of it may do penance. One of you. While falsehood is reproved, truth is proved and fulfilled. He that did eat 231Bmy bread, hath greatly supplanted me (Psal. XL). All are touched, that harmony may be made in the harp. All the strings, being well strung, answer with a concordant voice: Is it I, Lord? One, slack and moved by the love of money, said: Is it I, Rabbi?

VERS. 19.-- To be made sorrowful. As the eleven, because they had thought nothing evil against the Lord: but they believe their master more than themselves, and fearing their own frailty, sorrowful they ask concerning their own sin, of which they had no consciousness.

VERS. 20.-- One of the twelve. (HIER.) The wolf separates apart the sheep which he desires. The sheep which goes out of the fold lies open to the bites of the wolf. He that dippeth. (BEDA.) Wonderful is the patience of the Lord. First he said: One of you shall betray me, the betrayer persists in his evil. He reproves more openly: 231Cand yet he is not named outright. Judas, when the others are made sorrowful and draw back their hand from the food, with the rashness and shamelessness with which he betrays the Lord; puts his hand also with his master into the dish, that by boldness he might feign a good conscience.

VERS. 21.-- But woe. (ID.) For ever woe to that man who comes unworthily to the table of the Lord, etc., as far as the punishment is foretold, that he who is not overcome by shame may be corrected by fear.

VERS. 22.-- As they were eating. (ID.) The old pasch being ended, which was kept in commemoration of the deliverance of the people of God from Egypt, etc., as far as and therefore that he was to be raised the more swiftly from death. He took. (HIER.) Figuring his own body in the bread, etc., as far as the enemies are extinguished, which are the mysteries of the Church of Christ. 231D(BEDA.) The bread which strengthens the heart of man, etc., as far as or that we are saved without his passion.

VERS. 23.-- Thanks. He gave thanks and blessed, being now near to his passion, he who takes upon himself the punishment of another's iniquity: who did nothing worthy of suffering: that he might show with how even a mind every man ought to bear the scourges of his own fault.

They had drunk. (HIER.) Happy drunkenness: saving fulness, etc., as far as for the blood of the New Testament which is shed for many cleanses not all.

VERS. 25.-- Now I will not drink. (ID.) Here he changes the sacrifice, but he does not change the time; so that we should never keep the supper of Jesus before the fourteenth moon. He who makes the resurrection on the fourteenth, keeps the supper of the Lord on the 232Aeleventh, which has never been found either in the New or in the Old Testament. The vine. (BEDA.) The vine or the vineyard of the Lord is the Synagogue, etc., as far as I shall be filled with a new joy together with you over the salvation of that same people regenerated by baptism.

VERS. 26. And a hymn being said. (ID.) It can be understood as the hymn which, according to John, he sang, giving thanks to the Father, etc., as far as and that we ought to ascend by the graces of the Holy Spirit with which we are anointed in the heart. Unto the mount of Olives. (HIER.) On Mount Olivet Jesus is held fast: and thence he ascends to the heavens: that we may know that we ascend to the heavens from that place where we watch, and pray, and are bound, and do not fight back on the earth.

You shall all be scandalized. (BEDA.) He foretells what they shall suffer: that when they have suffered they may not despair, but 232Brepent.

VERS. 27.-- In this night. They who are drunk, are drunk in the night (I Thess. V), and they who are scandalized, are scandalized in the night, that is, being darkened in mind. (HIER.) All fall, but not all lie prostrate. Because he sleepeth, shall he not rise again? (Zach. XIII.) To fall is carnal: to lie prostrate is devilish. I will strike the shepherd. Strike the shepherd. The prophet asks for the passion of the Lord: the Father answers: I will strike the shepherd. At the prayers of the saints the Son is sent by the Father: that is, he is made flesh and is struck, and suffers: the sheep are scattered, the shepherd being taken. The resurrection is promised, that hope be not extinguished.

VERS. 29.-- But Peter. (ID.) A bird without feathers strives to fly on high: but the body weighs down the soul, so that, 232Cby the fear of a human death, the fear of the Lord is overcome.

VERS. 30.-- The cock shall twice give voice. (BEDA.) Others simply: Before the cock crow, thou shalt deny me thrice, etc., as far as as also that word: He that shall look on a woman to lust after, hath already committed adultery with her (Matth. V). (HIER.) The cock crows: Peter denies thrice, etc., as far as and he washed away the filth of the threefold denial with tears.

VERS. 32.-- Gethsemani. (ID.) That is, the valley of fat ones, where fat bulls beset him and many calves surrounded him. Sit ye here. (ISID.) They are separated in prayer who are separated in the passion: because he prays, while they sleep, weighed down by the fatness of the heart. Till I pray. (BED.) When the Lord prays on the mountain, etc., as far as whence: He humbled himself, becoming obedient unto death. And greater love than this 232Dno man hath, that a man lay down his life for his friends (Phil. II; Joan. XV).

VERS. 33.-- And he began to fear. (HIER.) We are taught to fear and to be sorrowful before the judgment of death, we who cannot say of ourselves, save through him: The prince of this world cometh, and in me he hath not anything (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 34.-- My soul is sorrowful, etc. (BED.) He is not sorrowful because of death, etc., as far as but from the sleep of unbelief and from the torpor of the mind.

VERS. 35.-- And he prayed. (ID.) This voice is the sound of our infirmity, etc., as far as but because it will not be otherwise, Not what I will, but what thou wilt.

(HIER.) This is against the Eutychians, who say that there is in Christ only one operation, one will: 233Abut here he shows the human, which through the infirmity of the flesh refuses the passion, and the divine, which is ready to accomplish the dispensation.

VERS. 36.-- But not what I will. (HIER.) Even to the end he ceases not to teach us to obey our fathers: and to set their will before our own.

VERS. 37.-- And he says to Peter, etc. He who had said: And if it behove me to die with thee, I will not deny thee: even if all shall be scandalized, yet not I (Matth. XXVI); now, through the greatness of his sorrow, cannot overcome sleep.

VERS. 38.-- That ye enter not. He does not say, that ye be not tempted, but that ye enter not into temptation, that is, that temptation may not overcome you, may not hold you within its nets.

(HIER.) He enters into temptation who neglects to pray. Thrice the disciples sleep, thrice the Lord in prayer 233Brouses them. The threefold sleeping signifies the three dead whom the Lord raised up. The first in the house, the second at the sepulchre, the third out of the sepulchre. The Lord's threefold watching teaches us that we have three persons in praying, and to ask pardon both for things past and for things present and for things to come. But the flesh is weak. (BEDA.) For the flesh by its own weight ever draws to the lowest things.

VERS. 42.-- Arise. After he had prayed the third time, he taught that the fear of the apostles was to be corrected by penance, and goes untroubled to the passion, saying: Arise, let us go. As if he should say, let them not find us fearing, but let us go of our own accord to meet them, so that, we being about to suffer, they may see joy and confidence.

VERS. 44.-- A sign. (HIER.) He gives the sign of a kiss with the venom of the devil: as Cain offered a sacrifice 233Cguileful and reprobate, whence they set wine with vinegar upon the cross.

VERS. 45.-- Rabbi. (BEDA.) With shameless and wicked assurance he calls him master, etc., as far as he fulfils that also: With them that hated peace (Psal. CXIX), etc.

VERS. 47.-- One. (ID.) Peter, according to John, with the same ardour of mind as in the rest. For he knew that Phinees, by punishing the sacrilegious, had received the reward of justice and of a perpetual priesthood. Luke says: That the Lord touched the ear and healed it (Luc. XXII). For he himself never forgets loving-kindness, and suffers not even his own enemies to be wounded. Mystically: teaching that they who in consenting to his death contracted a wound of the soul can, if they have worthily repented, deserve 233Dsalvation.

VERS. 48.-- As to a robber. (ID.) As if he should say: It is foolish to seize with swords and clubs one who delivers himself up to you of his own accord: and by night, as though he were in hiding, to hunt him out by means of a traitor, when daily he teaches in the temple. But therefore you gather against me in darkness, because your power is in darkness.

VERS. 50.-- Then, etc. (ID.) That is fulfilled which he said, that all should be scandalized, etc., as far as and they that were to be called back by God learned to give to their adversaries.

VERS. 52.-- But he, etc. As Joseph, leaving his cloak behind, fled naked out of the hands of the unchaste mistress. But whoever wishes to escape the hands of the wicked, let him leave in mind the things that are of the world, and flee after Jesus. 234AFrom them. Whose presence he loathed, and whose deeds. Not from the Lord, whose love, though absent in body, he kept fixed in his mind.

VERS. 53.-- The high priest, etc. Caiphas, namely, who according to John was the high priest of that year, concerning whom Josephus writes to the same effect, that he had bought the high priesthood for himself from the Roman prince without any merit of worth: what wonder then, if an unjust high priest judges unjustly?

VERS. 54.-- Afar off. (BEDA.) Because he was near to denial, had he been near to Christ he would not have denied: in this, nevertheless, he is to be admired, that he did not leave the Lord even though he fears. That he fears is of nature, that he follows is of devotion: that he denies is of stealth, that he repents is of faith. The court. (HIER.) The court is the worldly 234Bcircuit. The servants are the demons. The fire is fleshly desire, and he who abides with these is not able to weep for his sins. And he warmed himself. (BEDA.) There is a fire of charity; whereof it is said, etc., as far as and forthwith he set the secret places of his heart aflame with the coals of love.

VERS. 56.-- False testimony. (ID.) He is false who does not understand things said in the same sense in which they are said, etc., as far as so that he may seem to have spoken properly of the Jewish temple.

VERS. 60.-- And rising up, etc. Being angry, because he found no place for calumny, he shows by the motion of his body the madness of his mind. Not answering, etc. [Vulg. Mc 14:60 Non respondes, "Dost thou not answer"] He provokes him to an answer, that from any occasion of speech he may find place for accusing. But Jesus answered not, 234Cbecause he foresaw that whatever he should answer would be turned into calumny.

Thou art Christ. (HIER.) Whom he was awaiting from afar, him he did not see near at hand: as Isaac, with darkened eyes, did not know Jacob under his hands, but long afterwards sings of things to come concerning him.

VERS. 62.-- And you shall see. The priest asks after the Son of God; Jesus answers the Son of man; that we may understand the same to be Son of God and Son of man, lest we make a quaternity in the Trinity, and that man may be in God and God in man. On the right hand of power. Because he humbled himself, becoming obedient unto death, even the death of the cross: for which cause God also hath exalted him, and hath given him a name which is above every name 234D(Phil. II).

With the clouds of heaven. (HIER.) He ascended in a cloud, with a cloud he will come: that is, he ascended with his own body alone, which he took from the Virgin, and he is to come to judgment with the manifold Church, which is his body and his fulness, as Matthew says: But when the Son of man shall come, and all the angels with him, etc.

VERS. 63.-- But the high priest. (BEDA.) The same madness which before had shaken him from his seat, etc., as far as they did, when in Lycaonia they were honoured as gods. His garments, etc. (HIER.) That is, they lost the ephod in which the Jews had their honour, etc., as far as yet her chastity remains inviolate 235Aeven to the consummation of the world in those whom the lot of election shall find.

VERS. 64.-- Who all, etc. (HIER.) That by his own guilt he might loose our guilt, etc., as far as hence the prophet: What shall I render to the Lord for all the things that he hath rendered to me? (Psal. CXV.)

VERS. 65.-- To cover the face, etc. Not that he might not see their crimes, but that, as they themselves once did to Moses, they might hide from themselves the grace of the knowledge of him. This veil remains upon their heart until this day, which is taken away for them that believe in Christ. Whence, at his dying, it was as though the temple was rent, and the secret things of the holy of holies laid open.

They struck him with the palms of their hands. (BEDA.) He who was then struck with blows is now struck with the blasphemies of false Christians. 235BHe who was consumed with the spittle of unbelievers is now dishonoured by the raving reproaches of the faithful.

VERS. 68.-- But he, etc. Note that he denies Christ who denies that he is his disciple. For the Lord did not say: Thou shalt deny thyself to be my disciple, but thou shalt deny me. He denied him therefore when he denied that he was his disciple.

And he went forth without, etc. (HIER.) Peter, without the Spirit, yielded to the voice of a maidservant, etc., as far as and we go forth outside of what we were.

VERS. 70.-- For thou art also a Galilean. (BEDA.) The Galileans and the men of Jerusalem have the same tongue, but nevertheless every province and region has its own sound of speaking, which to change, etc., as far as because the men of Jerusalem sounded that name in one way, and the Galileans 235Cin another.

VERS. 72.-- And he remembered. (ID.) Peter denies by night, at the cock's crowing he repents. By day he professed three times that he loved him whom he had three times denied. For that which, etc., as far as he goes forth without (as the other Evangelists relate), that, set apart from the ungodly, he may the more freely wash away the guilt of his denial with tears.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- Et confestim, etc. Sequitur: Vincientes Jesum. Mos erat Judaeis, ut quem morte adjudicassent, vinctum judici traderent. Et notandum, quod non tunc primum ligaverant, sed mox comprehensum nocte in horto, ut Joannes dicit: Et sic adduxerunt eum ad Annam primum (Joan. XVIII).

(HIER.) Adest Samson vinctus a Dalila. Samson 235Dinterpretatur, etc., usque ad et fontem perennis vitae sitientibus nobis, id est corporis sui, aperit.

VERS. 2.-- Tu es rex Judaeorum? Cum Pilatus nihil criminis interrogat nisi an sit rex Judaeorum, arguitur impietas eorum qui nec falso quidem quod objiciant invenerunt. Tu dicis. Sic temperat responsum, ut verum dicat, nec tamen calumniae pareat. Et nota, quod Pilato qui invitus fert sententiam, aliqua in parte responderit: sacerdotibus autem et principibus respondere noluerit, indignosque sermone suo judicaverit.

VERS. 4.-- Vide, etc. Ethnicus condemnat Jesum, sed causam refert in populum Judaeorum. Vide in quantis, etc.

236AVERS. 11.-- Ut magis Barabbam. (BEDA.) Adhaeret Judaeis usque hodie petitio sua, quam toto labore impetraverunt. Data enim optione sibi, pro Jesu latronem, pro Salvatore interfectorem elegerunt. Merito ergo salutem et vitam perdiderunt et latrociniis ac seditionibus in tantum se subdiderunt, ut regnum et patriam perderent, et libertatem corporis et animae nunquam reciperent.

VERS. 14.-- Pilatus vero. Multas occasiones dedit Pilatus liberandi Salvatorem, primo justo conferens latronem, deinde inferens: Quid ergo vultis faciam regi Judaeorum? Cumque respondissent: Crucifigatur, non statim acquievit, sed secundum suggestionem uxoris quae mandaverat ei (ut Matthaeus dicit): Nihil tibi et justo illi (Matth. XXVII), respondens ait: 236BQuid enim mali fecit? Quod dicendo, Jesum absolvit. At illi magis, etc. David: Circumdederunt me canes multi (Psal. XXI), etc. Isaias: Exspectavi ut faceret judicium, et ecce iniquitas: et justitiam, et ecce clamor (Isa. V). Jeremias: Facta est haereditas mea mihi sicut leo in silva: dedit contra me vocem (Jer. XII).

VERS. 15.-- Barabbam, et tradidit, etc. (HIER.) Hi sunt duo hirci, unus apompeius, id est emissarius, cum peccato populi in desertum inferni absolutus dimittitur. Alter pro peccatis absolutorum, ut agnus occiditur. Pars Domini semper mactatur, pars diaboli, qui est magister eorum, effrenata in tartara praecipitatur.

Jesum flagellis. Ut nos flagellis liberaret; unde: 236CFlagellum non appropinquabit tabernaculo tuo (Psal. XC): cum prius diceretur: Multa flagella peccatoris (Psal. XXXI).

(BEDA.) Jesum flagellis caesum. Caesus est ab ipso Pilato, unde Joannes: Apprehendit Pilatus Jesum et flagellavit: et post subjunxit: Et milites plectentes coronam de spinis (Joan. XIX), etc. Et militibus illudendum praebuit. Quod ideo fecit, ut ejus poenis et opprobriis saturati ultra mortem non sitirent.

VERS. 17.-- Et induunt. (BEDA.) Matthaeus ita: Et exuentes eum, chlamydem coccineam circumdederunt ei (Marc. XXVII). Quod idem est. Pro regia enim purpura data est, etc., usque ad purpura vel cocco vestitur, cum triumpho gloriosorum martyrum 236Dgloriatur.

VERS. 19.-- Et percutiebant, etc. Caput Christi Deus est. Caput ergo Christi percutiunt qui eum denegant esse Deum. Et qui Scripturae auctoritate errorem suum defendunt (quia scriptura arundine solet fieri), quasi arundine caput ejus feriunt. In faciem ejus spuunt qui praesentiam gratiae ejus exsecrandis verbis ex interna caecae mentis insania conceptis respuunt, et eum in carne venisse negant.

Adorabant eum. Quasi falso se dixisset Deum. Sed hodie (quod est gravioris insaniae) quidam eum certa fide ut Deum verum adorant, sed perversis actibus mox verba ejus quasi fabulosa despiciunt, 237Aac promissa regni illius temporalibus illecebris postponunt.

Et educunt illum. (HIER.) Hic educitur Abel in agrum a fratre ut perimatur. Hic adest Isaac, etc., usque ad hic adest Jonas de ligno navis foris et in mare ventremque ceti triduo missus.

VERS. 20.-- Ut crucifigerent. (HIER.) Ipsa crucis species quid est nisi forma quadrati mundi? Oriens de virtute fulget, arcton dextera tenet, auster laeva consistit, occidens sub plantis firmatur. Unde Apostolus: Ut sciamus quae sit latitudo, longitudo, sublimitas et profundum (Ephes. III). Aves in forma crucis ad aethera volant, homo adorat, et natat in forma crucis, navis per maria antenna cruci assimilata sufflatur. Tau littera signum salutis et crucis 237Bdemonstrat.

VERS. 21.-- Et angariaverunt. (BEDA.) Ipse Dominus primo crucem suam portavit, sicut Joannes dicit: Post iste Simon, de quo et alii dicunt, et hoc satis congruo ordine mysterii. Christus enim passus pro nobis, vobis relinquens exemplum ut sequamini vestigia ejus (Joan. XIX; I Petr. II).

(HIER.) Simon qui portat crucem in angaria, hic est qui laborat pro laude humana. Cogunt eum homines huic labori quem non cogit timor vel dilectio Dei.

Patrem Alexandri. Magnae opinionis Simon iste videtur esse, cum et filii ejus tanquam noti omnibus ex nomine designantur.

(HIER.) Alii per merita patrum suorum commemorantur, 237Calii per merita filiorum. Hic autem Simon, etc., usque ad unde: per Jeremiam ad Judaeam dicitur: Olivam uberem, pulchram, fructiferam, speciosam vocavit Dominus nomen tuum.

VERS. 22.-- Calvariae locus. (HIER.) Tradunt Judaei quod in hoc loco montis immolatus fuerit aries pro Isaac (Gen. XXII), ut ibi decalvetur Christus, id est carne sua, scilicet Judaea carnali, separetur.

(BEDA.) Extra portam truncabantur capita damnatorum, et calvariae decollatorum loca dicuntur. Ideo ibi crucifixus Dominus ut ubi erat area damnatorum, ibi erigantur vexilla martyrum. Et quoniam pro nobis factum est maledictum crucis et flagellatus et crucifixus pro salute omnium, quasi noxius inter noxios voluit crucifigi.

237DVERS. 23.-- Et dabant. (BEDA.) Matthaeus, cum felle mistum (Matth. XXVII), quod idem est. Fel enim pro amaritudine posuit. Myrrhatum enim vinum est amarissimum; quanquam fieri possit ut et felle et myrrha amarissimum redderent vinum.

(HIER.) Et dabant, etc. Hoc aceto succus lethalis pomi abstergitur. Et non accepit. Id pro quo patitur. Unde de eo: Quae non rapui tunc exsolvebam (Psal. LXVIII).

Et non accepit. Matthaeus: Et cum gustasset, noluit bibere. Indicat Matthaeus quod pro nobis amaritudinem mortis gustaverit, sed die tertia resurrexit.

VERS. 24.-- Et crucifigentes eum. (HIER.) Hic 238Afigitur salus in ligno. Primo infixa est mors in ligno. Lignum primum, scientiae boni et mali; secundum boni tantum et vitae nobis lignum est. Extensio primae manus ad lignum mortem apprehendit; extensio secundae vitam quae perierat invenit. Ligno hoc vehimur per mare undosum ad terram viventium.

Vestimenta sua. (BEDA.) Quadripartita vestis Christi secundum numerum militum, significat Ecclesiam quatuor partibus orbis aequaliter, id est concorditer distributam.

(HIER.) Vestimenta Domini mandata sunt quibus tegitur corpus ejus, quod est Ecclesia, quia dividuntur inter milites gentium ut sint quatuor ordines cum sit una fides, id est conjugati, viduati, 238Bpraepositi, separati. Sortiti sunt tunicam indivisam quae est pax et veritas in cunctis in modum regalis amictus vel annuli. Mittentes sortem. (ID.) Super tunicam, scilicet quae erat inconsutilis. Hic sortita, etc., usque ad et sors solius Dei judicio venit vel cedit.

VERS. 25.-- Erat autem hora ter. (HIER.) Hoc proprie Marcus, nam sexta hora tenebrae suffuderunt terram, ut non quis posset movere caput. A tricesimo namque, etc., usque ad qui magis dicendi sunt eum crucifixisse quamvis nitantur hoc dissimulare, quam milites qui secundum officium suum duci suo paruere.

VERS. 26.-- Et erat titulus. (BEDA.) Titulus ostendit quod nec occidendo potuerunt efficere quod 238Cnon esset eis rex; unde: Ego autem constitutus sum rex ab eo, etc. Bene enim simul est rex et pontifex, cum eximiam Patri suae carnis hostiam offerret in altari crucis, et regis dignitatem titulo praetenderet, ut cunctis insinuaret quod per crucis patibulum non perdiderit, sed confirmaverit imperium.

VERS. 27.-- Et cum eo crucifigunt. (BEDA.) Sicut dicit Apostolus: Vetus homo noster simul cum illo crucifixus est ut destruatur corpus peccati, ut ultra non dominetur super nos peccatum. Quandiu ergo agunt opera nostra ut evacuetur corpus peccati, quandiu, etc., usque ad unde Apostolus: Ut in charitate radicati et fundati possitis comprehendere cum omnibus sanctis quae sit latitudo, longitudo, sublimitas et profundum (Ephes. III). Duos latrones. 238D(ID.) Latrones qui cum Domino crucifixi sunt signant eos qui fide et confessione Christi, vel agone sui martyrii, vel quamlibet arctam continentiam subeunt. Et qui hoc pro aeterna gloria gerunt, dextri latronis merito et fide signantur. Qui autem pro humana gloria vel qualibet minus digna intentione, sinistri latronis mentem imitatur et actus.

(HIER.) Cum iniquis veritas deputata unum relinquit sinistrum, alterum assumit dextrum; sic faciet in die judicii, ex simili crimine dissimiles sortiuntur vias. Alter praecedit Petrum in paradisum, alter Judam in infernum. Confessio brevis vitam acquirit longam, et blasphemia finita poena plectitur aeterna. Hic pullus Judae ligatur ad 239Avitem ἀναβόλαιον, id est, pallium tingitur in sanguine uvae, hic lacerant haedi vineam.

VERS. 29.-- Moventes capita. (HIER.) Emittitur mors iniquorum quando excitantur vincula inferni usque ad cervicem, quae est cervix humani generis.

VERS. 31.-- Alios salvos. (HIER.) Etiam nolentes confitentur quod alios fecit salvos, et se propria damnant sententia, quia alios salvos fecit et se si vellet salvare poterat.

VERS. 32.-- Descendat nunc. Plus est de sepulcro surgere, et tamen non crediderunt. Hi omnes declinaverunt, simul inutiles facti sunt (Psal. XIII).

Et qui cum eo. (BEDA.) Secundum Lucam (Luc. XXIII) unus latro blasphemabat dicens: Si tu es 239BChristus, salvum fac te et nos. Alter, etc., usque ad qui coelestia petunt, ad ea sine dubio Christo mediante et miserante perveniunt.

VERS. 33.-- Et facta hora sexta. (BEDA.) Notandum quod hora sexta, id est sole recessuro a centro mundi, crucifixus sit, et diluculo, oriente sole resurrexit, etc., usque ad et qua hora primus Adam peccando mundo mortem intulit, eadem hora secundus Adam mortem moriendo destrueret.

Tenebrae. (HIER.) Hic adest Noe inebriatus ac nudatus coelo ac terra ebrioso pallio tectus, et ab homine irrisus hic stillavit de ligno sanguis.

VERS. 35.-- Et quidam, etc. Non omnes. Forsitan milites Romani non intelligentes proprietatem Hebraei sermonis, ex eo quod dicitur Eloi, putaverunt 239CEliam ab eo vocari. Vel Judaei solito sibi sermone verba Domini depravantes, imbecillitatis infamabant eum quod Eliae vocaret auxilium.

VERS. 36.-- Currens autem, etc. (BEDA.) Joannes plenius dicit: Postea sciens Jesus, etc. Quasi: Hoc minus fecistis, date quod estis. Erant enim, etc., usque ad de quo: Dominus plantavit vineam Soreth: et quomodo conversa es in amaritudinem vitis alienae: et exspectavi ut faceres uvas, et fecisti spinas (Isa. V).

VERS. 37.-- Jesus autem, etc. (BEDA.) Quid dixerit Lucas aperit: Pater, in manus tuas commendo spiritum meum; et hoc dicens exspiravit (Luc. XXIII). Quod autem dicit Joannes: Cum accepisset acetum, dixit: Consummatum est: et inclinato capite tradidit 239Dspiritum (Joan. XIX). Inter hoc quod ait: Consummatum est, et illud: inclinato capite emisit spiritum, emissa est vox illa magna, quam Joannes tacet, caeteri commemorant: In manus tuas, etc.

(HIER.) Infirmata etenim carne mox virtus divina invaluit quae dicit: Aperite mihi portas justitiae (Psal. CXVII), etc. Nos autem cum nulla, vel una voce morimur qui de terra sumus; ille enim cum exaltata voce exspiravit qui de coelo descendit.

VERS. 38.-- Et velum templi. Ut arcam testamenti, et omnia legis sacramenta quae tegebantur appareant, et ad gentes transeant. Ante dicebatur: Notus in Judaea Deus, in Israel magnum nomen ejus (Psal. LXXV); nunc autem: Exaltare super coelos 240ADeus (Ibid. LVI), et in omni, etc.; et in Evangelio prius: In viam gentium ne abieritis (Matth. X).; et post passionem: Ite, et docete omnes gentes (Ibid. XXVIII).

VERS. 39.-- Videns autem, etc. Ostenditur quare centurio miratur, quia vidit eum sic exspirasse, id est spiritum emisisse. Nullus enim habet potestatem emittendi spiritum, nisi conditor animarum.

Vere, etc. (HIER.) Nota quod centurio ante crucem in ipso scandalo passionis vere Filium Dei confitetur: et Arius, etc., usque ad et vere Filium Dei Synagoga tacente confirmat.

VERS. 40.-- Erant autem. (ID.) Sicut non excluditur muliebris sexus a salute per Mariam virginem: ita non repellitur a mysterio crucis scientiae 240Bet resurrectionis per viduam Mariam Magdalenam et caeteras matres.

Jacobi minoris et Joseph. (BEDA.) Minorem Jacobum dicit Jacobum Alphei, qui et frater Domini, eo quod esset filius Mariae materterae Domini. De qua Joannes: Stabant autem juxta crucem Jesu mater ejus (Joan. XIX), etc. Cleophae videtur eam dicere a patre vel a cognatione.

VERS. 42.-- Et cum jam sero esset, etc. (ID.) Παρασκευὴ Graece, praeparatio Latine. Hoc nomine Judaei inter Graecos morantes sextam sabbati appellabant, quia in eo quae sabbato erant necessaria praeparabant secundum quod, etc., usque ad donec octava veniente aetate et ipsa corpora resurrectione 240Cglorificata cum animabus simul incorruptionem aeternae haereditatis accipiant.

VERS. 43.-- Venit Joseph. (HIER.) Joseph venit sero parasceve ab Arimathia, quae interpretatur deponens, ad deponendum corpus Christi, etc., usque ad aspicientibus a longe electis qui sunt stellae maris, quando, si fieri potest, scandalizabuntur etiam electi.

VERS. 46.-- Joseph autem, etc. (BEDA.) Ex simplici sepultura Domini, ambitio divitum condemnatur, qui nec in tumulis, etc., usque ad lineo terreno celebrat, sicut corpus Domini in syndone munda sepultum est: sic a papa Sylvestro statutum est.

Quod erat excisum. (BEDA.) Dicitur quod monumentum Domini domus rotunda fuit de subjacente rupe excisa tantae altitudinis ut homo rectus consistens 240Dvix extenta manu culmen possit attingere. Et habet introitum, etc., usque ad color autem monumenti et loculi albo et rubeo dicitur esse permistus.

VERS. 47.-- Maria autem, etc. (ID.) Lucas dicit: Quia astabant omnes noti ejus a longe, et mulieres quae secutae fuerant eum (Luc. XXIII). Sed aliis deposito corpore ejus ad sua remeantibus, solae mulieres, etc., usque ad et si forte valeant imitari pia curiositate, quo ordine sit ejus passio amplectenda, perpendunt.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- And straightway, etc. There follows: Binding Jesus. It was the custom of the Jews that whomsoever they had adjudged to death they should deliver bound to the judge. And it is to be noted that they had not bound him then for the first time, but as soon as he was seized by night in the garden, as John says: And thus they led him away to Annas first (Joan. XVIII).

(HIER.) Here is Samson bound by Dalila. Samson 235Dis interpreted, etc., as far as and he opens to us who thirst the fountain of everlasting life, that is, of his own body.

VERS. 2.-- Art thou the king of the Jews? When Pilate asks of no crime save whether he be the king of the Jews, the impiety of those men is convicted who found not even falsely anything to lay against him. Thou sayest. So he tempers his answer that he speaks the truth, and yet gives no occasion to calumny. And note that to Pilate, who bears the sentence unwillingly, he answered in some part: but to the priests and the chief men he would not answer, and judged them unworthy of his speech.

VERS. 4.-- See, etc. The heathen condemns Jesus, but refers the cause to the people of the Jews. See in how many things, etc.

236AVERS. 11.-- That rather Barabbas. (BEDA.) Their own petition cleaves to the Jews unto this day, which with all their labour they obtained. For, the choice being given them, they chose a robber instead of Jesus, a murderer instead of the Saviour. Deservedly therefore they lost salvation and life, and gave themselves over to robberies and seditions so far that they lost their kingdom and their country, and never received again the liberty of body and of soul.

VERS. 14.-- But Pilate. Pilate gave many occasions of freeing the Saviour, first in comparing the robber with the just one, then in bringing forward: What will you then that I do to the king of the Jews? And when they had answered: Let him be crucified, he did not at once give in, but according to the suggestion of his wife, who had sent to him (as Matthew says): Have thou nothing to do with that just man (Matth. XXVII), answering he said: 236BWhy, what evil hath he done? And in saying this he acquitted Jesus. But they the more, etc. David: Many dogs have encompassed me (Psal. XXI), etc. Isaias: I looked that he should do judgment, and behold iniquity: and justice, and behold a cry (Isa. V). Jeremias: My inheritance is become to me as a lion in the wood: it hath cried out against me (Jer. XII).

VERS. 15.-- Barabbas, and delivered, etc. (HIER.) These are the two goats, the one apompeius, that is, the emissary, is loosed and sent away with the sin of the people into the desert of hell. The other is slain as a lamb for the sins of those who are loosed. The Lord's portion is always sacrificed, the devil's portion, who is their master, is hurled unbridled into Tartarus.

Jesus with scourges. That he might free us from scourges; whence: 236CThe scourge shall not come nigh thy dwelling (Psal. XC): whereas before it was said: Many are the scourges of the sinner (Psal. XXXI).

(BEDA.) Jesus scourged with whips. He was scourged by Pilate himself, whence John: Pilate took Jesus and scourged him: and afterwards he added: And the soldiers platting a crown of thorns (Joan. XIX), etc. And he gave him over to the soldiers to be mocked. This he did for this end, that being sated with his punishments and reproaches they might not thirst further after his death.

VERS. 17.-- And they clothe him. (BEDA.) Matthew thus: And stripping him, they put a scarlet cloak about him (Marc. XXVII). Which is the same thing. For it was given in place of the royal purple, etc., as far as she is clothed in purple or in scarlet, when in the triumph of the glorious martyrs 236Dshe glories.

VERS. 19.-- And they struck, etc. The head of Christ is God. They therefore strike the head of Christ who deny him to be God. And they who defend their error by the authority of Scripture (because writing is wont to be made with a reed) strike his head as it were with a reed. They spit in his face who spurn the presence of his grace with execrable words conceived out of the inward madness of a blind mind, and deny that he came in the flesh.

They adored him. As though he had falsely called himself God. But today (which is of a graver madness) certain men adore him with sure faith as true God, yet by their perverse deeds forthwith despise his words as though they were fables, 237Aand set the promises of his kingdom after the enticements of things temporal.

And they lead him out. (HIER.) Here Abel is led out into the field by his brother to be slain. Here is Isaac, etc., as far as here is Jonas, cast out from the wood of the ship and into the sea and into the belly of the whale for three days.

VERS. 20.-- That they might crucify him. (HIER.) What is the very shape of the cross but the form of the four-square world? The east shines from the power, the right hand holds the north, the south stands at the left, the west is made firm beneath the feet. Whence the Apostle: That we may know what is the breadth, the length, the height and the depth (Ephes. III). Birds fly to the upper air in the form of a cross, a man adores, and swims, in the form of a cross, a ship is driven through the seas by a yard-arm made like to a cross. The letter Tau shows the sign of salvation and of the cross 237B.

VERS. 21.-- And they forced. (BEDA.) The Lord himself first bore his own cross, as John says: afterwards this Simon, of whom the others also speak, and this in an order of mystery altogether fitting. For Christ suffered for us, leaving you an example that you should follow his steps (Joan. XIX; I Petr. II).

(HIER.) Simon, who bears the cross under compulsion, is he who labours for human praise. Men force him to this labour whom the fear or the love of God does not force.

The father of Alexander. This Simon seems to have been of great repute, since his sons also are marked out by name as being known to all.

(HIER.) Some are called to mind through the merits of their fathers, 237Cothers through the merits of their sons. But this Simon, etc., as far as whence: by Jeremias it is said to Judea: A plentiful olive tree, fair, fruitful, goodly, the Lord called thy name.

VERS. 22.-- The place of Calvary. (HIER.) The Jews hand down that in this place of the mountain the ram was offered for Isaac (Gen. XXII), that there Christ might be made bald, that is, might be severed from his own flesh, namely from carnal Judea.

(BEDA.) Outside the gate the heads of the condemned were struck off, and the places of the beheaded are called calvaries. Therefore the Lord was crucified there, that where was the threshing-floor of the condemned, there the standards of the martyrs might be raised up. And because for us was made the curse of the cross, and he was both scourged and crucified for the salvation of all, he willed to be crucified as one guilty among the guilty.

237DVERS. 23.-- And they gave. (BEDA.) Matthew, mingled with gall (Matth. XXVII), which is the same thing. For he put gall for bitterness. For wine mingled with myrrh is most bitter; although it may be that they made the wine most bitter with both gall and myrrh.

(HIER.) And they gave, etc. By this vinegar the juice of the deadly apple is wiped away. And he took it not. That for which he suffers. Whence of him: That which I took not away, I then paid (Psal. LXVIII).

And he took it not. Matthew: And when he had tasted, he would not drink. Matthew shows that for us he tasted the bitterness of death, but on the third day rose again.

VERS. 24.-- And crucifying him. (HIER.) Here 238Asalvation is fastened upon a tree. First death was fastened upon a tree. The first tree was of the knowledge of good and evil; the second is for us a tree of good only, and of life. The stretching out of the first hand to the tree laid hold on death; the stretching out of the second found the life that had perished. By this wood are we carried over the billowy sea to the land of the living.

His garments. (BEDA.) The garment of Christ, divided into four according to the number of the soldiers, signifies the Church distributed equally, that is, harmoniously, through the four quarters of the world.

(HIER.) The garments of the Lord are the commandments with which his body is covered, which is the Church, because they are divided among the soldiers of the Gentiles, so that there are four orders while the faith is one, that is, the married, the widowed, 238Bthe prelates, the set apart. They cast lots for the undivided tunic, which is peace and truth in all things, after the manner of a royal mantle or of a ring. Casting lots. (ID.) Upon the tunic, namely, which was without seam. Here the things allotted, etc., as far as and the lot comes or falls by the judgment of God alone.

VERS. 25.-- Now it was the third hour. (HIER.) This Mark has properly, for at the sixth hour darkness overspread the earth, so that none could move his head. For from the thirtieth, etc., as far as who are rather to be said to have crucified him, though they strive to dissemble it, than the soldiers, who according to their office obeyed their captain.

VERS. 26.-- And there was a title. (BEDA.) The title shows that not even by killing him could they bring it about that 238Che should not be their king; whence: But I am appointed king by him, etc. For he is fittingly at once king and priest, since he offered to the Father the excellent victim of his own flesh upon the altar of the cross, and held forth the dignity of a king by the title, that he might make known to all that through the gibbet of the cross he had not lost, but had confirmed, his empire.

VERS. 27.-- And with him they crucify. (BEDA.) As the Apostle says: Our old man is crucified together with him, that the body of sin may be destroyed, that sin may no more have dominion over us. [Vulg. Rom. 6:6 vetus homo noster simul crucifixus est, ut destruatur corpus peccati, et ultra non serviamus peccato, "and that we may serve sin no longer"] So long therefore as our works act, that the body of sin may be emptied out, so long, etc., as far as whence the Apostle: That, being rooted and founded in charity, you may be able to comprehend with all the saints what is the breadth, the length, the height and the depth (Ephes. III). Two thieves. 238D(ID.) The thieves who were crucified with the Lord signify those who, by faith and confession of Christ, or by the struggle of their own martyrdom, undergo any strict continence whatsoever. And they who do this for eternal glory are signified by the merit and the faith of the thief on the right. But whoever does it for human glory or with any less worthy intention imitates the mind and the deeds of the thief on the left.

(HIER.) Truth, reckoned among the wicked, leaves the one on the left and takes up the other on the right; so will he do in the day of judgment, out of a like crime they obtain unlike ways. The one goes before Peter into paradise, the other before Judas into hell. A short confession purchases a long life, and a blasphemy that has an end is punished with an eternal penalty. Here the colt of Juda is bound to 239Athe vine, ἀναβόλαιον, that is, the cloak is dyed in the blood of the grape, here the kids tear the vineyard.

VERS. 29.-- Wagging their heads. (HIER.) The death of the wicked is sent forth when the bonds of hell are stirred up even to the neck, which is the neck of the human race.

VERS. 31.-- He saved others. (HIER.) Even unwilling they confess that he made others safe, and they condemn themselves by their own sentence, because he made others safe and could have saved himself if he would.

VERS. 32.-- Let him now come down. It is more to rise from the sepulchre, and yet they did not believe. These all have gone aside, they are become unprofitable together (Psal. XIII).

And they that were with him. (BEDA.) According to Luke (Luc. XXIII) one thief blasphemed, saying: If thou be 239BChrist, save thyself and us. The other, etc., as far as they who seek heavenly things without doubt attain to them, Christ mediating and having mercy.

VERS. 33.-- And when the sixth hour was come. (BEDA.) It is to be noted that at the sixth hour, that is, when the sun was about to withdraw from the centre of the world, he was crucified, and that at daybreak, at the rising of the sun, he rose again, etc., as far as and at what hour the first Adam by sinning brought death into the world, at that same hour the second Adam by dying should destroy death.

Darkness. (HIER.) Here is Noe made drunk and stripped, covered by heaven and earth with a drunkard's cloak, and mocked by man, here blood dripped from the wood.

VERS. 35.-- And some, etc. Not all. Perhaps the Roman soldiers, not understanding the property of the Hebrew tongue, from that which is said Eloi, thought 239Cthat Elias was being called upon by him. Or the Jews, perverting the Lord's words in their accustomed speech, defamed him of weakness, in that he was calling for the help of Elias.

VERS. 36.-- And running, etc. (BEDA.) John says more fully: Afterwards Jesus, knowing, etc. As if: This you have done the less, give what you are. For they were, etc., as far as whereof: The Lord planted the vine Soreth: and how art thou turned into the bitterness of a strange vine: and I looked that thou shouldst bring forth grapes, and thou hast brought forth thorns (Isa. V).

VERS. 37.-- But Jesus, etc. (BEDA.) Luke discloses what he said: Father, into thy hands I commend my spirit; and saying this he expired (Luc. XXIII). But that which John says: When he had taken the vinegar, he said: It is consummated: and bowing his head he delivered up 239Dthe spirit (Joan. XIX). Between this which he says: It is consummated, and that: bowing his head he sent forth the spirit, there was sent forth that great voice which John passes over in silence, and the rest record: Into thy hands, etc.

(HIER.) For, the flesh being made weak, the divine power straightway prevailed, which says: Open ye to me the gates of justice (Psal. CXVII), etc. But we who are of the earth die with no voice, or with one; for he expired with a lifted-up voice who came down from heaven.

VERS. 38.-- And the veil of the temple. That the ark of the testament and all the mysteries of the law which were covered may appear, and may pass over to the Gentiles. Before it was said: God is known in Judea, in Israel his name is great (Psal. LXXV); but now: Be thou exalted above the heavens, 240AO God (Ibid. LVI), and in all, etc.; and in the Gospel before: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles (Matth. X).; and after the passion: Go ye, and teach all nations (Ibid. XXVIII).

VERS. 39.-- And seeing, etc. It is shown why the centurion wonders, because he saw that he had so expired, that is, had sent forth his spirit. For no one has the power of sending forth his spirit, save the creator of souls.

Truly, etc. (HIER.) Note that the centurion before the cross, in the very scandal of the passion, confesses him truly the Son of God: and Arius, etc., as far as and, the Synagogue keeping silence, he affirms him truly the Son of God.

VERS. 40.-- And there were. (ID.) As the female sex is not shut out from salvation through Mary the Virgin: so it is not driven back from the mystery of the cross, of knowledge 240Band of the resurrection, through the widow Mary Magdalen and the other mothers.

Of James the less and of Joseph. (BEDA.) By James the less he means James the son of Alpheus, who is also the brother of the Lord, in that he was the son of Mary the Lord's mother's sister. Of whom John: Now there stood by the cross of Jesus his mother (Joan. XIX), etc. He seems to call her Cleophas's from her father or from her kindred.

VERS. 42.-- And when it was now evening, etc. (ID.) Παρασκευὴ in Greek, preparation in Latin. By this name the Jews dwelling among the Greeks called the sixth day of the week, because on it they prepared the things that were needful for the sabbath, according as, etc., as far as until, the eighth age coming, the bodies themselves also, by the resurrection 240Cglorified, may together with the souls receive the incorruption of the eternal inheritance.

VERS. 43.-- Joseph came. (HIER.) Joseph came in the evening of the parasceve from Arimathia, which is interpreted laying down, to lay down the body of Christ, etc., as far as the elect, who are the stars of the sea, looking on from afar, when, if it can be done, even the elect shall be scandalized.

VERS. 46.-- And Joseph, etc. (BEDA.) From the simple burial of the Lord the ambition of the rich is condemned, who not even in their tombs, etc., as far as celebrates with earthly linen, as the body of the Lord was buried in a clean linen cloth: so was it decreed by pope Sylvester.

Which was hewn out. (BEDA.) It is said that the Lord's monument was a round house hewn out of the underlying rock, of such a height that a man standing upright 240Dcan scarcely reach the top with his hand stretched out. And it has an entrance, etc., as far as but the colour of the monument and of the tomb is said to be mingled of white and red.

VERS. 47.-- And Mary, etc. (ID.) Luke says: That all his acquaintance stood afar off, and the women that had followed him (Luc. XXIII). But when the others, his body being laid down, had returned to their own, the women alone, etc., as far as and they weigh carefully, with pious curiosity, whether haply they may be able to imitate him, and in what order his passion is to be embraced.

CAPUT XVI.

VERS. 1.-- Et cum transisset. (BEDA.) Lucas dicit quia revertentes a monumento paraverunt aromata et unguenta, et sabbato siluerunt, quia mandatum 241Aerat ut sabbati silentium, etc., usque ad illuminata facie decussis vitiorum tenebris odorem bonorum operum Domino et orationum suavitatem offerre.

(HIER.) Nunc cum aromatibus redolentibus cum sponsa et adolescentulis currunt, post eam conspergimus librum, id est cubile, etc., usque ad et Domino in eo cum triumpho resurgente et dicente: Haec est dies quam fecit Dominus, etc. (PSAL. CXVII).

VERS. 2.-- Et valde mane. (HIER.) Quod alius dicit diluculo. Diluculum est inter tenebras noctis et diei claritatem in qua salus venit in Ecclesia declaranda more solis, qui consurgens roseam praemittit auroram, ut tantus splendor praeparatis oculis possit videri, cum tempus Dominicae resurrectionis illuxit, ut tunc laudes Christi tota cantaret ecclesia sanctarum 241Bfeminarum, quando vitam praestitit et lumen credulitatis infundit.

VERS. 3.-- Et dicebant ad invicem. (HIER.) Aquilae congregantur ad corpus, martyres et apostoli vident lapidem revolutum, qui est lex mortis, quasi dicat: Ubi est, mors, aculeus tuus (I Cor. XV)?

VERS. 4.-- Revolutum lapidem. (BEDA.) Quomodo lapis per angelum sit revolutus, Matthaeus dicit? Allegorice autem, lapidis revolutio resurrectionem sacramentorum Christi quae velamine litterae legis tecta erant, insinuat. Lex etenim in lapide scripta est cujus ablato tegmine, gloria resurrectionis ostensa est, et abolitio mortis antiquae, et vita nobis speranda perpetua toto orbe coepit praedicari.

241CVERS. 5.-- Juvenem. (HIER.) Non senem, non infantem, sed jucundum aetate, ut dicitur: Laetare, juvenis, in adolescentia tua (Eccle. XI), quae non est hic vera dum senio mista.

(BEDA.) Mulieres angelos vident quae cum aromatibus venerunt, quia illae mentes supernos cives aspiciunt, quae cum virtutibus ad Deum per sancta desideria veniunt.

Sedentem in dexteris. (BEDA.) Ad meridianam partem loci illius, ubi positum fuerat, etc., usque ad intrantes in monumentum duos angelos ibi stantes invenerunt.

(GREG.) Per sinistram vita praesens, per dexteram vita aeterna, etc., usque ad et ad angelorum numerum restituens coelestis patriae damna reparavit.

241DVERS. 6.-- Nolite expavescere. (GREG.) Quasi: Paveant illi qui non amant adventum Domini vel supernorum civium, qui vitiis pressi de eorum societate desperant. Vos autem, quid timetis quae vestros concives videtis? Unde Matthaeus angelum apparuisse describens ait: Erat aspectus ejus sicut fulgur, et vestimenta ejus sicut nix (Matt. XXVIII).

Jesum. Salutare. Sed quia multi non substantialiter sed nuncupative hoc nomine dicebantur, determinat per locum Nazarenum, et causam subdit: Crucifixum. Atque addit: Surrexit, non est hic. Per praesentiam carnis, qui tamen nusquam deest per praesentiam majestatis. Ecce locus. Ostenditur mortalitas mortalibus ad actionem gratiarum debitam, 242Aut intelligamus quid simus et fuerimus, et sciamus quid erimus.

VERS. 7.-- Et Petro. (BEDA.) Petrus vocatur ex nomine, ne desperet ex negatione. Nisi enim, etc., usque ad ut qui futurus erat pastor Ecclesiae in sua culpa disceret quomodo aliis misereri deberet.

Et Petro. (HIER.) Qui se indignum discipulatu indicat, cum ter magistrum negat. Sed peccata praeterita non nocent, quando non placent.

Praecedit vos. Bene de Redemptore nostro dicitur: de Galilaea namque transmigratio facta interpretatur. Jam enim a passione ad resurrectionem, a morte ad vitam, a poena ad gloriam transmigraverat. Et bene post resurrectionem in Galilaea a discipulis videtur, quia resurrectionis ejus gloriam 242Bpost laeti videbimus, si modo a vitiis ad virtutum celsitudinem transmigremus. Qui ergo in sepulcro nuntiatur, in transmigratione ostenditur; et qui in mortificatione carnis agnoscitur, in transmigratione mentis videtur.

(BEDA.) Nota quod Marcus dicit: Praecedit vos in Galilaeam, etc. Nec tamen ibi visum retulit. Matthaeus autem, etc., usque ad sicut in illa aeternitate cognoscetur, quo et nos perducet propter formam servi, ut liberi contemplemur formam Domini.

VERS. 8.-- Fugerunt. (HIER.) De futura vita dicitur: Fugiet dolor et gemitus. Imitantur mulieres ante resurrectionem omnium quod faciunt post, id est, quod facturae sunt fugiunt mortem et pavorem.

Et nemini. (HIER.) Quia illi soli mysterium resurrectionis 242Cvident qui meruerunt, unde, secundum Joannem: Petrus autem surgens cucurrit ad monumentum, ut videret quae audivit.

VERS. 9.-- Surgens autem. Mane surrexit, sero sepultus est, ut hoc adimpleret: Ad vesperum demorabitur fletus et ad matutinum laetitia (Psal. XXIX). Sepultus ergo sexta sabbati quae vocatur parasceve circa vesperam sequenti nocte et die sabbati, cum sequenti nocte in monumento positus, die tertia, id est primo mane prima sabbati, surrexit. Et bene una die et duabus noctibus in sepulcro jacuit, quia lucem suae simplae mortis nostrae duplae mortis tenebris adjunxit. In morte enim animae et spiritus tenebamur, unde ad nos suam, id est carnis mortem attulit, 242Det duas nostras solvit: simplam suam duplae nostrae contulit, et duplam nostram moriens abstulit. Apparuit primo Mariae Magdalenae. Mariae Magdalenae primo ostenditur, de qua ejecerat septem daemonia: quia meretrices, et publicani praecedunt Synagogam in regnum Dei, ut latro praecessit apostolos. Apostoli flent et lugent, quia necdum viderunt, sed cito consolabuntur.

(BEDA.) In principio mulier auctor culpae viro fuit, exsecutor vir erroris, etc., usque ad ut ipsis evangelistis et apostolis resurrectionem evangelizaret.

VERS. 11.-- Et illi audientes. (BEDA.) Quod discipuli resurrectionem tarde credunt, non tam est illorum infirmitas, quam nostra firmitas; dubitantibus enim 243Aresurrectio per multa argumenta monstrata est, quibus et nos firmius solidamur.

VERS. 12.-- Duobus ex his. (BEDA.) Hoc Lucas apertius (Luc. XXIV): Oculi eorum, etc. Post cognoverunt eum in fractione panis. Et sicut Lucas ait: Surgentes eadem hora reversi sunt in Hierusalem, etc.

Ambulantibus. (HIER.) Fides hic laborat agens activam vitam, illic contemplativa, etc., usque ad oblitus carnis suae postulat in ista vita quod post illam speramus in futura.

VERS. 14.-- Novissime, etc. (BEDA.) Quadragesimo die, quando erat jam ab eis recessurus in coelum, hoc eis maxime, etc., usque ad: Euntes in mundum universum, praedicate Evangelium.

Duritiam cordis, etc. (HIER.) Ut succedat cor carneum 243Bcharitate plenum: hinc quod catervae martyrum mortem hujus saeculi libenter affectant, quia norunt pro temporali interitu perpetuo se esse victuros.

VERS. 15.-- Omni creaturae. Omni nationi gentium. Ante enim dictum erat: In viam gentium ne abieritis (Matth. X): ut scilicet apostolorum prius a Judaea repulsa praedicatio, tunc gentibus in adjutorium fieret.

(HIER.) Omni generi humano, quod habet aliquid commune omni creaturae, angelis, pecoribus, lignis, lapidibus, igni et aquae, calido et frigido, humido et arido, quia minor mundus homo dicitur.

VERS. 16.-- Qui crediderit, etc. Per alios parvuli credunt, sicut ex aliis ea quae in baptismo eis remittuntur, 243Cpeccata extraxerunt.

VERS. 17.-- Signa autem, etc. Nunquid si signa 244Anon faciamus, non credimus? Sed haec in exordio necessaria fuerunt, ut fides miraculis nutriretur, fide autem Ecclesiae jam confirmata non sunt necessaria. Daemonia ejicient, etc. Hoc hodie spiritualiter facit Ecclesia, cum per exorcismi gratiam manum credentibus imponit, et malignos spiritus expellit. Linguis loquentur novis. Hoc fit dum fideles veteris vitae saecularia verba relinquunt, et sancta mysteria insonant, et Dei laudes et potentiam quantum valent extollunt.

VERS. 18.-- Si mortiferum, etc. Dum pestiferas persuasiones audiunt, nec ad operationes usque perducunt, quod inde eis non nocet si mortiferum bibunt. Super aegros manus, etc. Dum proximos in bono opere confirmatos roborant exemplo bonae operationis, 244Bsuper aegros manus imponunt, et bene habebunt. Haec miracula tanto majora, quanto spiritualia; per hoc enim animae suscitantur, non corpora.

VERS. 19.-- In coelum. (AUG.) Nota quod aliquando coelos pluraliter et aliquando coelum singulariter, etc., usque ad ubi fidelium credulitas plus actibus quam locutionibus eruditur.

Et sedet a dextris. (BEDA.) Et nunc omnia judicans, in fine omnium Judex venturus est. Stephanus autem vidit eum stantem, quia in certamine habuit eum adjutorem.

VERS. 20.-- Illi autem, etc. (BEDA.) Nota quod Marcus Evangelium suum quanto inchoavit tardius, tanto, etc., usque ad quo apostoli idem Evangelii 244Cverbum per totum orbem seminaverunt.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 1.-- And when the sabbath was past. (BEDA.) Luke says that returning from the monument they prepared spices and ointments, and on the sabbath they rested, because it had been commanded 241Athat the silence of the sabbath, etc., as far as with face enlightened, the darkness of vices being shaken off, to offer to the Lord the odour of good works and the sweetness of prayers.

(HIER.) Now with fragrant spices they run with the bride and the young maidens, after her we sprinkle the book, that is, the couch, etc., as far as and the Lord rising again in it with triumph and saying: This is the day which the Lord hath made, etc. (PSAL. CXVII).

VERS. 2.-- And very early. (HIER.) Which another calls the dawning. The dawning is between the darkness of the night and the brightness of the day, in which salvation came, to be made known in the Church after the manner of the sun, which rising sends the rosy dawn before it, so that so great a splendour may be seen by eyes made ready, when the time of the Lord's resurrection shone forth, that then the whole church of holy 241Bwomen might sing the praises of Christ, when he bestowed life and poured in the light of belief.

VERS. 3.-- And they said one to another. (HIER.) The eagles are gathered to the body, the martyrs and the apostles see the stone rolled away, which is the law of death, as if he should say: Where is, O death, thy sting (I Cor. XV)?

VERS. 4.-- The stone rolled back. (BEDA.) Matthew says: how was the stone rolled away by the angel? But allegorically, the rolling away of the stone signifies the resurrection of the mysteries of Christ, which had been covered by the veil of the letter of the law. For the law was written on stone, and when its covering was taken away the glory of the resurrection was shown forth, and the abolition of the ancient death, and the everlasting life that is to be hoped for by us began to be preached throughout the whole world.

241CVERS. 5.-- A young man. (HIER.) Not an old man, not an infant, but pleasant in age, as it is said: Rejoice, young man, in thy youth (Eccle. XI), which here is not true while it is mingled with old age.

(BEDA.) The women who came with spices see angels, because those minds behold the citizens above which come to God with virtues through holy desires.

Sitting on the right side. (BEDA.) Toward the southern part of that place where he had been laid, etc., as far as entering into the monument they found two angels standing there.

(GREG.) By the left hand is the present life, by the right the life eternal, etc., as far as and restoring them to the number of the angels he repaired the losses of the heavenly country.

241DVERS. 6.-- Be not affrighted. (GREG.) As if: Let them fear who love not the coming of the Lord or of the citizens above, who, weighed down by vices, despair of their fellowship. But you, why do you fear, who see your own fellow-citizens? Whence Matthew, describing the angel's appearing, says: His countenance was as lightning, and his garments as snow (Matt. XXVIII).

Jesus. Salvation. But because many were called by this name not substantially but by appellation, he determines it by the place, Nazarene, and adds the cause: Crucified. And he adds: He is risen, he is not here. By the presence of the flesh, who nevertheless is nowhere absent by the presence of his majesty. Behold the place. Mortality is shown to mortals for the giving of due thanks, 242Athat we may understand what we are and have been, and may know what we shall be.

VERS. 7.-- And to Peter. (BEDA.) Peter is called by name, lest he despair because of his denial. For unless, etc., as far as that he who was to be the shepherd of the Church might learn in his own fault how he ought to have mercy on others.

And to Peter. (HIER.) He shows himself unworthy of discipleship when he thrice denies his master. But past sins do not hurt when they do not please.

He goeth before you. Well is it said of our Redeemer: for Galilee is interpreted a passing over accomplished. For he had already passed over from the passion to the resurrection, from death to life, from punishment to glory. And well is he seen by the disciples in Galilee after the resurrection, because the glory of his resurrection 242Bwe shall afterwards see with joy, if only we now pass over from vices to the height of the virtues. He therefore who is announced in the sepulchre is shown in the passing over; and he who is known in the mortification of the flesh is seen in the passing over of the mind.

(BEDA.) Note that Mark says: He goeth before you into Galilee, etc. Nor yet did he relate that he was seen there. But Matthew, etc., as far as as he shall be known in that eternity, whither he will also lead us through the form of a servant, that being free we may contemplate the form of the Lord.

VERS. 8.-- They fled. (HIER.) Of the life to come it is said: Sorrow and mourning shall flee away. The women before the resurrection of all imitate what they do afterwards, that is, what they are to do, they flee death and dread.

And to no one. (HIER.) Because they alone see the mystery of the resurrection 242Cwho have deserved it, whence, according to John: But Peter rising up ran to the monument, that he might see what he had heard.

VERS. 9.-- But rising. In the morning he rose, in the evening he was buried, that he might fulfil this: In the evening weeping shall have place, and in the morning gladness (Psal. XXIX). Buried therefore on the sixth day of the week, which is called the parasceve, about evening, through the following night and the sabbath day, laid in the monument with the night following, on the third day, that is, at first light on the first day of the week, he rose again. And fittingly he lay one day and two nights in the sepulchre, because he joined the light of his own single death to the darkness of our twofold death. For we were held in the death of the soul and of the spirit, whence he brought to us his own, that is, the death of the flesh, 242Dand loosed our two: his single he set against our twofold, and dying he took away our twofold. He appeared first to Mary Magdalen. He is shown first to Mary Magdalen, out of whom he had cast seven demons: because harlots and publicans go before the Synagogue into the kingdom of God, as the thief went before the apostles. The apostles weep and mourn, because they have not yet seen, but soon they shall be comforted.

(BEDA.) In the beginning the woman was to the man the author of guilt, the man the executor of the error, etc., as far as that she might announce the resurrection to the evangelists and apostles themselves.

VERS. 11.-- And they hearing. (BEDA.) That the disciples are slow to believe the resurrection is not so much their weakness as our strength; for to them that doubted 243Athe resurrection was shown by many proofs, by which we also are the more firmly established.

VERS. 12.-- To two of them. (BEDA.) This Luke has more openly (Luc. XXIV): Their eyes, etc. Afterwards they knew him in the breaking of bread. And as Luke says: Rising up the same hour they went back to Jerusalem, etc.

As they walked. (HIER.) Faith labours here, leading the active life, there the contemplative, etc., as far as forgetful of his own flesh he asks in this life what after it we hope for in the life to come.

VERS. 14.-- Last of all, etc. (BEDA.) On the fortieth day, when he was now about to depart from them into heaven, this to them especially, etc., as far as: Going into the whole world, preach ye the Gospel.

The hardness of heart, etc. (HIER.) That there may succeed a heart of flesh 243Bfull of charity: hence it is that the troops of the martyrs willingly seek the death of this world, because they know that for a temporal destruction they shall live for ever.

VERS. 15.-- To every creature. To every nation of the Gentiles. For before it had been said: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles (Matth. X): namely, that the preaching of the apostles, first driven back from Judea, might then be made a help to the Gentiles.

(HIER.) To the whole human race, which has something in common with every creature, with angels, with cattle, with trees, with stones, with fire and water, with hot and cold, with wet and dry, because man is called a lesser world.

VERS. 16.-- He that believeth, etc. Little ones believe through others, as from others they drew those things which are remitted to them in baptism, 243Ctheir sins.

VERS. 17.-- And signs, etc. Is it then that if we work not signs 244Awe do not believe? But these were necessary in the beginning, that faith might be nourished by miracles, but now that the faith of the Church is confirmed they are not necessary. They shall cast out demons, etc. This the Church does today spiritually, when through the grace of exorcism she lays her hand upon believers and drives out the malignant spirits. They shall speak with new tongues. This comes to pass while the faithful leave behind the worldly words of their former life, and sound forth holy mysteries, and extol the praises and the power of God as much as they are able.

VERS. 18.-- If they shall drink any deadly thing, etc. While they hear pestilent persuasions, and do not carry them through even to deeds, thereby it does them no hurt if they drink a deadly thing. They shall lay hands upon the sick, etc. While they strengthen their neighbours, confirmed in good work, by the example of good working, 244Bthey lay hands upon the sick, and they shall do well. These miracles are the greater by so much as they are spiritual; for by this souls are raised up, not bodies.

VERS. 19.-- Into heaven. (AUG.) Note that sometimes the heavens in the plural and sometimes heaven in the singular, etc., as far as where the belief of the faithful is instructed more by deeds than by words.

And he sitteth on the right hand. (BEDA.) And now judging all things, at the end of all he is to come as Judge. But Stephen saw him standing, because in the contest he had him as helper.

VERS. 20.-- But they, etc. (BEDA.) Note that by how much the later Mark began his Gospel, by so much, etc., as far as whereby the apostles sowed that same word of the Gospel 244Cthroughout the whole world.

The Latin is Migne's printing of Evangelium secundum Marcum (PL 114, coll. 179A–244C), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/114/179a resolves to the first.
